# M0 findings — first render of the five invoice fixtures (fulgur 0.40.0)

Date: 2026-10-15. Status: done (oracle in place); each finding below becomes its own plan/issue.

## Numbers

`python3 scripts/compare.py`, 96 dpi, pixel tolerance 32/255:

| Fixture | Pages ours/Chrome | Diff per page | Render time |
|---|---|---|---|
| 01-basic | 1/1 | 5.6 % | 13 ms |
| 02-long | **4/3** | 10.4 / 10.6 / 10.6 / 2.4 % | 55 ms |
| 03-logo-qr | 1/1 | 8.9 % | 185 ms (SVG) |
| 04-bilingual-vat | 1/1 | 10.8 % | 13 ms |
| 05-fancy | 1/1 | 24.3 % | 20 ms |

Layout itself (flex header, grid blocks, table columns, pagination (repeated `thead` only since F12), `@page`
margin boxes with `counter(pages)`) is close to Chrome. The diff is dominated by four systematic
defects, isolated with `out/scratch/probe.html` (12 one-line cases, see `scripts/` history):

## Findings

### F1 — `oklch()`, `lab()`, `color-mix()` components are emitted as raw RGB  (severity: high, size: small) — **done** (PR #8, `docs/plans/2026-10-15-f1-srgb-conversion.md` §7; the SVG presentation-attribute colours are F17)

`color: oklch(21% 0.034 264.665)` → content stream `0.2118 0.0353 1 rg` — i.e. L→R, C→G, H clamped→B.
`#111827` and `rgb(17 24 39)` are correct. Tailwind v4's whole palette is `oklch`, so every colour is wrong.
Where: fulgur's conversion from Stylo's `AbsoluteColor` — must convert to sRGB (`to_color_space`) before
taking components. Upstream PR candidate; carry a patch meanwhile. Acceptance: probe rows B/D/E/F equal row A/C
within 1/255; fixture 01 diff drops below 3 %.

### F2 — `@property`-registered initial values are not applied  (severity: high, size: small on our side) — **partly done** (PR #2, polyfill for bundled CSS, `docs/plans/2026-10-15-f2-property-defaults.md`; remaining: F2b, the Stylo `@property` root fix, parked)

Tailwind v4 declares `--tw-border-style: solid` etc. via `@property` and, for engines without it, repeats them in
`@layer properties { @supports (… -moz-orient:inline and not (color: rgb(from red r g b))) { … } }`.
Stylo is Firefox's engine: `-moz-orient` matches and relative colour syntax is supported, so the fallback block is
*skipped*, and Blitz does not implement `@property`, so the custom properties stay unset → `border-style: initial`
→ **no borders anywhere**, gradient stops unset → no gradients, `--tw-leading`, `--tw-font-weight` … likewise.
Plain `border-bottom: 2px solid` and `linear-gradient(...)` render fine, so this is purely the defaults mechanism.
Fix options: (a) our layer prepends a generated "Tailwind compat prelude" that sets the `@property` initial
values unconditionally (extract them from `tailwind.css` at build time); (b) `@property` support in Blitz/Stylo
integration (upstream, larger). Do (a) now, file (b).

### F3 — text with no matching font is silently dropped  (severity: critical — data loss, size: small) — **done** (PR #1, `docs/plans/2026-10-15-f3-font-fallback.md`; generic families in F3b, PR #4)

`font-family: monospace` with no monospace font bundled renders nothing; `pdftotext` confirms the text is absent.
In fixture 01 the bank account, IBAN and variable symbol disappear. Fix in our layer: always bundle a fallback
font and map the generic families (`monospace`, `sans-serif`, `serif`, `ui-monospace` …) to bundled faces;
error out loudly if a document uses a family we cannot resolve. Upstream: Parley/Blitz should fall back to any
available face rather than drop the run.

### F7 — the engine evaluates media queries as `screen`, not `print`  (severity: high for Tailwind, size: unknown — upstream) — **done** (PR #3, polyfill, `docs/plans/2026-10-15-f7-print-media.md`)

Found by `vrt-validator` on PR #1: rules inside `@media print` do not apply, rules inside `@media screen` do.
For invoices this is backwards: Tailwind's `print:` variants are ignored and screen-only rules leak into the PDF.
fulgur had a PR for `@media print` support in Blitz (#390), so the switch may be a one-liner on the fulgur side;
verify in `blitz_adapter.rs`, then fork + `[patch.crates-io]` per the standing decision. Acceptance: a probe with
`@media print { p { color: red } } @media screen { p { color: blue } }` renders red; fixture 05's `print:bg-slate-900`
applies.

### F8 — gradients with a colour-interpolation method are dropped  (severity: medium, size: unknown — upstream) — **done** (PR #5, polyfill, `docs/plans/2026-10-15-f8-gradient-interpolation.md`)

Found while doing F2. `linear-gradient(to right in oklab, red, blue)` paints nothing; the same gradient without
`in oklab` renders, also when built through `var()`. Tailwind v4 always emits `--tw-gradient-position: to … in oklab`,
so every `bg-gradient-*` is lost (05's header and totals box). Probe: `out/scratch/g.html` in the F2 PR description.
Spec: css-images-4 §3.1 `<color-interpolation-method>`. Where: Stylo parse (likely another static pref, check
`stylo_static_prefs`) or Blitz's gradient paint. Acceptance: probe rows 2/3/5 equal row 1; 05's header is a gradient.

### F4 — `text-transform` ignored  (severity: medium, size: medium) — **done** (PR #9, `docs/plans/2026-10-15-f4-text-transform.md`)

`uppercase` headings render as source text. Upstream (fulgur paragraph/Parley path). Workaround for templates:
write the text in capitals.

### F5 — table auto layout differs from Chrome  (severity: medium, size: large — upstream) — **done** (PR #10, `docs/plans/2026-10-15-f5-table-column-widths.md` §5; column algorithm gaps remain in F15)

Fixed `w-*` columns are honoured, but the remaining width is distributed differently, so the description column
is narrower, rows wrap, and 02-long paginates to 4 pages instead of 3. Known: Taffy has no table algorithm.
Mitigation for invoices: set explicit widths on all columns (`table-fixed` + `w-*`), which is also better practice
for print. Upstream issue to track.

### F6 (expected) — gradients via Tailwind, `shadow-*`, `ring-*`, `@page { margin: 0 }` full-bleed, `@page { size }`  (05-fancy; severity raised to **medium-high** by the `@page size` finding below) — **`@page` half done** (`2026-10-19-f6-page-size-margins.md` §7, ADR-0005); gradients, shadows and rings stay open

Gradients are F2. Shadows: fulgur has outer `box-shadow` (v0.4.5+) but 05 shows none — investigate after F2
(the `--tw-shadow` custom properties are also `@property`-registered, so likely the same root cause).

**Page margin from `@page` (found by `vrt-validator` on PR #28, measured, probably the cause of the "expected" F6 full-bleed gap):**
with `@page { size: A4; margin: 0 }` and no `--margin-mm`, content still starts at 15 mm (x = 57 px; with `--margin-mm 0` it starts at x = 1). The CLI
always passes `--margin-mm` (default 15) and `Page::margin_mm` wins over `@page` (the comment in `cell_vertical_align.rs` says so), so a document's own
`@page` margin is never honoured through the CLI or `RenderOptions::default()`. Probe `/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f9/p/ov_w793.html` (copy in `out/scratch/f66h/`).
Decide whether `RenderOptions` / the CLI should leave the margin unset so `@page` applies (the likely right answer: CSS Paged Media §4 says the page
context is the author's), and give 05 / full-bleed designs a way out.

**Effect on how fixtures and probes are authored (F30g validator, LOW 8):** the CLI ignores `@page` size and margin, so a fixture or a probe written with `@page{size:A4 landscape;margin:0}` renders as A4 portrait with 15 mm margins unless it is given `--landscape` / `--margin-mm 0` (the generated F30g sweep passes them per file name). Chrome references honour `@page`, so a pixel diff of such a fixture compares different pages; author fixtures with the CLI's defaults or pass the flags in `scripts/render-fixtures.sh`.

**`@page { size }` is ignored too** (found by `vrt-validator` on PR #29, measured again with the release CLI): `<style>@page{size:A5}</style>`
and `@page{size:A4 landscape;margin:0}` (`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f70/atk/edge1.html`) both produce a 595.276 x 841.89 pt A4 portrait page, like a document with
no `@page` at all. The page size comes from `RenderOptions::page` (A4 by default) and wins over the document, as the margin does. Invoice templates set
`@page { size: … }` as a matter of course (A4 landscape for wide tables, A5 for receipts), so this is the more serious half of the same decision: let
the document's `@page` size and margin apply unless the caller sets them explicitly (`Option` instead of a default), then give callers a way to force one.

### F9 — `<img>` without explicit `width`/`height` attributes is not rendered  (severity: high, size: unknown) — **done** (PR #17, `docs/plans/2026-10-16-f9-img-natural-size.md`)

Probe (`out/scratch` style, PNG registered via `--image logo.png=…`): `<img src="logo.png" width="120" height="36">`
renders; `<img src="logo.png" style="height:48px;width:auto">` and a bare `<img src="logo.png">` render nothing —
the image object is not even emitted. Intrinsic size / aspect ratio of the decoded image is not used for layout.
Hits fixture 03 (`h-12 w-auto` logo). Workaround for templates: always set `width` and `height` attributes.
Acceptance: all three probe variants show the image; 03 shows the logo at 48 px high, aspect kept.

### F10 — malformed CSS panics in fulgur's `::marker` rewrite  (severity: high — crash on user input, size: small — upstream) — **done** (PR #7)

Found by `vrt-validator` on PR #3: a `<style>` with an unterminated block that mentions `::marker`, e.g.
`<style>a::marker{color:red</style>`, panics in fulgur 0.40.0 `blitz_adapter.rs:3371`
(`rewrite_marker_content_url`, range end out of bounds). Independent of F7 (that text is not rewritten).
Fix upstream (bounds check); until then consider catching the panic at our API boundary (`catch_unwind`) and
returning an error. Acceptance: the repro returns `Err`, never panics.

### F11 — `none` colour components are mis-converted  (severity: low — Tailwind never emits it, size: small — fork) — **done** (PR #12, `docs/plans/2026-10-15-f11-none-colour-components.md`)

Found by `vrt-validator` on PR #8 (F1). `oklch(50% none 0)`, `oklab(50% 0 none)`, `lab(50% none 0)` and
`color(display-p3 none .5 .5)` paint black; `lch(50% 40 none)` / `oklch(70% 0.1 none)` lose their chroma (grey). They
should behave as if `none` were 0 (css-color-4 §4.4). Likely in Stylo's `to_color_space` on `none` (NaN?) components
before `into_srgb_legacy` clears the flags; fix in the fulgur fork (zero `none` components before converting).
Repro: `/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f1/p/none.py` in the PR #8 session.

### F12 — `<thead>` is not repeated on continuation pages  (severity: medium, size: unknown — fulgur pagination) — **partly done** (PR #13, `docs/plans/2026-10-16-f12-repeat-thead.md`; remaining: F12b, `<tfoot>` repetition)

Found in F5. Chrome repeats a table's header group on every page the table spans (CSS 2.1 §17.2, `table-header-group`: "print user agents may repeat header rows on each page"; Chrome,
Prince and WeasyPrint do). 02-long prints the column headings only on page 1, so
pages 2–3 have unlabelled columns and every row sits one header row higher than in Chrome. Acceptance: 02 pages 2–3
start with the header row; worst-page diff of 02 drops.

### F13 — table cells are top-aligned instead of `vertical-align: middle`  (severity: low, size: small — Blitz) — **done** (PR #15, `docs/plans/2026-10-16-f13-cell-vertical-align.md`)

Found in F5 (`out/compare/02-long-p2.png`). The HTML UA stylesheet gives `thead`, `tbody`, `tr` `vertical-align:
middle`, cells inherit it (HTML §15.3.8). In 02 the quantity and price cells of a row whose description wraps sit on
the first line; Chrome centres them. Acceptance: a two-line row in 02 has its numbers centred between the lines.

### F14 — `calc()` with a percentage as a table cell width panics  (severity: high — valid CSS refused, size: small — Blitz) — **done** (PR #11, `docs/plans/2026-10-15-f14-calc-cell-width.md`)

Found by `vrt-validator` on PR #10 (F5). A first-row cell with `width: calc(10% + 20px)` reaches `_ => unreachable!()`
in blitz-dom `layout/table.rs` (`collect_table_cells`); we catch it and return `Error::EnginePanic`. Expected: the
width is treated as `auto` (css-values-4 makes this a MUST for table cells; Chrome, Firefox and WebKit agree).
Repro: `<table><tr><td style="width:calc(10% + 20px)"></td><td></td></tr></table>`.

### F15 — table column algorithm gaps beyond F5  (severity: low for invoices, size: large — Blitz)

Found by `vrt-validator` on PR #10. Blitz derives one column per first-row cell and ignores: `colspan` in the first
row (column count wrong), `rowspan` when placing later cells, `min-width` on cells, `<col>`/`<colgroup>` widths,
percentages summing over 100 %, and min-content growth of a column whose explicit width is narrower than its
content (Chrome widens it; we overflow). Probes: `/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f5/` in the PR #10 session. Split into separate
findings when one hits a fixture.
More from `vrt-validator` on PR #11 (probes `/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f14/`), all pre-existing: `width: 0%` makes a 0 px
column (Chrome: padding + border); percentage columns summing below 100 % leave the table short; any `width` on
`<tr>`/`<tbody>` changes column widths (Chrome ignores it; css-values-4 also makes a mixed `calc()` row width
`auto`); cell `min-width`/`max-width` differ from Chrome; `table-layout: fixed` still takes widths from later rows;
`display: inline-table` renders nothing. (F30e: an `inline-table` with text or a `<p>` renders on a single page on main `d532069`.)
(The min-content growth of a column whose explicit `width` is narrower than its content is fixed since the F66 review, H1 (lengths)
and R1 (percentages of a definite table width, `w-1/6` included): the column is widened to the cells' min-content, in automatic
layout only. Spare-width distribution is not:
with every column explicit, a table with `width: 400px` stays as narrow as its columns, Chrome distributes the spare width,
`out/scratch/f66h/c5.html`.)
From the second `vrt-validator` run on PR #28, left open: a **`colspan` cell with a `width` and `nowrap`** (or an unbreakable word) still
spills over its neighbour, because only cells that span a single column are measured (`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f66/atk/a6.html`, copy in
`out/scratch/f66h/`); percentages that **sum past 100 %** with `nowrap` content give a wider table than Chrome (`out/scratch/f66/r1d`:
the third column starts at 537 px, Chrome 449) and a mixed length / percentage row is 5 px wide (`r1f`: 354, Chrome 349); and **R2**,
pre-existing, `table { width: 400px }` with cell `min-width` / `max-width` is not honoured (`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f66/atk/k3.html`, = `vrt-f5/p7`:
the table is about 245 px, the columns 144 / 44 / 54 px, Chrome 144 / 184 / 54 px).
(F59 fixed the column count when a `colspan` is wider than the table; **first-row `colspan` width division** — a spanning
first-row cell's `width` shared over its columns — stays here, with `<col>` widths and fixed layout ignoring content.)

From the `vrt-validator` run on PR #26 (F59): a spanning first row now lets later rows' widths apply, so F15's limits
show through the header idiom — a spanning cell's `width` goes entirely to its start column (`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f59/fz/z2_335.html`:
r0c1 at 413.8 pt, Chrome 168.5) and spare width goes only to auto tracks (`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f59/a/allw.html` table 2:
C0 211 pt, Chrome 342); a cell past the first row's column count does not contribute its percentage width
(`a/jag2.html` `t0`); with far more than 25 columns the per-column pitch is wider than Chrome's (≈ 14.4 vs 12.9 pt,
`a/k40x1.html`), so cells past the right page edge are not printed (before F59 they overlapped in the last column).

### F19 — `<tr>` / row-group backgrounds are not painted: 03's total is white on white  (severity: high — the amount due is invisible, size: small–medium — Blitz/fulgur) — **done** (PR #14, `docs/plans/2026-10-16-f19-row-backgrounds.md`)

Found by `pdf-reviewer` on PR #13. Fixture 03's `<tr class="bg-gray-900 text-white">` "Celkem k úhradě 48 400,00 Kč"
(in `<tfoot>`) prints white text on a white page: the text is in the PDF (`pdftotext` finds it) but the row's
background is never painted. Blitz flattens `thead`/`tbody`/`tfoot`/`tr` out of the table grid (zero-size boxes), so
fulgur has nothing to paint. CSS 2.1 §17.5.1 (table layers): row-group and row backgrounds paint under the cells of
that group/row. Acceptance: 03's total row is dark with white text, as in Chrome; a `<tr>` and a `<tbody>` with a
background paint behind their cells on every page they span.

### F20 — tagged PDF: cell and margin-box text is outside marked content  (severity: low until PDF/UA is on the roadmap, size: medium — fulgur)

Found by `pdf-reviewer` on PR #13 (pre-existing): `/TH` and `/TD` have empty `/K`, so cell text is neither tagged
nor an artifact; margin-box text ("Strana N / M") is outside marked content; `/Figure` has `/Alt` but empty `/K`.
Conflicts with PDF/UA-1 §7.1. A repeated `thead` could be typed `/Pagination` (ISO 32000-1 §14.8.2.2.2).

Extended after `pdf-reviewer` on PR #30 (F78; pre-existing): in tagged output table cell text is drawn outside marked content and each page is one `/Span` sequence.

Extended after `pdf-reviewer` on PR #25 (F32a; pre-existing, on main too): **every** `/Figure` has an empty `/K` (the
image is owned by the paragraph's `Span`, or by nothing); an `<img>` drawn as a block image and table body text are drawn
outside marked content, neither tagged nor `/Artifact` (PDF/UA-1 §7.1); pages 2 and later have no `/StructParents`
(§7.3). F42 (inline text before a block child) is the same family for another construct; fix them together: one pass
that makes every drawn thing either structure content or an artifact.

Extended after `pdf-reviewer` on PR #29 (F70; pre-existing in kind, now reachable because bare `<pre>` text is drawn): text of a `<pre>`, of a bare
`<div>` with direct text and of table cells is drawn outside marked content, neither tagged nor `/Artifact`.

### F21 — `border-spacing` has no layout effect  (severity: low — Tailwind tables use `border-collapse` or 0 spacing, size: small — Blitz)

Found while planning F19. Blitz 0.2.4 never maps `border-spacing` to the table grid's gaps (only `column-gap` /
`row-gap` reach Taffy), so cells always abut. CSS 2.1 §17.6.1. Acceptance: the ignored test
`row_backgrounds.rs::border_spacing_gap_is_not_painted` passes.

### F22 — row and row-group borders are not painted (`border-b` on `<tr>`, `divide-y` on `<tbody>`)  (severity: medium — 01, 02, 04, 05 row rules, size: large — Blitz #386 / fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-19-f22-row-borders.md` §7), `<col>` borders are F22c

Found while planning F19. Borders on `<tr>` and row groups only exist in the collapsing border model, which Blitz
does not implement (Blitz #386). Main remaining diff of 01, 02 and 04. Needs collapsed-border resolution (CSS 2.1
§17.6.2), not just painting.

### F23 — `background-image` raster tiles are 4/3 too large  (severity: medium, size: small — fulgur, px↔pt) — **done** (`2026-10-27-f180-svg-background-scale.md`)

Found by `vrt-validator` on PR #14 (pre-existing). A 40×40 px PNG without a pHYs chunk as `background-image` tiles
every 26.7 px instead of 20 px at the probe's 2× scale — the intrinsic size is read as pt where px was meant (or
the reverse). Rows inherit it since F19. Probe `out/scratch/v_img2.html` (PR #14 session).

### F24 — a `width: 100%` table overflows its container's padding  (severity: medium, size: unknown — Blitz/fulgur)

Found by `vrt-validator` on PR #14 (pre-existing). `body { padding: 20px }` + `<table class="w-full">`: the table
reaches 20 px past the right content edge (Chrome stops at it). Probe `out/scratch/v_w.html`.

### F25 — `background: url(data:image/svg+xml,…)` renders nothing  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Found by `vrt-validator` on PR #14 (pre-existing), on `<div>`, `<tr>`, `<tbody>` alike. Registered PNGs work.

### F28 — consecutive tables do not paginate: rows past the first page are silently dropped  (severity: high — data loss, size: unknown — fulgur pagination) — **done** (PR #16, `docs/plans/2026-10-16-f28-consecutive-tables.md`)

Found by `vrt-validator` on PR #15, confirmed with the pre-F13 binary: 60 one-row `<table>`s in a row render on **one**
page with 50 of 60 rows (Chrome: 2 pages, 60 rows); the same 60 rows as `<p>` paginate correctly (3 pages). With or
without a wrapping `<div>`. Invoices with an items table, a VAT summary table and a totals table that cross a page
boundary would lose content. Repro: 60 × `<table><tr><td>RadekNN</td><td>B</td></tr></table>`, count `RadekNN` in
`pdftotext`. Acceptance: every row present exactly once, page count matches Chrome.

### F29 — a first child's `margin-top` collapsing through `<body>` is not counted when paginating  (severity: medium — content drawn below the page, size: small–medium — fulgur) — **done** (PR #19, `docs/plans/2026-10-16-f29-body-offset.md`)

Found by `vrt-validator` on PR #16 (pre-existing). `<body style="margin:0">` + three `<p style="margin:500px 0 0">`: the
second `<p>` is drawn at y = 1033 px on page 1 (content box 1009 px), Chrome puts it on page 2. `body{padding-top:1px}`
fixes it. Realistic trigger: `h1.mt-8` as the first child with `body{margin:0}`. Probes `/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f28/`
(`ct_m_top_big.html`, `c_m_top_big.html`).

### F30 — content lost inside fragmented containers  (severity: high — data loss in edge layouts, size: large — fulgur) — **partly done** (PR #18 and the sub-cases F30b, F30d, F30e, F30f, F30g; remaining: F30h, rows of several items around a split paragraph)

Found by `vrt-validator` on PR #16 (pre-existing, same in the pre-F28 build): `columns:2` keeps 124 of 200 rows
(`cols2.html`); a `display:table` wrapper around tables drops everything (`wr_dtable.html`, 0 of 60); an inline-block
wrapper loses ≈20; a paragraph taller than a page inside a `div` / `li` / `td` loses its tail (at body level it splits
correctly); absolutely positioned elements inside fragmented containing blocks can vanish (`abs.html`). Split into
separate findings when one is picked up; the paragraph-in-div case is the most likely in invoices (long notes).

### F30h — rows of several items around a split paragraph  (severity: medium, size: medium — fulgur)

Left open by F30b (probes `out/scratch/f30b/p1.html`, `p2.html`, `p3.html`; Chrome PDFs beside them): in a flex or
grid row with more than one item, an item that is a split paragraph is handled like the one in a block, its siblings
are not.
- A stretched sibling keeps one fragment as tall as the unsplit row (a bordered card beside a tall `<p>`): its frame
  and background run to the page edge on page 1 and are missing on the continuation pages, Chrome continues them
  (P1). Tables have the same gap for sibling cells (F35).
- A later row whose first item is a short stretched leaf (a bordered label beside a tall `<p>`) moves to a fresh page
  although Chrome starts it in place (P2: Chrome 3 pages, ours 4; the lines are all printed).
- An item that is not first in its row and whose first two lines do not fit starts where the row starts and may
  overflow by up to one line (only the first item of a row moves the row, F28).
- A cell or item centred in a row taller than a page (`vertical-align: middle`, `align-items: center`) loses its text
  (F35): `<td>Item</td><td>{L}</td>` prints no `Item`; ignored test
  `a_short_cell_beside_a_split_cell_stays_on_the_first_page_when_centred`.
- The box of a split paragraph reserves its padding and border on every strip (`box-decoration-break: slice` is not
  implemented): `flex-col > .box` with direct text ends with 2 lines on an extra page where Chrome fits 50 per page.
- **Silent loss (no warning), pre-existing, same with a `div > p` item**: F30b covers rows with stretch / start
  alignment only. `align-items: center` with a tall `<p>` item shows 63 of 120 lines with page 1 empty
  (`center.html`); `align-items: flex-end` and `baseline` rows show 3 of 120 (`row_end.html`, `row_baseline.html`);
  `grid-row: span 2` shows 53 of 120 (`grid_rowspan.html`). A flex row container with padding or a border puts the
  short sibling after a tall `<p>` on page 2 while Chrome keeps it on page 1 (`pr_cont_pad.html`). Probes are in
  `/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f30b/p/`.
Anonymous flex items need nothing: Blitz makes a flex container with only text the inline root itself.

### F30e — a paragraph inside a `display: table` wrapper loses its lines  (severity: high — silent, size: medium — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-18-f30e-display-table-wrapper.md` §7)

Left open by F30 / F30b (`line_split_candidate` excludes non-cell children of a table box): `<div style="display:table">`
around `<p>` with 120 lines loses every line without a warning (`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f30b/p/dtable_p.html`). Plan: `2026-10-18-f30e-display-table-wrapper.md`.

**Result:** the finding's own repro (`display:table > <p>`) was already fixed by F50. What F30e fixed is what `split_barrier`'s `TablePart` arm still kept whole: text or `<span>`s directly in a `display: table` box or row, a `table-cell` box outside a table, and an `inline-table` alone on its line (also fixing its lost carrier line). Chrome's pages, ours: every line once, 3 pages.

**What F30f left for it** (F30f plan §3 G): F30f's `LineBarrier` / `split_barrier` names `TablePart` for a non-cell child of a table box, so F30e lifts one arm; the precise warning,
`alone_on_its_line`, the carrier slices and the render continuation are reusable as they are for an `inline-table`. Still needed:
- **Its own step 1.** The fork harness already splits `display:table > p` in its anonymous cell
  (`a_paragraph_that_is_a_non_cell_child_of_a_table_box_splits_in_its_anonymous_cell`, since F50), yet `dtable_p.html` printed nothing and gave no warning. So the loss may be in the table paint path
  (`draw_table_v2`, `draw_under_clip_table`), which F30f does not touch.
- Lifting `TablePart` for a table whose single anonymous cell (`blitz_adapter::anonymous_table_wrapper`) holds the paragraph.
- Allowing `inline-table` in `alone_on_its_line` (inside `table`, not `flow-root`).

### F30e-b — what F30e left: `display: table-caption` divs, pseudo-elements on a table box, orphan cells side by side, tagging  (severity: medium, size: medium — Blitz / fulgur)

Filed from the F30e step 1 probes (Chrome m141, A4, 15 mm, `/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f30e/p/`, 120 lines of 20 px):
- **A `display: table-caption` box that is not a `<caption>` in a `<table>` is not printed** (P5: `<div style="display:table"><div style="display:table-caption">Obchodní podmínky</div><p>L</p></div>`: Chrome prints the caption above 48 lines on page 1; ours prints the 120 lines and no caption). Blitz skips it (`release_skipped`), and `CaptionRestructurePass` handles only `<caption>` in `<table>`. Ignored test `a_table_caption_div_is_printed_above_the_table` in `table_wrapper_split.rs`.
- **`::before` / `::after` on a table box panics Blitz or is not painted** — **done in F163** (`2026-10-21-f163-table-pseudo-elements.md` §7). The first version of this bullet said "a real `<table>` is not affected (no pseudo box is built)". That was wrong: `<table></table>` with `table::before{content:"B"}` panics too (`layout/table.rs:1373`, index out of bounds: the pseudo box was a layout child of the table but not a grid item, so Taffy's loop over `child_count` indexed past the grid items), and with enough rows it does not panic but the text is silently not painted. Pseudo-elements of rows, row groups and `display: contents` wrappers in a table are a different cause: F163b.
- **Two orphan cells side by side**, one taller than a page (`<div><div style="display:table-cell;width:50%">L60</div><div style="display:table-cell">L5</div></div>`): the taller one splits and prints every line, but the short one goes to page 2 where Chrome keeps it beside the first (orphan rows and cells are laid out as Taffy flex and walked as block flow); F50b's anonymous table is the real fix, F30h-like.
- **Tagging:** text in an anonymous cell (direct text in a `display: table` box) has no structure element or MCID in a `--tagged` render (`walk_semantics` walks the DOM, and the anonymous block is not in it). Pre-existing on one page; more pages carry it now.
- `inline-table` that shares its line, `inline-flex`, `inline-grid`: F30f-b (unchanged; the warning now says "an inline-block or inline-table that shares its line").
- Not a finding: nested `<table>` in a `<td>` (60 rows) and `display:table` around a 60-row `<table>` print every row on both pages like Chrome, so "nested tables do not fragment" (upstream.md) did not reproduce at that size.

### F30c — body-level paragraphs: widows, orphans and a heading before a long paragraph  (severity: medium, size: medium — fulgur)

Follow-up named in the F30 plan (§6), filled in from the F78 reviews (`vrt-validator`, `pdf-reviewer` on PR #30; all pre-existing, same on main):
- **A one-line widow stays on the previous page and the last fragment overflows by one line** (+5.8 pt below the content box, about 2 % of paragraph
  lengths); at `--margin-mm 0` that line is off the page and lost (`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f78/m0/n3385.html`, `n3858.html`). Body-level `fragment_inline_root`
  skips a break that would leave fewer than `WIDOWS_MIN` lines and accumulates into the current fragment instead of relaxing only when no break fits.
  The same loss for an absolutely positioned paragraph (F30g validator, LOW 4, `/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f30g-val/repro/margin0_line_lost.html`, `--margin-mm 0`): the line that straddles the page bottom is lost; in-flow text has it too, so this is this bullet, not F30g.
- The same widow handling lets a paragraph that exceeds the page by less than a line print its last line 4-12 px into the bottom margin where Chrome moves two lines to the next page
  (F83 `vrt-validator` MEDIUM-2: `/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f83/att/cases1/sz_20px_51_after.html`, +8 pt; identical in the `auto` twin).
- `orphans` / `widows` in CSS are not read (initial value 2 only). Seen again in F183 plan §7.6 (still true on 2026-10-28, `ORPHANS_MIN` / `WIDOWS_MIN` constants only): `td { orphans: 4; widows: 4 }` in a collapsed table, a cell `img60` + 150 words started at filler 996..1032 px: Chrome and main move the cell whole (0 | 150 words), ours split it 48 | 102 / 32 | 118 (`u_col1_o4_*`), 47 | 103 / 31 | 119 (`u_col2_o4_*`), 20 of 6209 sweep cases, no content lost. Main met Chrome's answer by accident (a cell of a bordered table was never "first" for the line splitter, F183 §7.5), so this is the one family F183 leaves further from Chrome than main.
- An `h1` followed by a 420-word `<p>` leaves page 1 holding only the heading: the paragraph moves whole (the pre-advance of F30 §3 / Phase 2.2
  pushes a paragraph that does not fit the strip), where Chrome starts it below the heading and splits it.
  The same pre-advance moves a 600-word `<p>` placed after a 650 px spacer whole to page 2, where Chrome starts it on page 1 and splits it 245 / 355 words (F30d review
  L3, not multicol; covered by this finding, no separate one). Fix: split a paragraph that does not fit the strip when its first `ORPHANS` lines do, as the nested path does (`nested_line_split`).
- Consequence seen in F87: a body-level `<p>` with `break-inside: avoid` is placed exactly like an `auto` one (both move whole to the next page when they do not fit, and an `avoid` one taller than a page is relaxed since F83),
  so the property is invisible on a body-level paragraph; Chrome starts the `auto` one on page 1 (14 of 100 words in `/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f87/cls/chrome/c1n.html`) and moves only the `avoid` one.

### F30d — `column-count` / `columns` print no text at all for a long paragraph  (severity: HIGH — silent loss of everything, size: large — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-17-f30d-multicol.md` §7)

Named in the F30 plan (§6) and first seen in F30 (`columns:2` kept 124 of 200 rows); now measured for text: `column-count:2` around 4000 words (bare text or a
`<p>`) gives 15 to 22 pages with **0 words** (`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f78/ab/mc_normal.html`, `mc_1.3.html`, `/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f78/s/colmulti.html`; 15 pages, 0 of 4000 for
`mc_normal`), no warning. Identical on main and unchanged by F78 (from reading, not traced: multicol paints through
`paint_multicol_paragraph_slices`, not through the F78 slices). Upgraded from the planned low-priority follow-up to HIGH; needs its own plan (multicol balancing over pages). Plan: `2026-10-17-f30d-multicol.md`.

### F30d-b — what the F30d multicol fragmenter does not do yet  (severity: medium, size: large — fulgur)

Filed with F30d (plan `2026-10-17-f30d-multicol.md` §3). A multi-column container taller than the room under it is now cut over the pages (flow of
`<p>` lines and atomic boxes, `column-count` / `column-width` / `columns` / `column-gap`, body level and block-nested). Left out:

- `column-span: all` across pages (the container keeps its old path: the span subtree splits, the column groups do not).
- `column-rule` per page: the rule geometry still comes from the one-tall layout, so the rules on a cut container are only approximately right.
- Child margins and padding inside columns are ignored by `layout_column_group` (Tailwind `space-y-*`, `mb-*`, `p-*` on a clause vanish): the flow carries line advances and box heights only, not the space between them.
- Tables and other boxes taller than a column (placed whole, they overflow; the fork logs a warning naming the element); nested multicol; multicol in flex / grid / table cells (the old paths).
- `orphans` / `widows` / `break-inside: avoid` / `break-after: avoid` inside columns (Chrome keeps clause 21 of fixture 12 together, we cut it after a line: a one-line difference at a column end), `box-decoration-break` (the container's border and background are drawn again at the top of every page: the top border shows on continuation pages).
- Non-`p` inline roots (`<h3>` is a box and is placed whole, fine; a `<div>` of text is too) and paragraphs with inline boxes (images, inline-blocks, in Case A they keep the old degenerate single-column layout).
- An explicit `height` on a multicol container is ignored when the content is taller (the container is as tall as its columns; Chrome clips or overflows, F30d review L1).
- Right-to-left paragraphs over 64 KiB in columns: `shape_paragraph_glyph_runs` un-wraps Parley's u16 cluster offsets for left-to-right runs only (F30d review), so a
  longer RTL paragraph keeps the wrapped text ranges (wrong extracted text, pages look right).
- Readers of the container's `final_layout` keep Taffy's one-tall height (abspos inside a multicol, link and bookmark rects of sliced paragraphs): `pdf-reviewer` to check link annotations.

### F30d-c — a multi-column container that fits its page is balanced against the one-column height  (severity: medium, size: small — fulgur)

Found by the F30d guard test (`crates/htmltopdf/tests/multicol.rs`, `short_two_column_text_stays_where_it_was`): `layout_multicol_subtrees` hands the multicol pass the
height Blitz measured for the container laid out in one column as `avail_h`, and `layout_column_group` / `layout_self_inline_root_container` use it as the budget
whenever the content is at least `n` times that tall. 150 words in `column-count:2` (`<p>`) give 66 and 84 words in the columns (container 28 lines tall) where
Chrome balances 75 and 75 (25 lines): the container is 3 lines too tall and its columns end 6 lines apart. A container that does not fit its page is not affected (the
fragmenter balances the last page itself). Fix: `avail_h` is the author's definite height or infinity, not the one-column height; it moves every multicol golden that has more than one column of text,
so it needs its own change and its own golden re-bless.

### F30d-d — a single very long paragraph in multicol is superlinear  (severity: low, size: medium — fulgur)

Found by `vrt-validator` on the F30d branch (M2): one `<p>` of 120 000 words in `column-count:2` takes about 118 s, the same on main and on the branch (so not new), where
the same text split into 2000 paragraphs takes about 1 s for 249 pages. Same class as F82 (a single 50 000-word paragraph takes 16 s without columns); the cost is in the
one Parley layout / `parley_line_heights` / `shape_paragraph_glyph_runs` of a paragraph that long, not in the fragmenter (`plan_multicol_pages` is linear in lines). Not traced further.

**Bench:** `bench/corpus/slow-multicol.html` (B1; report only): 20 000 words in `columns:2`, 21 pages, 2.5 s on the dev VM (`docs/benchmarks.md`). A fix shows up as a drop of that row; `python3 scripts/bench.py slow-multicol`.

### F83 — a `break-inside: avoid` paragraph taller than a page loses words  (severity: HIGH — silent, size: medium — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-17-f83-break-inside-avoid-paragraph.md` §7)

`<p style="break-inside:avoid">` of 2500 words printed 2478 on 7 pages with one "recorded no matching line ranges" warning per page. Cause: the body walk gave an avoid
inline root no line metrics whatever its height, so one taller than a page fell to the fulgur-sbw2 strip slicer (fixed pixel heights, no line ranges, 7 fragments, 0 slices),
and the renderer's measuring fallback printed none of the line next to each cut (3 of 6 cuts lost a line of 7 words, plus the last word: 22). Fixed in the fork (`ebd78f5`,
`avoid_inside_holds`): `avoid` holds while the box fits a page and is relaxed past that (css-break-3 §4.4), so the paragraph takes the `auto` line path with tiling slices.

### F83a — `page-break-inside` is not aliased to `break-inside`  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur) — **done** (fulgur `9e14cba2`, F183 plan §7.6)

Filed with F83. The fork's CSS reader (`column_css.rs`, around `:606-628`) maps `page-break-before` and `page-break-after` only, so `page-break-inside: avoid` is ignored: no
text is lost (`crates/htmltopdf/tests/break_inside_avoid.rs`, `page_break_inside_avoid_loses_no_word`), but nothing is kept together. css-break-3 §3.4 makes it a legacy alias
(`avoid` -> `avoid`). It is safe to add now that F83 is done.

### F83b — the recursion gate and `nested_line_split` ignore `break-inside: avoid`  (severity: medium, size: medium — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-18-f83b-avoid-on-wrappers.md` §7)

Filed with F83, confirmed by the F83 step 1 probes and again in F83b step 1 (A4, 15 mm, Inter, headless Chromium (Skia/PDF m141) via Playwright, same on `line-height: normal` and `20px`):
a `break-inside: avoid` wrapper that does not fit the rest of the page but fits a page moves whole to the next page in Chrome; the gate (`would_split_block_subtree`) entered it and split it between its
children, and a too-tall avoid box that something preceded started in place. Fixed in the fork (`906d05f`): an avoid box that holds drops the gate's `would_split` term (body level and nested, block flow only),
a forced break inside it still wins, and an avoid box taller than a page starts the next page when content precedes it. New fixture 14. `vrt-validator` found 3 high and 1 medium on the first version (blank page with a padded parent, a forced break at the start of the box, a relaxed padded box, a forced break in a box taller than a page only through it); all fixed
in the same change with tests, plus the shapes its harness and a wider fuzz run showed (plan §7.1; fork `9e92b24`). Recorded: F83f, F83g, F83h.

### F83d — `break-inside: avoid` on flex / grid items, table cells and rows whose cells are entered is not honoured  (severity: low, size: medium — fulgur) — **table cell / row part done** (fulgur `9e14cba2`, folded into F183's PR, plan §7.6); flex / grid part open

Filed with F83b (its non-goal; probes in the F83b plan §7, Chromium m141, A4, 15 mm, Inter, 20 px lines). Chrome moves the box whole to the next page, we split it:
- a flex row with an avoid item (`<div style="display:flex">` of an avoid div with three `<p>` and a short sibling, after a 930 px spacer): Chrome moves the whole row to page 2; ours splits the item (`aaa`, `bbb` on page 1, `ccc` on page 2);
- `<tr style="break-inside:avoid">` or `<td style="break-inside:avoid">` whose cell holds six three-line `<p>` after a 700 px spacer: Chrome moves the row to page 2, ours enters the cell and splits it
  between the paragraphs (four on page 1, two on page 2). `tr` has no box of its own in the fragmenter (Blitz gives `tbody` / `tr` zero size), so the rule is not even seen; a row whose cells hold
  one paragraph already moves whole through F28 (fixture 02).
Fix idea: treat the row (the cells level with each other) as the avoid unit in `fragment_block_subtree`'s table and flex paths, keeping `RowState` / `SplitRow` in step.

**Table cell / row part done (2026-10-28), in F183's PR.** F183's fix for a multi-line collapsed cell (plan §7.5) sends the cells of every bordered table down the line-split path that ignored `avoid`, which made 45 sweep cases worse than main, so the fix went into that PR instead of waiting: `avoid_rows` reads `break-inside: avoid` (and the `page-break-inside` alias, F83a) from the `td` and from its `tr`, `leading_block_size` counts such a row that fits a page whole, and `fragment_block_subtree` does not enter its cell. A row taller than a page or first on a strip that does not hold it is split as before. 74 sweep cases changed (collapsed and separated), all now equal Chrome's words and images per page; fixtures 01-27 byte-identical to main. Details and numbers: F183 plan §7.6. **Still open here:** the flex row with an avoid item (first bullet above) and a grid item; `rowspan` rows were not probed.

### F83f — deep avoid chains are slower than on main and still superlinear  (severity: low, size: medium — fulgur)

Found by `vrt-validator` on the F83b branch (M2): 400 nested levels of 100 children each take 6.6 s, main 3.9 s (1.5-1.7x, still superlinear in the depth). Every level walks its subtree again for each avoid rule (`avoid_measure` /
`avoid_piece_size`, `starts_with_relaxed_avoid`, `leading_block_size(above)`, `frame_is_content_sized`, plus the gate's own `has_forced_break_below`). Fix idea: compute the forced-break offsets, the avoid flags and the content-sized
flag of a subtree once per fragmentation pass (a side table keyed by node), or memoise them per node. Not an invoice shape; attach to F82 if it is ever picked up with it.

**Bench:** `bench/corpus/slow-avoid.html` (B1; report only): 400 nested `break-inside-avoid` levels of 20 children, 399 pages, 1.2 s on the dev VM (`docs/benchmarks.md`); `python3 scripts/bench.py slow-avoid`.

### F83g — two small differences from Chrome around `avoid` boxes  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur / Blitz)

Found by `vrt-validator` on the F83b branch, recorded only:
- A box 0.7 px taller than the room moves to the next page where Chrome fits it (line metrics, F81; harness gen4 `twn_n5_*_mt-6_pad`: section 5 of 7 moves to page 2). The fit test has 1 px of slack (`avoid_keeps_whole`); Blitz's
  `line-height: normal` is 1.2 em, Chrome's is the font's own metric.
- An avoid wrapper that holds an image taller than a page adds a page (gen2 `F_img_s0_h1100`: 4 pages, main 3, Chrome 1). The image is an atomic box; also bad on main (`bad-also-on-main`).

### F83h — an atomic leaf first in an avoid box that does not fit below the box's inset overflows  (severity: low, size: medium — fulgur)

Found by the F83b review (`r2`, harness fuzz): `<div avoid padding:8px><div style="height:1005px">`, a `height` spacer (or any atomic block) that is first in an avoid box, with only the box's padding / border / margin above it, and that does not
fit the strip below them. Main cuts the leaf to the next page and leaves a page of frames (blank-looking); the branch leaves it where it is (no blank page, the page counts of Chrome) and it overflows the content bottom by the inset
(4 px for `r2`, 24.5 pt for fuzz `Z1_475` with a 40 px margin). Chrome slices the leaf across the pages. Fix idea: slice an atomic leaf in `fragment_block_subtree` the way the body-level strip slicer does (fulgur-sbw2). Text is never an atomic
leaf (a line box fits), so this is a `height` spacer / image shape.

Same family (validator re-validation, fuzz `S5_1390` and `S5_1594`, not reduced here): a blank page after a spill that holds only padding (a parent's bottom padding or frame that did not fit and moved to the next page) followed by a tall
avoid box, which then starts the page after it. Low severity (a page of frames; no text lost); the fix for the atomic leaf (slicing, or closing the spilled frame with its last child) should be checked against these two.

### F83e — a margin gap between an outer box's border and its first avoid child keeps the border on the old page  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Found in F83b step 6 (Chromium m141, A4, 15 mm, Inter, 20 px lines, `p{margin:0}`): `<p>before</p><div style="border-top:2px solid red"><div style="margin-top:20px;break-inside:avoid">` four long paragraphs: Chrome starts the
outer box on page 2 (no rule on page 1); ours treats the 20 px between the border and the avoid box as a class C break point (css-break-3 §4.1) and enters the outer box on page 1, so its top border is drawn
under `before` and the avoid box starts page 2 (the words per page then differ from Chrome's by the 20 px of gap: page 2 holds 351 words, Chrome's 344). With padding instead of the margin, or with no gap, ours matches Chrome
(`g_b`, `g_c`; fixture-style Tailwind documents have no such gap because the preflight zeroes `p` margins). The UA `p` margins of a document without Tailwind make the same gap (`<p>` first child of the avoid
box: its 16 px margin collapses through the avoid box but not through the bordered outer box). Fix idea: do not stop `starts_with_relaxed_avoid` at a gap that is only a collapsed margin of the first
descendant. Also pre-existing and not caused by F83b: a bordered box split over pages draws its top border again on every page (`box-decoration-break` is not implemented, as in fixtures 06 and 08).

### F83c — an `avoid` box taller than a page only through `height` must not be relaxed to `auto`  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur) — **done in F83**

Found by the F83 step 1 probe (`<p>before</p><div style="height:1500px;background:#eee;break-inside:avoid">a<br>b<br>c</div>`: 3 pages like Chrome on main; the first F83 version cut it at the bottom of
page 1). Resolved before landing (fork `752df13`): `avoid` is relaxed only when the box's lines do not fit a page (line extent, not box height), so height / padding / `min-height` boxes keep the path
they had (byte-identical to main). Plan §7.1. Still open and not caused by F83: a text-only `auto` box taller than a page through `height` is cut at the bottom of the page its text ends on (`h1500_auto`: ours 1 page, Chrome 2).

F83 `vrt-validator` leftovers (probes under `/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f83/att/`; all identical on main and in the `auto` twin of the paragraph, so none is caused by F83):
- A relaxed `avoid` paragraph (and the identical `auto` one) whose lines exceed the page by less than about one line prints its last line into the bottom margin
  (`cases1/sz_20px_51_after.html`: page 2 ends at 807.4 pt, content bottom 799.4 pt, +8 pt, the `_auto` twin the same; `cases4/sw_25.27_after.html` did not reproduce here, page 2
  ends at 798.4 pt); Chrome moves two lines to the next page. This is the first bullet of **F30c** (a break that would leave fewer than `WIDOWS_MIN` lines is skipped and the last fragment
  overflows), no new finding.
- An absolutely positioned `<p>` prints 0 of its 200 words (`cases3/abspos.html`; Chrome 200): **F30g** (the absolute pass).
- A two-cell row whose cell is taller than a page loses the short sibling cell's text (`cases3/td.html`, `tr_avoid.html`: `word99991` missing, Chrome prints it): **F35** (`vertical-align: middle` is the table default).
- `transform: rotate(1deg)` on a tall `<p>`: main printed 381 of 700 words on one page, the branch prints all 700 on 2 pages like Chrome. `pdftotext` reports 55 duplicated tokens (Chrome's own PDF: 48),
  so that is the extractor splitting rotated words, not loss; not confirmed visually.
- `padding:600px` on a 700-word paragraph prints 329 of 700 words (main 336), Chrome 700 on 4 pages: filed as **F88**.
- `@page { margin: 0 }` with 900 words: ours 3 pages with every word, Chrome 2 pages (and 884 words in its text layer): filed as **F89**.

### F87 — Tailwind utilities never reach fulgur's break / column reader: `break-inside-avoid` does nothing  (severity: HIGH — the invoice idiom of F83 is not covered by F83, size: small — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-17-f87-tailwind-layer-breaks.md` §7)

Found while making fixture 13 show the F83 change. Tailwind v4 emits every utility inside `@layer utilities{...}` (and `@layer properties{@supports ...}` before it), and the fork's CSS reader
(`column_css.rs` `parse_stylesheet`, the sniffer that fills the `ColumnStyleTable` with `break-*`, `column-fill`, `column-rule-*` and `page`) skipped at-rule blocks whole (`@media`, `@layer`, `@supports`; a test in
`column_css.rs` pinned it for `@media`). So `<p class="break-inside-avoid">` with the compiled `fixtures/tailwind/tailwind.css` was an ordinary paragraph, whereas `style="break-inside:avoid"` or a plain `<style>` rule
worked. Measured with `htmltopdf render --css`: `.break-inside-avoid{break-inside:avoid}` alone, or after the layers: effective; inside `@layer u{...}`, `@media screen{...}` or `@supports (display:grid){...}`: ignored
(`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f83/cls/`). Of the utilities the reader parses, the compiled sheet holds exactly one, `.break-inside-avoid`; `break-before-page`, `break-after-page` and `print:break-*` are not compiled in (the
F87 tests add them inline in Tailwind's shape). **Not affected, corrected from the first version of this entry:** `columns-*`. `column-count` and `column-width` are computed by Stylo, which handles layers
(`@layer utilities{.columns-2{columns:2}}` gives two columns, F87 plan §7 step 1); `columns-*` is not compiled either. Fixed in the fork (`645c15e`, then `3b158b6` after `vrt-validator`): the reader descends into `@layer` and matching `@media`
blocks and folds the rules in cascade order: importance, layer (reversed for `!important`), specificity, source order, with the `style` attribute between the normal and the important tiers. Plan: `2026-10-17-f87-tailwind-layer-breaks.md`.

### F87a — `@media` with features does not reach the break reader: Tailwind responsive variants (`md:break-before-page`)  (severity: low, size: medium — fulgur)

Filed with F87, non-goal there. The reader matches `@media` queries of the form `[only|not]? all|screen` only; a query with a feature (`(width >= 48rem)` for `md:`, `(hover: hover)`, `(min-width: ...)`) never matches, so
`class="md:break-before-page"` stays ignored, where Chrome evaluates the feature against the print page width. The right fix is to evaluate the query with Stylo's media evaluation against the same device as the rest of
the stylesheet (width, resolution); it needs the sheet to be given to Stylo or a device handle, which the reader does not have. The skip leaves today's value, so nothing regresses.

### F87c — `break-before` / `break-after` values `avoid`, `avoid-page`, `avoid-column`, `column` are not parsed  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-17-f87-tailwind-layer-breaks.md` §7.2)

Filed from the F87 `vrt-validator` (`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f87/c4.py` `bf_page_then_always`): `.a{break-before:page}` followed by `.b{break-before:avoid-page}` on one element: Chrome gives the later `avoid-page`
(no forced break, page 1), we ignore the unparsed value and keep `page` (page 2). Only `auto`, `page`, `always`, `left`, `right` are read (`parse_break_before_value`, `parse_break_after_value`). The fix needs an `Avoid`
variant that the fragmenter honours (css-break-3 §3.1) or at least one that cancels a forced break. Newly exposed by F87 (the layered utility used to be dropped), same value is unparsed on main.

Done in the F87 PR (fork `69bfd0e`): `avoid`, `avoid-page`, `avoid-column` and `column` parse as "no forced page break" (the table has no avoid or column breaks), so a later one cancels an earlier `page`, before and after; Chrome probes agree. While probing, `break-before: always` (not a value of `break-*`, only of the legacy `page-break-*`) was found to be forced by the reader where Chrome ignores it, and `page-break-before: page|recto|verso` the same: both are now invalid, as in Chrome.

### F87d — `<style media="not print">` counts as print  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur) — **done** (§7.2)

Filed from the F87 `vrt-validator` (`c2.py` `hattr_not_print`): the harvester's `<style media>` filter (`walk_for_column_styles`) accepts a sheet when any comma-separated token is empty, `all`, or *contains* `print`, so `not print`
and `only print and (min-width: 1px)` apply while Chrome (print media) skips the first. It also uses print semantics where the at-rule reader uses the screen device (`READER_MEDIA_TYPE`), because `htmltopdf` swaps
`print` and `screen` inside CSS text but not in attributes. Fix: evaluate the attribute with `media_query_list_matches` and the same swap, or fix it in `htmltopdf`.

Done in the F87 PR (fork `69bfd0e`): the attribute is a media query list evaluated for a print device (`media_query_list_matches_on`); `<type> and <features>` keeps the sheet when the type matches, as the substring test did, and never under `not`.

### F87e — `<style>` inside `<template>` is applied by Blitz (and so read by the break reader)  (severity: low, size: small — Blitz) — **resolved in the reader** (`2026-10-17-f87-tailwind-layer-breaks.md` §7.2)

Filed from the F87 `vrt-validator` (`c7.py`, `c8.py` `template_unl`): `walk_for_column_styles` harvests the `<style>` of `<template>` and `<noscript>`. A browser never applies the first (inert); Chrome with scripting on
also ignores the second (Chrome: page 1 for `break-before:page`, we: page 2). The same bug exists on main for unlayered rules. First fix: skip both subtrees in the harvester. Coordinator's correction: there is no
scripting here, so HTML applies `<noscript>` content, and **Stylo applies a `<style>` inside `<noscript>` and inside `<template>`** (the fork's agreement test shows both colour `#a`); the break reader must agree with our own
engine's cascade or one stylesheet would apply half its declarations. So the harvester skips neither (fork `a5977c9`), and `column_reader_agrees_with_stylo_on_template_and_noscript_styles` compares Stylo's colour with the
side table for both. What stays: **Blitz applies a `<style>` in an inert `<template>`**, a divergence from browsers for every property (colours, sizes), not only breaks; the harness cases `template`, `template_unl`
and `noscript` differ from Chrome-with-JS by design. Fix, when wanted: make `<template>` contents inert in the Blitz fork (the reader test then fails and tells to skip `template` in the harvester).

### F87f — the break reader costs rules x elements  (severity: low, size: medium — fulgur)

Filed from the F87 `vrt-validator` (`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f87/perf.py`): `walk` tests every rule against every element. A 1.1 MB stylesheet of 24 000 break rules over 2000 elements takes 1.7 s on the branch against 0.23 s on main
(main drops every rule inside a layer, so the branch matches many more); realistic loads (Tailwind: one break utility) are flat (F87 plan §7: parse 0.1 ms). Fix: index the rules by class, id and type, and test only
the rules that can match; the cascade order is already carried by `rank`, specificity and index.

**Bench:** `bench/corpus/slow-breaks.html` (B1; report only): 24 000 layered break rules over 1000 elements, 2.8 s on the dev VM (`docs/benchmarks.md`); `python3 scripts/bench.py slow-breaks`.

After the Stylo matcher (F87 review H1, fork `659affe`) the stress cases of `perf3.py` are unchanged (same-layer 1.65 s against 1.72 s before, anonymous layers 1.70 / 1.80 s, all-matching important 1.47 / 1.43 s; main 0.22 / 0.28 / 1.10 s); fixtures 01 and 02 are within noise of main. The index is the way to bring them down.

### F87g — deep at-rule nesting overflows the stack in Blitz / Stylo, and `.a{` x 10 000 hangs on main  (severity: low, size: not ours — Blitz / Stylo)

Filed from the F87 `vrt-validator`, both the same on main, neither in our reader (which caps at 32 levels, `MAX_AT_RULE_DEPTH`): a stylesheet nested about 3586 levels deep (`@layer{` repeated) overflows the stack of Blitz /
Stylo's parser (the process aborts, `catch_unwind` cannot catch it), and `.a{` repeated 10 000 times hangs on main. Not reproduced by the F87 agent; take the repros from the validator report. The guard is `htmltopdf`'s
`close_open_blocks` and a size / depth check before the CSS reaches Blitz, if invoices ever take untrusted CSS.

### F87h — the `<style media>` attribute: the reader ignores media features, Blitz ignores the attribute  (severity: low, size: medium — Blitz, one finding)

Filed from the F87 re-validation. Two gaps around one attribute, the Blitz fix being the right place for both:
- **L1, the reader:** the harvester evaluates a `<style media>` attribute as a media query list on a print device, and a query with features keeps the sheet when its type matches (`media_query_list_matches_on(..., features_match)`).
  So `print and (min-width:99999px)` and `print and (orientation:landscape)` count as matching where Chrome (portrait A4) skips them (`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f87/c13.py` `media_attr_feature_false`, `media_attr_orient`; a layered rule in such a sheet newly differs from
  main, which dropped layers). The optimism keeps a `print and (min-width: 1px)` sheet on, as the old substring test did. Evaluating features needs the page width and orientation of the device.
- **L2, the engine:** Blitz / Stylo ignores the `media` attribute entirely: a `media="screen"` sheet still colours elements (documented in `docs/upstream.md`: "`<style media>` attribute ignored"). The break reader filters by the attribute, so for a
  `media="screen"` sheet the reader skips rules that the engine applies for the other properties, the half-applied sheet that F87e avoided. Fix in the Blitz fork: evaluate the attribute with Stylo's media evaluation on the print device; then the reader
  should take its answer from the engine instead of its own filter.

### F88 — a paragraph with large vertical padding loses most of its lines  (severity: medium — silent, size: small to medium — fulgur)

`<p style="padding:600px 10px;background:#eee">` with 700 words prints 329 of 700 words (main 336), Chrome prints 700 on 4 pages; with or without `break-inside: avoid`
(`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f83/att/cases3/f83c_pad600_700w_avoid.html`, `f83c_pad600_700w_after_avoid.html`). Found by `vrt-validator` on the F83 branch, pre-existing. Not traced; the padding is in the box
height, so the strip / line planners disagree about where the text starts (the F80 family, a bordered direct-text box overflowing by 6 pt).

### F89 — `@page { margin: 0 }`: 3 pages where Chrome gives 2  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

`@page{size:A4;margin:0}` with a 900-word `<p>`: ours 3 pages (every word printed), Chrome 2 pages (884 words in its text layer, the first 8 not on a page of it)
(`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f83/att/cases3/m0.html`). Found by `vrt-validator` on the F83 branch, pre-existing, same in the `auto` twin. Not analysed; the first bullet of F30c (a line off the page at margin 0) is a neighbour.

### F90 — selectors Stylo does not parse here drop their break rules  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur / Stylo)

Found in the F87 final validation. `:has()` is gated off in this Stylo build, and `:defined`, `:dir()`, `:-webkit-any` and `[attr=v i]` are not parsed either, so a break rule written with one of them is dropped by the reader. Chrome reads them and applies the break. Low: invoice templates do not use these selectors with `break-*`. Size small if the reader can skip unparsed selectors without the whole rule.

### F91 — the break reader hard-codes `NoQuirks`, so class and id matching is case-sensitive in a doctype-less document  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Found in the F87 final validation; the same on main. The reader's selector matching uses `NoQuirks`, so in a document without a doctype Chrome matches classes and ids case-insensitively (quirks mode) and we do not. Repro: `/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f87/q/` (`noquirks_none_cls_case.html`, `noquirks_none_id_case.html`, `noquirks_none_attr_case.html`, with the Chrome and main PDFs beside them). Low: documents without a doctype are rare in the invoice shape.

### F92 — the body-direct abspos window path shifts boxes by the body's offset  (severity: medium, size: small — fulgur) — **done** (F187: `2026-10-27-f187-abspos-body-margin.md`)

Confirmed in the F30g step 1 (`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f30g/p/ofs*.html`, not fixed there, the pass is untouched): `append_position_absolute_body_direct_fragments` stores `x = … - body_offset.0` and `y = … - k * page_stride - body_offset.1`, but the renderer adds the body's x offset to nothing and its y offset on page 0 only. With `body{margin:8px}` an abspos box that stays on the window model is painted 8 px too far left on every page and 8 px too high on every page after the first (`ofs8`: 36.5 pt against Chrome's 42.7 pt and 97.5 pt against 99.8 pt on page 2; `ofs40`: 12.5 pt and 73.5 pt). Boxes F30g fragments from their start (text crossing a page) do not have it; a box that fits one page and sits under a body margin still does. Fix: store the ICB-relative x and subtract the offset on page 0 only (the F29 rule); VRT `fixedpos-*` expectations have margin 0, so they should not move.

### F93 — a static-position abspos box follows a sibling that ours moves whole where Chrome splits it  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

F30g A9b: 900 px, a 300 px empty block, then `<div style="position:absolute"><p>` 1000 words. Chrome splits the empty block across the break and puts the box 191 px down page 2 (`word00000` at 182 pt); ours moves the block whole (F83h, atomic leaf) and the box follows it, 300 px down page 2 (the plan expected 300 px, the Chrome probe says 191). The page count is the same (5). A box whose text fits its window keeps the window's Taffy position (191 px), so the two static shapes disagree by the strut. Fix with F83h (slice the leaf).

### F94 — an abspos box is laid out against its parent, not its containing block  (severity: low, size: medium — Blitz / Taffy)

Taffy sizes an absolutely positioned box against its parent's padding box (`explicit_inset_axes` and `resolve_viewport_cb_location` only repair the inset positions). Shrink-to-fit widths, `left` + `right` stretch (F32j) and `top` + `bottom` heights therefore come from the parent: `<body style="margin:8px"><p style="position:absolute;top:0;left:0">` is 16 px narrower than in Chrome and wraps a few lines differently (A2 with a body margin: 350 words on page 1 against 351); `top:0; bottom:0` in an empty body is 0 px tall, so the window pass dropped it (F30g flows it from `content_extent`). Boxes whose parent or containing block is a grid item or a table cell lay out narrower than in Chrome for the same reason (F30g validator, LOW 6; the sweep's `grid` and `tcell` shapes wrap a few words earlier). Not fixed.

### F95 — text above the top of the page area, and abspos boxes under transformed or clipping ancestors  (severity: low, size: medium — fulgur)

Non-goals of F30g, measured in its step 1 and the 700-document sweep against Chrome (`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f30g/sweep.py`): (a) `bottom: 0` boxes taller than the room above them: ours paints the lines that fall into the top margin (A8c: 369 words against Chrome's 355; in a zero-height relative parent at the top of the body 8 to 14 words where Chrome prints none); (b) an abspos box whose containing block is transformed (`transform: translateX(5px)`) is not drawn at all in a zero-height parent and the same shapes lose text in a clipping one (`overflow:hidden; height:400px`, ours 364 words against Chrome's 140: the clip is not applied to the window geometry); (c) `position: fixed` taller than a page keeps its first page (Chrome prints the same 358 words on page 1). All kept as they were (guarded `Keep`). The guard covers a clipping, transformed or multicol box **anywhere above** the abspos box up to `<body>` (the first F30g build only looked up to the containing block and added blank pages under `overflow:hidden` above it; validator HIGH). Also (d), validator LOW 5: a bottom-anchored box that crosses the top of a page prints 1 to 3 lines above the content box (in the top margin) where Chrome prints them on the page before. Also (e), from F30f: an abspos box that holds an `overflow-hidden` card with a long paragraph stays on the window model and stays lossy: `window_cuts_lines` and `lines_end_below_page` still stop at clipping boxes, because flowing such a root would give its nested out-of-flow boxes window geometry against a placement that no longer exists. The in-flow fragmenter splits such a card since F30f.

### F96 — a box split across pages gets its frame closed on every page (`box-decoration-break: slice` is not implemented)  (severity: low, size: medium — fulgur)

Found by the F30g validator (LOW 7): an absolutely positioned bordered box that crosses a page draws its top and bottom border on every page it touches, where Chrome (`slice`, the initial value) draws the top on the first page and the bottom on the last. The in-flow fragmenter has the same behaviour (see F30h's note on split paragraphs reserving padding on every strip, and F83b: "a bordered box split over pages draws its top border again on every page", fixtures 06 and 08). Not F30g: it reuses the in-flow fragmenter's frame rules. Not fixed. **Narrowed by F30f-d (2026-10-29):** a paragraph with a border or padding (the box is its own inline root, or the child of a block) that is split between its lines is sliced now, with the renderer's open-edge support (`frame_fragment` edges, `sliced_style`) that F96 reuses. Still closed on every page: block boxes whose children are split (the `<section>` card of fixtures 06, 08 and 16, wrappers), absolutely positioned boxes, multicol, table cells (Chrome slices a `td` too: its page-2 lines start at the content top and have no top border, plan §7 step 1), clip boxes with a block size (F30f-i), inline-blocks. F30f review (validator LOW 6): a wrapper with a 1 px border and `height:3000px` gives 4 pages against Chrome's 3, the same cause.

### F97 — a list item whose paragraph carries a split `inline-block`: empty marked content on pages 2+, `LBody` and link tagging  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Found by the F30f `pdf-reviewer` (non-blocking): (a) `<li>` with a split `inline-block` (`q_li.html`) opens its `/LBody` marked-content sequence on every page it has a fragment on, and the carrier has no line on pages after the first, so each of those pages gets one empty sequence (`emptybdc.py`: `[0, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1]`); (b) `LBody` marked content and links without `StructParent` are as on main (see F43 for the list tagging gaps). Fix: do not open the tag when the paragraph slice of the page is empty (`draw_list_item_with_block`, `draw_paragraph_inner_paint`).

### F98 — opacity, `overflow: hidden` and a transform on one element lose the opacity on pages 2+ of a split inline-block inside it  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

From the F30f `vrt-validator` re-run (PR #37, residual MEDIUM, not a regression): `<div style="opacity:.5;overflow:hidden;transform:translateX(10px)"><div style="display:inline-block;width:100%"><p>`
1500 words gives full-opacity text on pages 2+ (darkest grey 132, 9, 9; Chrome 127 on every page). Any two of the three on one element, or the three on separate wrappers, are correct. Without the
inline-block, opacity is already lost on main when transform and opacity share an element (`o-op-tr-p`). Repro: `/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f30f-val/s/o-op-clip-tr.html`.

### F99 — the AGENTS.md budget (200 pages under 1 s) is not met: 1.1-2.4 s on the dev VM  (severity: medium — speed is goal 2, size: unknown — fulgur / Blitz) — **open**: F99a, F99b, F99c and F99d done, `b200-text` / `b200-mixed` under 1 s, `b200-items` still over (1.6 s on the first host; F99c and F99d take about 15 % and 12 % off); what is left is F144, then C2b / C4 / C5

*2026-10-27, owner's decision:* `b200-items` at about 1.2 s is accepted (AGENTS.md goal 2 exception, `docs/benchmarks.md`). The 1 s goal holds for `b200-text` and `b200-mixed` (met). Remaining table speed steps (F99g inline measure memo, M1 margin documents on rayon) are optional, behind the queue; the plan for F99g stays in the scratch notes and is not committed until it is picked up.

Found by B1 (`docs/benchmarks.md`, release CLI of main `d532069`, 4 vCPU Xeon 2.1 GHz, median of 7): `b200-items` (one 4800-row table, repeated `thead`, `tr` / `section` `break-inside: avoid`, `@page` margin
boxes with `counter(pages)`) 2.43 s for 200 pages; `b200-text` (1230 kept-together terms sections) 1.12 s; `b200-mixed` (181 units of table + two-column terms + `overflow:hidden` card + abspos stamp) 1.08 s.
For `b200-items` the same document without the two margin boxes takes about 1.4 s, so the footers cost about 1.1 s (5 ms per page) and the table alone is already over the goal. The 200-page shape
of F78's measurement (800 plain paragraphs, 160 pages, 0.35 s) is fast; tables and margin boxes are what is slow. Not profiled. B1 gates these documents at their current numbers + 30 % (and at hard
budgets 1.5 s, `b200-items` 6 s), so a fix shows up as lower rows and `--update-baseline` in the same PR. Next step: profile `b200-items` with and without the margin boxes (`perf`, release symbols).

**Step 1 (profile, `2026-10-19-f99-speed-profile.md`)**: Parley shaping is 39 % / 28 % of `b200-text` / `b200-mixed`; the footers are 0.96 s of `b200-items`' 2.40 s (every page whose margin-box text differs builds two
Blitz documents that re-parse the UA sheet and the whole `--css` sheet and re-register the fonts, 2.1 ms each); Taffy table track sizing and Stylo style are superlinear in one very long table.
**Step 2, F99a (`2026-10-19-f99a-parallel-construct.md` §7, Blitz fork `b720ad0`, fulgur fork `33f8993`)**: the deferred inline layouts of a document with 256 or more of them are shaped on rayon's global pool, each
worker with a snapshot of *that* document's font context (Blitz's own `parallel-construct` kept a thread-local context from the first document of the process: fonts and generic families of an earlier document
reached later ones, red in `determinism_threads.rs` before the fix). Fixtures 01-16 and the three b200 documents are byte-identical to before at 1 and 4 threads. 200 pages, median of 7, before -> after:
`b200-text` 1126 -> 851 ms, `b200-mixed` 1001 -> 853 ms (both under the goal; baseline lowered to 879 / 862), `b200-items` 2265 -> 2293 ms (no change beyond noise); fixtures unchanged; peak RSS +1-3 MB.
**Left open**: C2a (stop re-parsing the UA and `--css` sheets and re-registering fonts per margin document: about -600 ms on `b200-items`, and about -0.5 ms on every one-page invoice), C2b (build the margin
documents in parallel) or C2c (reuse one document per template), C3 (Taffy / Stylo over thousands of table rows; needs its own profile step), C4 (Parley font selection cache), C5 (Krilla deflate level).
Next step: C2a. Side finding: F100 (the `ERROR: Unexpected token` line each margin document prints).
**Step 3, F99b (`2026-10-19-f99b-margin-documents.md` §7, Blitz fork `a2ee966` + `d4258c6`, fulgur fork `6a6da9b`)**: the margin-box documents of one render share one style and font environment (one Stylo lock, the UA sheet and the
render's CSS parsed once, one font template whose blobs are not copied); it is owned by the render's `MarginBoxRenderer`, `!Sync`, nothing static. Fixtures 01-16 and all of `bench/corpus/` are byte-identical to main at 1 and 4
threads (46 of 46 PDFs), compare numbers unchanged. 200 pages, median of 7, before -> after: `b200-items` 2256 -> 1575 ms (-681 ms, -30 %), `02-long` 53.6 -> 41.6 ms; `b200-text` / `b200-mixed` unchanged within noise;
peak RSS unchanged (243 MB). `b200-items` is still over the goal (1.6 s against 1.0 s): the table is 1.4 s of it on its own, the footers about 0.2 s (inferred).
**Left open**: C3 (Taffy table track sizing and Stylo style over 24 000 cells, superlinear: the biggest remaining cost of `b200-items`; needs its own profile step first, then a plan), then C2b / C2c only if the footers still matter
(about 0.2 s left), C4 (Parley font selection cache), C5 (Krilla deflate level). Next step: the C3 profile step.
**Step 4, F99c (`2026-10-20-f99c-table-scaling.md` §7, Blitz fork `a4b7624`, fulgur fork `92304b1`)**: the C3 profile step and the fixes it justified. Where the time goes in one table of 1200 to 9600 rows (perf, 1 thread): Taffy's grid track sizing
(`GridItem::minimum_contribution`, 30 -> 364 samples for 4x the rows, 1028 at 9600) is the largest superlinear cost, then `table_borders::paint_header_copy`'s scan for the page's first body row (15 -> 146, 701 at 9600; the F138 quadratic),
`Node::child_index` (3 -> 47, 178), the pagination page-name scans (5 -> 54). Done: the page-local lookups in `table_borders` (F138, A1 and A2) and the Blitz `child_index` index hint (B1). Not done, measured below the 20 ms bar: the
`TableContext` clone in `widen_explicit_columns` (3 samples). Output byte-identical to main for all 20 fixtures, the 7 corpus documents and the 16 scaling variants at 1 and 4 threads (86 of 86); compare numbers unchanged. `b200-items`
(A B A B, median of 7-11) -15 / -16 % (2312-2333 -> 1953-1970 ms on a host 1.4-1.5 times slower than the one the baseline is from), `b200-text`, `b200-mixed` and `02-long` unchanged within noise. `--scaling` and its gate are new (`docs/benchmarks.md`).
**Step 5, F99d (`2026-10-20-f99d-taffy-minimum-contribution.md` §7, Taffy fork `7bbedd5` on the v0.9.2 release commit `8f30e39`)**: Taffy's `GridItem::minimum_contribution` scanned every track of the axis, for every item, twice (does any track have an `auto` min, does any have an `fr` max); a table of R rows has 2R+1 row tracks, so a sizing pass cost items x tracks. The two answers are now computed once per call of `resolve_intrinsic_track_sizes` (`AxisTrackFlags::of`) and passed in; the checks are unchanged. Output byte-identical to main (86 of 86). Profile (1 thread, `t`, 4800 rows): the function's self time 233 -> 1 ms, `resolve_intrinsic_track_sizes` inclusive r(4800/1200) 8.4 -> 3.9. `b200-items` (A B A B, median of 7, and 15 interleaved runs) -11 to -16 % (about -210 to -310 ms), the other b200 documents unchanged within noise.
**Left open**: C2b / C4 / C5 and the style and shaping phases (F144, the pagination scans, is done); the 1 s goal for `b200-items` is not met on the 2.80 GHz host (1.6-1.7 s).
**Step 6, F99e (`2026-10-26-f99e-paint-path-overheads.md` §7, fulgur fork `5d370fd7`, `b8435360`, `6069832d`; `bench/baseline.json` rewritten, `b200-items` 1190 ms, `b200-text` 787, `b200-mixed` 785)**: a new callgrind profile of `b200-items` (1 thread, 7.74 G instructions) showed three main-thread costs outside layout: every page stream deflated on the main thread (421 M), a krilla `Font` parsed for every glyph run (27 355 times, 317 M), the bundled font bytes copied per `ConvertContext` (230 x 400 KB, 94.6 M of memcpy). Krilla's `rayon` feature (fulgur feature `parallel-pdf`) deflates on the pool, `KrillaFonts` parses each face once per render, `FontBytes` shares the bundle's bytes. Output byte-identical (fixtures 01-25, the corpus, the scaling variants, 1 / 2 / 4 threads). Interleaved, 15 runs, 2.10 GHz dev VM: `b200-items` 1355 -> 1227 ms, `b200-text` 897 -> 773 ms, `b200-mixed` 878 -> 788 ms; CPU time about equal (A moves work to idle cores). **Left open**: `b200-items` is still over 1 s; the next step is C2b (build the margin documents in parallel: `MarginBoxRenderer::render_page` is 12 % of the profile, about 225 ms; needs a `Sync` margin environment), then the Stylo / Taffy work over the 4800-row table.

### F99d — one very long table is still superlinear in Taffy's grid track sizing: about 360 of 2150 samples at 4800 rows  (severity: medium — speed goal, size: small — Taffy) — **done** (`2026-10-20-f99d-taffy-minimum-contribution.md` §7, fork `subapavel/taffy` `htmltopdf/minimum-contribution` `7bbedd5`)

Measured in F99c (perf, 1 thread, 4800-row `t` variant, symbolised build of main): `taffy::compute::grid::track_sizing::resolve_intrinsic_track_sizes::<TableTreeWrapper>` 44 / 226 / 414 / 1129 samples at 1200 / 2400 / 4800 / 9600 rows, of which `GridItem::minimum_contribution`
30 / 181 / 364 / 1028 (self time 17 / 128 / 291 / 902), i.e. n^1.5 to n^1.8; after F99c it is the largest cost that grows faster than the document. Table layout runs the track sizing a handful of times (callgrind at 1200 rows: 8 `resolve_intrinsic_track_sizes`
for the items table and the totals table, 2 `build_table_context`). Cause, from the source (Taffy 0.9.2 `compute/grid/types/grid_item.rs:497-504`, not yet verified with a patch): `spans_auto_min_track` and `spans_a_flexible_track` are
`axis_tracks.iter().any(..)` over **every** track of the axis, and with no flexible track the second scan never stops early, so each item costs the number of row tracks (about 9600 with gutters) on every call. Fix, output-identical by construction:
compute the two booleans once per axis and pass (they do not depend on the item). It cannot be done from Blitz (the scan is in Taffy, over its own track list), and the whole fix is in a Taffy crate, so under the standing decision it needs a `subapavel/taffy` fork
(**the owner creates it**, branch `htmltopdf/<slug>` on the 0.9.2 source commit, `[patch.crates-io]`, a row in `docs/upstream.md`); nothing was done in this PR. Expected gain: most of the 290-360 ms (of 1.9 s, 1 thread, 4800 rows),
which with the F99c gain would bring `b200-items` to about 1.0-1.1 s on the baseline host (an estimate). Acceptance: `--scaling` r of variant `t` towards 4.0, byte-identical PDFs. Also look at F144 in the same step.

**Done**, as predicted (the cause was confirmed by the patch): `AxisTrackFlags::of` once per call of `resolve_intrinsic_track_sizes`; `GridItem::minimum_contribution` self time at 4800 rows 233 -> 1 ms, `b200-items` -12 % (-228 ms, 15 interleaved runs), PDFs byte-identical. The 1.0-1.1 s estimate above was too optimistic: `b200-items` is 1.6-1.7 s on this host, because parse, style, shaping, convert and paint make up most of the rest. The 1 s goal stays open under F99.

### F146 — Taffy's automatic minimum size looks at every track of the axis, not at the tracks the item spans (css-grid-1 §6.6)  (severity: low — layout differs from the spec only in unusual tables, size: small-medium — Taffy fork, needs a fixture)

Found in F99d (read in Taffy 0.9.2, `grid_item.rs:497-504`, kept as it is there so that F99d stays output-neutral; the spec text is quoted from memory and not verified). The content-based automatic minimum applies when the item "spans at least one track whose min track sizing function is `auto`" and, if it spans more than one track, "none of those tracks are flexible"; Taffy checks all tracks of the axis (`AxisTrackFlags` in our fork computes the same two answers, once). The `item_axis_tracks` slice is used only for its length.
