Fixing it changes sizes when a fixed-width column sits next to an `auto` one (a cell in the fixed column no longer gets a content-based minimum), so it moves pixels: it needs a fixture first (an invoice with `w-24` columns next to an auto column, compared with Chrome), then the change in the fork on `htmltopdf/minimum-contribution` and a golden update. "Known, not reported".

### F147 — Taffy re-runs the row sizing after a column re-run only for items that cross an intrinsic column  (severity: low, size: small — Taffy; side finding of F99d, not verified)

`compute/grid/mod.rs:423` (Taffy 0.9.2) filters the items whose row sizes are redone on `crosses_intrinsic_column`, which reads as if the row re-run should apply to the items that cross an intrinsic *row*, or to all items whose available width changed. Read in F99d and not tested: it may be intended (the spec step is about the items whose column sizes changed), and it is linear for tables, so it is not a speed issue. Check against css-grid-1 §11.1 / §11.5 and a probe in Chrome before touching it; any change moves layout, so it needs a fixture. "Known, not reported".

### F100 — `ERROR: Unexpected token` is printed on stdout, once per `@page` margin-box document and once per document without a doctype  (severity: low — noise, but a library writing to fd 1, size: small — fulgur / Blitz) — **done** (`2026-10-20-f100-stdout-parse-errors.md` §7)

Seen in F99 step 1 (416 lines on `b200-items`, 9 on fixture 02, none on documents without margin boxes), traced in F99a: `blitz-html` `html_sink.rs:149` does `println!("ERROR: {error}")` for every non-fatal html5ever parse
error, to **stdout** (the CLI's `2>/dev/null` does not hide it), and the error here is html5ever's missing-doctype error: the CLI prints it for `<p>hi</p>` and for `<html><head><style>p{}</style></head><body><p>hi</p></body></html>`
and not for `<!doctype html><p>hi</p>`. fulgur's margin-box documents are built from exactly such a wrapper (`render.rs` `"<html><head><style>{}</style></head><body style=...>"`, three places), so each one prints a line;
the fixtures have a doctype, which is why only documents with margin boxes show it. Consequences: a caller that writes the PDF to stdout (the planned HTTP service, `-o -` if the CLI ever gets it) gets text in front
of or inside the stream, and fulgur's `CLAUDE.md` forbids library crates from touching fd 1. No declaration is dropped (the profile suspected one): it is the missing doctype, not the CSS.
Fix options, not tried: (a) Blitz fork: report the error through `log::debug!` instead of `println!` (silences user documents without a doctype too); (b) fulgur fork: start the wrapper with `<!doctype html>` (html5ever's
quirks-mode tree building could move tables inside `<p>`; Blitz's own `Stylist` is built `NoQuirks` regardless, so style should not move, but the margin-box VRT goldens and fixtures 01-16 must be compared). (a) is the safe one.
Covered by: the CLI test `stdout_clean.rs` checks stdout of a document without margin boxes only; extend it with a margin-box document when this is fixed.

### F101 — a character with no glyph in any bundled font is drawn as `.notdef` without a warning  (severity: medium — silent, and blocks PDF/A, size: small — fulgur / ours)

From the F99a `pdf-reviewer` run (pre-existing, identical on main): fixture 04's "⇄" (U+21C4, "Trutnov ⇄ Berlin") has no glyph in Inter or JetBrains Mono. It is drawn as CID 0
(`.notdef`, an empty box or nothing depending on the viewer) with no ToUnicode entry, so text extraction drops it, and `--strict-fonts` does not report it (the F70 `DroppedText` check
only covers text with no glyph run at all). PDF/A-2/3 forbid references to `.notdef` (ISO 19005-2/-3 §6.2.11.8, not confirmed with veraPDF). Fix: count glyph-id-0 clusters in
`extract_paragraph` and report them like `DroppedText` (warning by default, an error with `strict_fonts`); fixture 04 then needs a font that has the arrow or a different character.
Repro: `python3 -I /tmp/claude-0/pdfrev-f99a/scripts/tuc.py` on fixture 04's PDF.

### F102 — an element with `position: running()` is also painted in the body, on page 1  (severity: HIGH — visible duplicate content, size: small — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-19-f102-running-element-twice.md` §7)

From the F99b `pdf-reviewer` run (pre-existing, identical on main): an element taken out of the flow by `position: running(name)` and shown in a margin box through `element(name)` is
also drawn once in the body at its source position on page 1, above the body text, with a second link annotation for its `<a>`. CSS GCPM 3 §2.2.1 (cited from memory): a running element is
removed from the normal flow and only appears where `element()` places it. Repro: `/tmp/claude-0/pdfrev-f99b/docs/own1.html` (page 1, a `/Link` at Rect [87 781 141 793] besides the margin-box ones).
Invoice relevance: a footer or header written as a running element (the GCPM way of putting company details or a logo on every page) shows up twice on page 1.

### F103 — the content of a top margin box sits at the page edge instead of being centred vertically  (severity: medium, size: small — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-20-f103-margin-box-vertical-align.md` §7)

From the F99b `pdf-reviewer` run (pre-existing): `@top-*` box content is drawn at y 832–842 pt, at the very top edge of the page, where css-page-3 §4.2 (cited from memory, the margin box's
`vertical-align: middle` default for top/bottom boxes) centres it in the margin box. Repro: `/tmp/claude-0/pdfrev-f99b/docs/own1.html`.

### F104 — `strip_display_none` removes every author `display: none` from the margin-box CSS  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Found while planning F102: `render.rs` `strip_display_none` (the margin documents re-use the cleaned CSS, whose `position: running()` rewrite is `display: none`) deletes *every* `display: none` declaration, so Tailwind's `hidden`, or an author's own `display: none` on something inside a running element, is shown in the margin box. The rewrite itself and the stripping are unchanged by F102 (changing them moves the margin-box CSS of every GCPM document). Needs a rewrite that marks the running rule instead of a global strip.

### F105 — `parse_gcpm` recognises `running()` only in top-level simple-selector rules  (severity: medium — silent, size: medium — fulgur)

Found while planning F102: not inside `@layer` or `@media`, not with compound selectors (`footer.paticka`), not in a `style=""` attribute. Tailwind v4 puts utilities in `@layer`, so a running element cannot be declared from the Tailwind build; the rule must be plain CSS in a `<style>` (fixture 17 does that). An unrecognised rule is not an error: the element is simply printed in the flow.

### F106 — no warning when a running name is never placed by `element()`  (severity: medium, size: small — fulgur / ours)

Found while planning F102 (case R8 of `crates/htmltopdf/tests/running_elements.rs`): `position: running(x)` with no `@page` rule that has `element(x)` makes the content silently disappear (CSS GCPM 3 §2.2 says so, but a typo in the name looks the same). Warn by default, error in strict mode (standing decision on silent refusals). Raised to **medium** after F102 (`vrt-validator` M1): before F102 the inline-`<style>` path painted an unplaced running element in the body by accident, so the text showed up; now it disappears silently (`scratchpad/cases/unused-no-at-page.html`).

### F107 — an `<img>` in a running element is not painted in the margin box  (severity: medium — blocks a logo in a running header, size: small — fulgur)

Found while writing R4 of F102: `render.rs` converts the margin-box documents with `assets: None` (`MarginBoxRenderer`, the `ConvertContext` it builds), so a bundled image (`--image logo.png=…`) referenced from the running element is not found and nothing is painted (`pdfimages -list` shows no image on a page whose only image is in the margin box; same on main). Repro: `<div class="run"><img src="logo.png"> text</div>` with `@page { @bottom-center { content: element(run) } }`. `running_elements.rs` R4 accepts 0 or 1 image per page and equal on both pages, so it turns into a 1/1 check once this is fixed.

### F108 — a link to an `id` on or inside a running element has no destination  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

From the F102 reviews (expected by the F102 plan §6): since running elements are no longer laid out in the body, `<a href="#x">` where `x` is a running element (or inside one) has no
destination; fulgur warns "unresolved internal anchor" and drops the annotation (main kept it through the body copy), and in tagged output a `/Link` structure element is left without an OBJR —
the same as main for any unresolved anchor (`/tmp/claude-0/pdfrev-f102/docs/hf-anchor.html`, `hf-bogus.html`). Options: point such links at the margin-box copy on the first page that shows it,
or drop the `/Link` structure element when the annotation is dropped (the OBJR-less `/Link` is an F20-class tagging defect on its own).

### F109 — every page is paginated against page 1's content box, so a `:first` / `:left` / `:right` rule or size that gives another page a different box clips or loses content  (severity: medium — silent loss, size: medium — fulgur) — **warning landed** (F6 follow-up), the pagination fix is open — also the F184 canvas phase: with different `@page :first` margins the canvas background phase is off from page 3 (k × page-1 height).

Found while planning F6 (plan §6): the fragmenter paginates every page against page 1's content box (`engine.rs`, `PageStrips`), while size and margins are resolved per page at paint time (`render.rs`). Since F6 the document's `@page :first` rules are honoured, so:
- a `:first` rule with a **smaller margin** than the other pages draws pages 2 and up into their bottom margin (`page_from_css.rs` `t12b_...`, `#[ignore = "F109"]`: `Line095` on page 2 ends at 743.2 pt, the margin edge is at 728.5 pt);
- a **different `size`** is worse (found by `vrt-validator`): `@page{size:A5;margin:10mm} @page:first{size:A3;margin:10mm}` with 299 `<p>Line N lorem ipsum</p>` gives 4 pages and 780 of 1196 words, the tail dropped without a message (the A5 pages are filled to the A3 box and the rest is below the page); the reverse (A4 first, A5 rest) draws text into the bottom margin (yMax 589 against a 567 edge);
- `:left` / `:right` rules with other vertical margins do the same; a larger `:first` margin (the letterhead shape, `t12`) loses nothing but the later pages are only filled to page 1's height.

The warning (fork `2103e35`, `render.rs`): one `log::warn!` per render names page 1's box, the first page whose box differs and whether it is smaller ("content may be clipped or lost, or drawn into the margin") or larger ("filled only as far as page 1's box"); tests `w1_*` in `page_from_css.rs`. Pagination output is unchanged. Fix: paginate against each page's own content box (`resolve_page_settings` per page number in the fragmenter), a fulgur change in `engine.rs` / `pagination_layout.rs`; the warning is then dropped.

### F110 — text in a centre margin box is not centred horizontally  (severity: medium, size: small — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-20-f103-margin-box-vertical-align.md` §7, folded into F103)

Found while writing fixture 18 (F6): `@page { size: A4; margin: 15mm; @bottom-center { content: "PATICKA" } }` prints the text at the left margin edge (x = 28.35 pt on A5 with 10 mm, 42.52 pt on A4 with 15 mm), where css-page-3 §5 and Chrome centre it (`text-align: center` is the default of the `-center` boxes). With `@bottom-left`, `@bottom-center` and `@bottom-right` all present the centre text starts at x = 195 pt of A4, 88 pt left of the page centre (probe `/tmp/claude-0/t5.html`). Fixture 18's footer (5.3 %) and 02's `Strana 2 / 3` suffer from it and from F103; 17 does not (a full-width flex container). `page_from_css.rs` `t11` pins today's x on purpose and says so; change it when this is fixed.

### F111 — text directly in `<body>` (no block around it) that is taller than a page loses its tail without a warning  (severity: HIGH — silent, size: small-medium — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-19-f111-body-text-tail-lost.md` §7)

Found while writing the F6 tests, measured on main's binary (`33b0519`, A4, 15 mm): `<body>Line001<br>Line002<br>…Line200</body>` prints 1 page with 55 of the 200 lines and says nothing; the same lines in `<div>…</div>` or `<p>…</p>` print 4 pages and all 200 (probes `/tmp/claude-0/t6.html`, `t7.html`). F30 / F78 split a paragraph taller than a page "inside a block"; the anonymous block box Blitz makes around inline content of `<body>` is not that case, and the F30 warning ("placed whole and ends N px below the content box") does not fire either. `crates/htmltopdf/tests/page_from_css.rs` wraps its lines in `<p>` and says why; a test that needs the bare shape belongs to this finding.

### F112 — `!important` in an `@page` declaration drops the declaration  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

`@page { size: A5 !important; margin: 0 !important }` prints A4 with 15 mm: the parser rejects the value because of the trailing `!important` token, with no message, and `@page { margin: 0 !important; margin: 20mm }` ends at 20 mm (css-cascade-4: the important declaration wins). Found by `vrt-validator`; repro measured on the release CLI.

### F113 — an `@page` margin in `rem`, `Q`, `calc()`, `vw`, `%`, `auto` or `em` silently becomes 15 mm  (severity: low-medium, size: small — fulgur)

`@page { margin: 1rem }`, `40Q`, `calc(10mm + 5mm)`, `5vw`, `5%`, `auto`, `1em` all print with the 15 mm default: `css_unit_to_pt` knows `mm cm in pt px` only and the declaration is dropped (`parser.rs`). `rem`, `Q` and `calc()` of absolute lengths have a defined value in a page context (css-page-3: `em` / `rem` use the initial font size, `%` and `vw` refer to the page box); at least a warning is due. The plan listed `%`, `em`, `auto` and `vh` as non-goals; this is the missing message.

### F114 — `@page` inside `@media print` (or `@media all`) is ignored  (severity: low-medium, size: small — fulgur)

`@media print { @page { size: A5; margin: 0 } }` and `@media all { @page {…} }` print A4 with 15 mm; `parse_gcpm` reads top-level `@page` only (like F105 for `running()`). Tailwind-built stylesheets put print rules in `@media print`. Repro measured.

### F115 — CLI `--landscape` replaces the document's size instead of rotating it  (severity: low, size: trivial — ours)

`htmltopdf render a5.html --landscape` on an `@page { size: A5 }` document gives A4 landscape (841.89 x 595.28), as `--landscape` always did with the caller's `Page` default. **Decision (F6 follow-up): keep it** (a flag that forces a page property forces it whole, ADR-0005: caller's size is final) and say so in the `--help` text, which now reads "the flag replaces the document's size, it does not rotate it". Rotating the document's own size would need an orientation-only override in the engine (a plan non-goal): revisit when someone asks.

### F116 — no sanity cap on page sizes  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur / ours)

`@page { size: 100000mm }` gives a 283464.56 pt square MediaBox; PDF 1.x viewers limit pages to 14400 units (200 in) and Acrobat refuses larger. Clamp or warn above 14400 pt in `render_v2` (and for `--page-size`).

### F117 — `@page { margin: 0; @bottom-center { content: "x" } }` draws no margin box, silently  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

With a 0 margin the bottom band has zero height and nothing is drawn (`pdftotext` finds no "FOOT"). What Chrome does here (overflowing text, clipped, or nothing) was not measured. Add a warning when a margin box has content and its band is empty.

### F118 — CLI `--page-size -5x50` and `--margin-mm -1` get clap's "unexpected argument '-5' found" message, not ours  (severity: low, size: trivial — ours)

Clap reads the leading `-` as a flag (exit 2 either way, so the input is refused). `parse_page_size` / `parse_margin_mm` have the better messages ("unknown page size", "margin must be a number of millimetres >= 0"); `allow_hyphen_values = true` on the two arguments would reach them.

### F119 — an absolutely positioned box whose text starts at the bottom edge of a page is printed nowhere  (severity: low-medium — a warning exists, size: small-medium — fulgur)

Found by the F111 safety net (`abs_positioned_pagination::nested_abs_offset_resolves_against_cb_not_flow`; `/tmp/claude-0/f111/h/abs5.html`, `@page { size: 100pt 100pt; margin: 0 }`, `<div style="position:absolute;top:100vh">outer on page 2 <div style="position:absolute;top:300vh">inner on page 5</div></div>`): 5 pages, "inner on page 5" is printed, "outer on page 2" is on no page (main's binary the same). The pagination warning says "a paragraph of 1 lines is placed whole and ends 20px below the content box"; since F111 the render adds "content extends 11.8 pt past the bottom page edge on page 1 and is not printed". The abspos start-of-flow logic (F30g) places a one-line box that starts at the strip bottom on the page before. Chrome puts the box on page 2.

### F120 — an `inline-flex` / `inline-grid` box is laid out at its max-content width, not shrink-to-fit  (severity: medium, size: medium — Blitz / Taffy)

Found while writing F111 step 4: `<div style="display:inline-flex"><div>4000 words</div></div>` in A4 prints 1 page and 13 words with the warning "content extends 164556.0 pt past the right page edge" (the whole text is one line); Chrome wraps at the available width and prints 7 pages, 4000 words (`/tmp/claude-0/f111/h/ifx0.html`; same on main's binary). CSS 2.1 §10.3.9 / css-sizing-3: shrink-to-fit is `min(max-content, max(min-content, available))`. With `width:100%` the box is as wide as the page and F30f's "inline-flex … placed whole" warning applies (F30f-b).

### F121 — a link to the `id` of an inline element inside a paragraph that splits between lines is unresolved  (severity: low-medium, size: small — fulgur)

`<a href="#t">` to `<a id="t">TARGET</a>` (or a `<span id>`) in the middle of text taller than a page: "fulgur: unresolved internal anchor #t", the link annotation is dropped. The same with the text in a `<div>` on main's binary: zero-size inline wrappers get no geometry in a split paragraph (`record_split_inline_root_descendants` skips them), so there is no destination. Found by the F111 plan §6 probe (`/tmp/claude-0/f111/h/p-anchor.html`, `p-anchor-div.html`). Fix: record such wrappers with the page and y of their line, or resolve the anchor from the line slice that holds it.

### F122 — `body { height: 100vh; overflow: hidden }` (an app shell) is a clip barrier, CSS propagates body's overflow to the viewport  (severity: low, size: medium — fulgur)

css 2.1 §11.1.1: when `<html>` has `overflow: visible`, body's `overflow` is propagated to the viewport and body itself is not a clip box. Main prints no text at all for 200 lines in such a body (the body block hides its paragraph, F111 item 6); with F111 it prints the first page (53 lines) and warns ("a paragraph of 200 lines in a clipping box shorter than its content is placed whole", "content extends 2197.6 pt past the bottom page edge"). Chrome prints the viewport's worth and clips the rest, so the visible part now matches; propagating the overflow (and not treating body as a barrier) is the follow-up. `/tmp/claude-0/f111/h/p-shell.html`.

### F123 — tagged output: a `<body>` paragraph (body as the inline root) is not in the structure tree  (severity: medium for PDF/UA, low otherwise, size: small — fulgur)

Found by `pdf-reviewer` on the F111 branch, pre-existing on main: with `--tagged`, text directly in `<body>` (fixture 19, `<body>Line001<br>…</body>`) is neither in the structure tree nor marked as an artifact; the tree is an empty `Document`, no `/ParentTree`, no MCIDs. `try_start_tagged` (`fulgur/src/render.rs`, ~1508) returns `None` because `classify_element("body")` is `None`. PDF 1.7 §14.8.2.2 / ISO 14289-1 §7.1 require real content to be tagged. Fix: tag an inline-root body paragraph as `P` (one element, one marked-content reference per page fragment).

### F124 — tagged output: running-element text in margin boxes is not marked as an artifact  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Found by `pdf-reviewer` on the F111 branch, same on main: in a tagged PDF the text of a `position: running()` element painted in a margin box (repeated header/footer) is neither tagged nor inside an `/Artifact` marked-content sequence. Page furniture should be an artifact (PDF 1.7 §14.8.2.2). Repro: fixture 17 with `--tagged`.

### F125 — `bench/baseline.json` has no rows for fixtures 18 and 19  (severity: low, size: trivial — ours)

Found by the F111 validator: the relative speed guard does not cover the two newest fixtures; only the 100 ms hard budget does. Add their rows (min / median from a `bench.py` run on the dev VM) with the next change that touches the bench.

### F126 — a table footer that does not fit under the last body row starts a page of its own  (severity: low–medium, size: medium — fulgur)

Found while writing F36's `long_table_tfoot_first`: with 24 pt rows 120 rows fill four pages exactly, so the footer (one row, `tfoot` in any order) is printed alone on page 5 under a repeated header. Chrome repeats the footer on every page, so it reserves the footer's band on each of them and it never stands alone (not probed at the exact fit). Keeping the footer with the last body row (css-break-3 §4.1) belongs with F12b's reserved band. Repro: `long_table_tfoot_first` of `tfoot_order.rs` with 120 instead of 115 rows.

### F127 — a header group inside a `display: contents` wrapper moves to the top but is not repeated  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Blitz flattens `display: contents` children of a table before classifying them (`flatten_contents`), so since F36 a `thead` in such a wrapper is laid out first; fulgur's `first_table_header_group` (`blitz_adapter.rs`) reads the raw `children`, finds no header group and does not repeat it. Harmless for invoices (a wrapper around `thead` is rare); fix by flattening in `first_table_header_group`.

### F128 — a `display: table-header-group` div is never repeated on continuation pages  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Found by the F36 validator, pre-existing: a div table (`display: table` with a `display: table-header-group` div and a 100-row `display: table-row-group` div) prints its header on page 1 only, on main and after F36; Chrome repeats it. F12's header detection (`first_table_header_group`, `blitz_adapter.rs`) handles `<thead>` tables only. Since F36 a late header-group div moves to the top (as Chrome) but is still not repeated.

### F22c — borders on `<col>` and `<colgroup>` are not part of the collapsed borders  (severity: low — Tailwind has no utility for it, size: medium — Blitz)

F22 resolves the borders of cells, rows, row groups and the table. CSS 2.1 §17.6.2.1 rule 5 also has columns and column groups (between row groups and the table in the order of origins), and Blitz builds no boxes for `<col>` / `<colgroup>` (`TablePart::Column` is skipped), so a `col { border-left: 2px solid }` is ignored. Needs the columns' tracks and styles in `GridBuilder`, two more origins in `EdgeOrigin`, and the segments of the vertical lines in `resolve`. Acceptance: a P-style test with a column rule against a cell border of the same width.

### F129 — free width in a table is divided between auto columns equally, Chrome divides it in proportion to their contents  (severity: medium — column positions of every table narrower than its container, size: medium — Blitz / Taffy)

Found by F22's G6: `width: 300px` with two columns of `Aa` and `Bb` puts the second column at 158.0 px in Chrome, at 159.0 px in Blitz (equal split of the grid's free space; CSS 2.1 §17.5.2.2 distributes in proportion to max-content widths). The fixtures hide it because their columns carry explicit widths or one wide column. Acceptance: the G6 variant with different words matches Chrome's x.

### F130 — columns with an explicit `width` are not stretched when the table is wider than their sum  (severity: medium, size: medium — Blitz)

`table { width: 300px } td { width: 100px }` with two cells: Chrome lays out 150 + 150 px (the extra width is distributed over the columns), Blitz keeps 100 + 100 px. Found by F22's P7 / P8, which therefore expect 100 px columns. Same cause as F129 (the grid's explicit length tracks do not grow). Acceptance: P7 / P8 at x 150.

### F131 — translucent collapsed borders are darker where two lines cross  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

`table_borders` paints horizontal and vertical runs as separate strokes that overlap in the junction squares, so `rgba(0,0,0,.5)` borders show 25 % grey there (P8 checks only the straight parts). Opaque borders are unaffected. Fix: draw the junction square once, or shorten the runs by the crossing band when the colour has alpha.

### F132 — collapsed borders: 1 px offsets from Chrome's lines, and the line under a repeated header  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Blitz rounds the cell edges to whole px, so a line of odd width, snapped down (`floor(c - w/2)`), can stand 1 px above or below Chrome's, which keeps its fractional layout (03 and 20 still show every grid line in the diff for that reason, and the whole table is 1 px higher). At a page break the half lines are `ceil(w/2)` px each, Chrome draws 1.5 px as 2. The line under a repeated header is the better of the header's bottom line and the cut line by width, style and origin; Chrome may resolve it by the box that owns the next row. Acceptance: 03's table lines and P10 on Chrome's pixel rows.

### F133 — committed example PDFs that no longer reproduce  (severity: low, size: trivial — fulgur)

On the dev VM `examples/svg/index.pdf` (`committed_svg_matches_rendered` fails; the example has no table and SVG text uses host fonts, see the fulgur `CLAUDE.md` font caveat), `examples/wasm-demo/index.pdf` and `examples/template/output.pdf` differ from a fresh render with the pinned fontconfig. Not caused by F22 (no table in the first two; the third is rendered at a different size). Regenerate them in CI with `update-examples` or drop the svg cross-check from the local run. Not verified on the commit before F22.

### F134 — `bench/corpus/b200-items.html` is 210 pages after F22  (severity: low, size: trivial — ours)

Every ruled row is now as tall as Chrome's, so the items table is 4 % longer (204 -> 210 pages, `b200-mixed` 201 -> 203); `bench.py` prints a NOTE (outside 195-205) and the relative gate still passes (1662 ms against 1615 ms before). Regenerate the corpus with fewer rows or widen `B200_PAGES`, and rebase the baseline in the same change.

### F135 — a collapsed table with its own border, `border-radius` and `overflow: hidden` is clipped with broken corners  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Found by the F22 pdf-reviewer / vrt-validator. The table's own border is not painted (convert sets its style to `none`); the clip of `overflow: hidden` is the rounded border box, and the collapsed lines are painted inside it at the grid lines, so the outer line is cut at the corners. Chrome draws no table border in that case. Acceptance: a rounded, clipped collapsed table with a 2 px border has intact corners.

### F136 — collapsed borders: a dark junction tick above a vertical line where it meets a dark bar; `double` below 3 px is solid  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Reported by the F22 reviewers (not investigated): on 03's totals row a dark tick shows above a vertical line where it meets the dark bar; probably the vertical run's extension over the crossing horizontal band. `double` of less than 3 px paints as solid (CSS Backgrounds 3, pre-existing in `draw_border_line`). Acceptance: no tick on 03 at 96 dpi.

### F137 — a `thead` is not repeated when the table is split inside a transform or an opacity wrapper  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Found by the F22 pdf-reviewer, pre-existing: `paint_repeated_table_headers` paints the copy at page level, while the cells of a table in a wrapper are dispatched inside it (`draw_under_transform`, `draw_under_opacity`), so the copy is dropped (or, with F22, would lack its lines). Acceptance: a 120-row table inside `opacity: .9` repeats its header on every page.

### F138 — collapsed table borders cost about 14 % on a 4800-row table  (severity: low-medium — speed goal, size: small-medium — Blitz / fulgur) — **done** in F99c (`2026-10-20-f99c-table-scaling.md` §7)

Measured by the F22 validator and again on the dev VM after F22 (CLI wall clock, 5 runs): `bench/corpus/b200-items.html` takes 2.20-2.57 s on the F22 branch against 1.98-2.20 s on main `deacfe9` (+14 %), of which about 3 % is the 6 extra pages (210 vs 204, taller ruled rows like Chrome). `b200-mixed` +3 %, `b200-text` unchanged. Suspects: `resolve` over rows × tracks segments, the per-page segment walk in `table_borders::paint_page`, and the second style flush of `Node::collapsed_border`. Profile with the F99 C3 step (table scaling) and fix there.

### F139 — lines that do not fit in a margin box are dropped without a warning  (severity: low-medium, size: small — fulgur)

Found while probing Chrome for F103 (P7: five lines in a 10 mm band). Chrome centres content taller than its margin box, so it overflows at both ends and paints what lies on the page (L3-L5 of `L1..L5` at y 8.5 / 22.0 / 35.5 pt, L1 and L2 above the page edge). css-page-3 treats the box like a table cell (CSS 2.1 §17.5.3), whose row grows, so the content starts at the top and overflows downwards. Ours starts at the top (as before F103) and prints only the lines that fit the box (`L1`, `L2`); the rest is dropped silently, because the margin document is paginated at the box height and only page-0 fragments are painted (`render.rs`, stage 3). Acceptance: a warning naming the margin box and the number of lines lost (like `DroppedText`), and a decision whether to paint the overflow below the band; `margin_box_align.rs` `t07_overflow_starts_at_box_top` pins today's words.

Amended by the F103 validator: overflow is not always dropped. Since F103 a band shorter than one line paints its text across the band edge (`@page { size: A5; margin: 2.5mm 10mm; @top-center { content: "HEADER"; font-size: 10pt } }`: HEADER at y 0.1-12.2 pt, touching the first body line at 13.1; main dropped it, Chrome drops it too), while a bottom box that runs past the page is still dropped. Whatever the fix, it should warn in both cases.

### F140 — a `@page :first` / `:left` / `:right` margin-box rule replaces the default rule's whole box  (severity: low-medium, size: small — fulgur)

Read from `render.rs` (`effective_boxes`, the `should_replace` test) while writing the F103 plan, not probed against Chrome: for one position the most specific matching rule replaces the less specific one as a whole, so `@page { @bottom-center { content: counter(page); color: gray; font-size: 9pt } } @page :first { @bottom-center { content: "" } }` is right, but `@page :first { @bottom-center { color: red } }` loses the `content` of the default rule instead of keeping it (css-page-3 cascades the declarations of the margin box per property). Acceptance: declarations of the `@page` rule and the pseudo-class rule are merged per property; a test that changes only the colour on `:first`.

### F141 — an author `margin`, `width`, `height` or `display` on a margin box is not applied as css-page-3 section 5.3 says  (severity: low-medium, size: medium — fulgur)

F103 puts the content in the one cell of a table as large as the box's rectangle and forces the cell's margin to 0. So: `margin` on a margin box no longer moves it (before F103 `margin-top: 12px` was a workaround for the missing centring, `examples/header-footer-split`); `width` / `height` do not resolve the box's own size (css-page-3 §5.3.2: the variable dimensions are distributed from them) because the rectangle comes from the max-content distribution of `margin_box.rs`; `display: flex` (or any display other than the default) on the box turns the cell into an ordinary block, which Blitz wraps in an anonymous cell (F50) that aligns at the top. `padding`, `border`, `background`, `color` and the font properties work. Acceptance: §5.3.2 width distribution with an author `width` on `@top-left` / `@top-center` / `@top-right`, margins on margin boxes honoured or documented as ignored, a `display` other than `block` keeping the alignment; probes in Chrome first.

### F142 — the row backgrounds of a long table are looked up by a scan of the whole table on every page  (severity: low-medium — speed, quadratic only for zebra tables, size: small — fulgur)

Found reading `render.rs` `paint_table_row_backgrounds` in F99c (A4 of its plan; not measured, no corpus document has a row background): for each page it walks every background cell of the table, so a 4800-row table with `even:bg-gray-50` rows
costs pages x rows. Fix like F138's: index the backgrounds by page, or find the page's first background cell from the page's node list (`collapsed_cell_of` is the model). Add a zebra variant to `--scaling` first, to see the number.

### F143 — grid lines are `i16` in Blitz's table layout: a table of more than about 16 000 to 32 000 rows would wrap  (severity: low, size: small — Blitz)

Side finding of F99c, read from the source, **not verified**: `layout/table.rs` stores the grid line of a row as `i16` (`grid.row as i16`, near lines 648 and 1096) while Taffy's track indices are `u16`. A table of 32 768 rows or more (about 1300 A4 pages of one-line rows; the `--scaling` ceiling is 9600) would wrap to a negative line.
Decide a limit (a clamp with a warning, or `i32` lines if Taffy takes them) and test with a 33 000-row table. No invoice is that long.

### F144 — fulgur's page-name scans are superlinear on a long table  (severity: low — about 30-50 ms at 4800 rows, size: small — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-20-f144-break-scans.md` §7, fork `subapavel/fulgur` `htmltopdf/srgb-colours` `9eaa005`, `7ae03d0`)

F99c profile, `t` variant: `pagination_layout::has_page_name_change_below`, `has_forced_break_below` and `blitz_adapter::walk_used_page_names` together are 5 / 18 / 54 / 81 samples at 1200 / 2400 / 4800 / 9600 rows (excess over linear about 34 ms at 4800), and
`relayout_position_fixed::collect_position_fixed_ids` 2 / 4 / 12 / 16: each asks "is there a break or a named page below this node" by walking the subtree below it, for every row of the table. Compute the answers in one bottom-up pass.

**Done.** Correction found when planning: the work was not quadratic (nothing in this path runs per page) but O(n x levels): the gates ask for every in-flow child that has children, up to twice, at the body, the `tbody` and every row, and each answer walked the child's whole subtree. `SubtreeFacts` answers both questions from two `Vec<u16>` levels built in one pass; the old walks stay as the oracle under `debug_assert_eq!`; no used page-name table is built without a named page. On `t` at 4800 rows the four functions go 52 -> 0 samples (1 thread, perf); output byte-identical (92 of 92). Wall clock: 1 thread -78 ms on `t` at 4800 rows, 4 threads inside the VM's noise (`docs/benchmarks.md`).

### F148 — `walk_used_page_names` builds a `String`-cloning table entry for every element when the document names a page  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Left by F144: on `t-names` (the `b200-items` table with `page: items` and a forced break after it) `blitz_adapter::walk_used_page_names` is 5 / 14 / 18 samples at 1200 / 2400 / 4800 rows (1 thread), linear (r 3.6), about 1 % of the render. Each element gets a `BTreeMap` insert and two `Option<String>` clones, and the sibling comparison compares strings. Intern the names (`u32` ids, `None` = 0) and keep the table as a `Vec` indexed by slab key. Only documents that use `page:` pay for it; measure on `t-names` before and after, output must stay byte-identical.

### F149 — `fragment_abspos_flows` walks the whole DOM on every render, also when nothing is absolutely positioned  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Seen while measuring F144 (not one of its candidates): `pagination_layout::abspos_flow::abspos_boxes_in_tree_order` is 13 samples (1 thread, about 0.7 %) on `b200-items`, which has no `position: absolute` element: one recursive walk that makes a `Vec` of kids per node (`before`, `laid_out_children`, `after`) and inserts every id into a `BTreeSet`. It could skip the walk when no style says `absolute` (a flag from the style pass), or iterate without the `Vec` and the set. Measure on `b200-mixed` (it has abspos stamps) as well.

### F150 — a Blitz panic (`document.rs:1146`, "invalid key") aborts the render on some generated documents  (severity: medium-high — a crash on user input, size: unknown — Blitz) — **done** (`2026-10-20-f150-display-contents-construction.md` §7)

Found by the F144 validator while fuzzing (250 seeded random trees mixing `page:`, `break-*`, flex / grid / `display: contents` / inline-block / tables, abspos / fixed / float, `vertical-rl`, `columns`, tables with per-row breaks and names): 7 of 250 documents panic in Blitz at `document.rs:1146` ("invalid key", a slab lookup of a removed or never-inserted node) on main `7397e3a` and on the F144 branch alike. The generator and the 7 documents were in the validator's scratch directory and are gone; re-create a generator with the same ingredients, bisect a failing document to a minimal repro, and add it as a test. Expected: no panic (and our API should turn any engine panic into an `Err`, see F10's `catch_unwind` note).

### F151 — `eprintln!` in Blitz and fulgur writes to stderr from library code  (severity: low, size: small — Blitz / fulgur)

Left over from F100 (fd 2 is not forbidden, so F100 did not touch it): `blitz-dom` `node/element.rs:305`, `:317`, `:348` (`Warning: unsupported property {name}` / `invalid property value`, from the style-attribute and property setters), fulgur `link.rs:91` (`fulgur: unresolved internal anchor #{id}`) and `convert/mod.rs:340`, `:359` (a debug tree dump). A caller with its own logger never sees them and cannot turn them off. Fix: `log::warn!` for the link and the two property messages (content or style the author wrote is dropped), `log::debug!` for the dump; Blitz in the Blitz fork, fulgur in the fulgur fork (a fork change there costs rebase work, so batch it with the next fulgur fork change). The link message is already a user-visible diagnostic: check how `htmltopdf` reports unresolved anchors first. Not verified whether any fixture or corpus document reaches them (none printed a line on stderr in F100's matrix).

### F152 — inline SVG `<text>` uses host fonts, and `usvg` prints to stdout when a host colour font is picked  (severity: medium — breaks the no-host-fonts invariant, size: medium — Blitz) — **raised to HIGH (2026-10-26)**: the F177b vrt-validator traced it: any inline `<svg>` with `<text>` makes Blitz's global `FONT_DB` (`blitz-dom/src/util.rs`, `load_system_fonts()`) open about 118 files under /usr/share/fonts and embed a host face (`LiberationSerif`, `FreeSerif`) — the output depends on the host, against AGENTS.md goal 3 and the no-host-fonts invariant. — **done** (`2026-10-26-f152-svg-text-bundled-fonts.md`, ADR-0007)

Already an open gap row in `docs/upstream.md`: `blitz-dom` hardcodes `fontdb::Database::load_system_fonts()` for inline `<svg>` parsing (`blitz-dom` `util.rs`), so `<text>` inside an inline SVG is shaped with whatever the host has installed and the same document can give different PDFs on two machines (AGENTS.md: "Determinism - never read host fonts"). Found while reading F100's stdout work: `usvg 0.45.1` `src/text/colr.rs:188` and `:288` call `println!` when a host COLRv1 font with a sweep gradient or an unsupported blend mode is picked, which writes to stdout (the CLI's `-o -` stream) and cannot be turned off. Fix in the Blitz fork: build the `fontdb::Database` from the bundled fonts of the document (the `FontContext` collection) and never call `load_system_fonts()`; check that `usvg` is then never handed a COLRv1 host font. Test: an inline SVG with `<text>` renders the same with and without an extra host font directory (`FONTCONFIG_FILE`), and prints nothing to stdout.

### F153 — everything inside a `display: contents` element is silently lost when it has block children with inline content  (severity: HIGH — silent, size: medium — Blitz) — **done** with F150 (`2026-10-20-f150-display-contents-construction.md` §7)

`<div style="display:contents"><p>Faktura</p><p>Celkem</p></div><p>end</p>` prints only `end`: `collect_layout_children` computed the layout children of each child of the contents element instead of placing the child, so a `<p>` (an inline root) was never laid out. Tailwind's `contents` class produces exactly this. Same cause as F150, one fix.

### F154 — Parley trims the whitespace at both edges of every inline element's text: `a <span>b </span>d` prints `a bd`, `a<span> b</span> d` prints `ab d`  (severity: medium-high — silent, visible on ordinary invoice markup such as `<b>Splatnost: </b>2. 11.`, size: small-medium — Blitz / Parley) — **done** (`2026-10-21-f154-span-edge-whitespace.md` §7)

Found by the F150 fuzz test. On main `ff67bde`, plain HTML without `display: contents`: `<p>a <span>b </span>d</p>` prints `a bd`, `<p>a <span>b <i>c </i></span>d</p>` prints `a b cd`, `<p>a<span> b</span> d</p>` prints `ab d` (Chrome keeps every space). Cause: Parley 0.6 `resolve/tree.rs` `push_uncommitted_text` does `trim_start()` when `is_span_first` and `trim_end()` when `is_span_last`, and `push_style_span` / `pop_style_span` call it for every span, so the space at the inner edge of an element is dropped instead of collapsing against its neighbours (CSS Text 3 §4.1.1, quoted from memory: collapsing is across element boundaries). A fix belongs in the Blitz fork (do not push the span's edge whitespace through `trim_*`; push it into the surrounding text, or collapse in `build_inline_layout_recursive` before calling Parley) or a Parley fork; check `<b>Celkem </b>` in the fixtures first. The F150 contents wrappers avoid it by opening no span when they change nothing (`contents_needs_span`), the fuzz generator avoids edge whitespace inside elements. Test: the three strings above, compared with Chrome.

### F155 — a `flex-wrap: wrap` row inside a `display: table-cell` of a `table-layout: fixed` table is laid out wider than the cell, its last items run off the page  (severity: low-medium, size: unknown — Blitz / Taffy)

From the F150 fuzz test (seed 1245, `DISPLAY_CONTENTS_FUZZ_SEEDS=1246`; the document has no wrappers in the failing part, the plain and the wrapped render agree): a `<p>w0109 w0110</p>` item of such a row starts at x 546 pt on a 595 pt page and `w0110` is past the page edge, so it is not printed. Chrome puts `w0109` at x 480 pt on the same line as `w0110`. The nesting is table cell > `display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap` > `<p style="max-width:150px">`. Needs a minimal repro (delta-debug the seed's body) before anyone fixes it; unverified whether it is the cell's available width (table-cell content measured at max-content) or the flex container.

### F156 — `text-decoration` on a `display: contents` element, and the UA underline of `<a style="display:contents">`, are drawn on its text; Chrome draws none  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur / Blitz)

Probe of the F150 step 0 (`<span style="display:contents;text-decoration:underline;color:blue">` and `<a href style="display:contents">`): Chrome prints the text blue and not underlined (the property applies to boxes, and the element has none); we draw the underline because the style span of the contents element carries its brush and fulgur's `get_text_decoration` reads the brush node's own `text-decoration-line`. The link annotation of the `<a>` is created (Chrome makes the text a link too). Fix: `get_text_decoration` returns no decoration for a node whose `display` is `contents`, or the span takes the decoration of the first non-contents ancestor (fulgur fork; batch with the next fulgur change).

### F157 — the marker of the first `<li>` of a list starts about 2.4 pt to the right of the others'  (severity: low, size: small — Blitz)

`<ol><li>Prvni</li><li>Druhy</li><li>Treti</li></ol>` at 16 px: `1.` at x 60.8, `2.` at 58.4, `3.` at 58.5; Chrome has 58.5 for all three. Same on main. The marker box is probably sized from the first item's text width (`list.rs` measures the marker with a parley layout per item); look at how the width of `1.` is used.

### F158 — an inline element with a larger `font-size` inside a fixed `line-height` sits lower than in Chrome  (severity: low, size: medium — Blitz / Parley)

`p{line-height:20px}` with `<span style="font-size:30px">B</span>`: our glyph box of `B` starts 6 pt lower than Chrome's (y 36 against 30 pt) and the surrounding text 3 pt lower (47 against 44 pt); same without `display: contents`. The line box of the span is floored to the root line height (`inline_span_style`), Chrome centres the span's half-leading around its own glyphs. Probe: `/tmp` only, re-create from this paragraph.

### F159 — leftovers of the `display: contents` work (F150 / F153), each unverified or not reached  (severity: low)

(a) `select { display: contents }` shows its `<option>` text (css-display-3 §2.7 "unboxing" is not applied; Stylo has it for gecko only). (b) `display: contents` on `::before` / `::after` themselves. (c) Pseudo-elements of a contents element inside a table context (`table.rs` `flatten_contents` ignores them). (d) Pseudo-elements of inline elements two or more levels deep in an inline formatting context are never flushed: `flush_inline_pseudos_recursive` (`construct.rs`) tests the display of the parent (`node_id`) where it means the child's (from reading; the contents case was fixed in F150 by also recursing into contents children). (e) Anonymous blocks of a previous `resolve` are leaked when the tree is resolved again (existing behaviour). (f) ~~A pseudo-element with a trailing space in its `content` (`content: "Pred "`) loses the space, an instance of F154.~~ **Done** with F154 (`inline_whitespace.rs` W10). (g) fulgur `prev_plain` (`pagination_layout.rs`, F111) may have lost its only trigger now that a wrapper does not make body an inline root; check and remove in a follow-up.

### F160 — `text-transform` is not applied to the text of `::before` / `::after` content  (severity: low-medium, size: small — Blitz)

Found while building fixture 21: `.x::before { content: "Poznámky"; text-transform: uppercase }` prints `Poznámky`, Chrome prints `POZNÁMKY`; same on main and without `display: contents`. `flush_pseudo_elements` (`construct.rs`) creates the text node of the pseudo-element but does not set its `parent`, and `build_inline_layout_recursive` reads `text-transform` from `node.parent` (F4). Set the parent of the new text node to the pseudo-element node; test with `uppercase`, `lowercase`, `capitalize` on `::before`. Fixture 21 spells its label in capitals until this is fixed.

### F161 — a multicol container loses the words of a paragraph that wraps in a column: `<div style="columns:2"><p>w0009</p><p>w0014 w0015 w0016 w0017 w0018 w0019 w0020</p></div>` prints 2 of 8 words  (severity: HIGH — silent, size: medium — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-21-f161-multicol-lost-words.md`)

Found by the F150 fuzz test (top-level multicol containers, seeds 14, 141, 157 and others, plain document without any wrapper) and reproduced on main `ff67bde`: the second paragraph, seven words on 300 pt in a 250 pt column, prints only its last word; a 12-word paragraph after a one-word one prints all 12, so it depends on where the column break falls (a paragraph whose first line ends the first column?). Also on main and in plain documents: `column-span: all` across pages loses a hundred words (`<p>top</p>` + 120 `<p>` + `<h2 style="column-span:all">` + 120 `<p>` in `columns:2`: 171 of 241 words), the known gap of F30d-b made visible as silent loss. Chrome prints every word in both. Find the lost lines in `layout_column_group` / the column flow (F30d) and test with these shapes. The fuzz test skips its 97 % floor for documents with a multicol container until this is fixed.

### F162 — `<ol style="display:contents">` numbers its items from 2  (severity: low, size: small — Blitz)

`<ol class="c"><li>x</li><li>y</li></ol>` with `.c { display: contents }` prints `2. x 3. y`; Chrome shows the markers without numbers (the list has no box, so no counter scope: `li` is `list-item` and counts against the nearest list-item ancestor's counter). `collect_layout_children` runs `collect_list_item_children` with `index = 1` for any `ol`, and the loop for the contents path starts one too high. Check Chrome's markers first (unverified: the report says "without a number").

### F163 — a Blitz panic in `table.rs:1373` (index out of bounds) on random documents with table displays  (severity: medium-high — a crash on input, queue right after F154, size: unknown — Blitz) — **done** (`2026-10-21-f163-table-pseudo-elements.md` §7)

From the vrt-validator's generator (about 180 of 400 seeds), on main and on the F150 branch alike, so not caused by `display: contents`. The F150 fuzz generator (`display:table` / `table-row` / `table-cell` divs) does not hit it. Re-create a generator from the validator's ingredients, bisect to a minimal document, add it as a test; the library turns the panic into `EnginePanic`, but the document is lost.

### F164 — tagged PDFs: three gaps around links and wrappers  (severity: low-medium, size: medium — fulgur)

Reported by the F150 pdf-reviewer, all on main or independent of F150's fix: (a) links inside flex and grid items get no `/Link` struct element and no OBJR; (b) in `<a>inl <div>blk</div> inl2</a>` the annotation after the block has no `/StructParent`; (c) every wrapper `<span>` becomes an empty `/Span` struct element. The F150 review fix (one parent per annotation) only decides which `/Link` group holds the annotation when a link spans several paragraphs: the first one, later groups hold content only.

### F165 — a `display: contents` wrapper after a multicol container (or after a block containing one) is mispositioned or loses the multicol's words  (severity: medium-high, size: small — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-21-f161-multicol-lost-words.md`)

Found by the third F150 validator run (pre-existing: main loses the same content, so F150 does not make it worse). Repro: `<div><div style="columns:3"><p>…12 short paragraphs…</p></div><div style="display:contents"><p>Q</p></div></div>` puts Q at y 223 pt wrapped against 103 pt unwrapped; a 120-word multicol followed by a wrapped `<p>` loses all 120 of its words (108 of 228 printed). Cause, from reading: `propagate_height_delta` (`crates/fulgur/src/multicol_layout.rs:1646`) shifts `node.children[idx+1..]` by the multicol's height change, and a contents sibling has no box, so its box children are never shifted. Fix direction: walk `box_tree_children` there. `<w>MC Q</w>` and `<w>MC</w> Q` are fine.

### F166 — a `display: table` made of divs, nested two levels deep in table cells with `table-layout: fixed`, lays out wider than the page: the words past the right edge are not printed  (severity: low-medium, size: unknown — Blitz / Taffy)

From the F150 fuzz test after it started to put white space at the inner edges of inline elements (`fuzz_long`, seed 2767, `cargo test --release -p htmltopdf --test display_contents -- --ignored fuzz_long`; the only failing seed of 2000). The plain document prints 174 of its 182 words (`w0124`, `w0126`, `w0129`, `w0131`, `w0133`, `w0136`, `w0138`, `w0140` are missing, every other word of two paragraphs in one cell). It is not about white space: with every edge space moved out of its element the render is the same on main's binary and on the F154 branch (same 8 words missing). The nesting is `display:table` > row > cell > `display:table;table-layout:fixed` > row > cell > `<p style="max-width:150px">`. Same family as F155 (content of a fixed-layout cell measured at max-content, then clipped by the page), but no flex-wrap in this tree. Needs a minimal repro (delta-debug `out/scratch/fz2/2767.txt` from the dump option `DISPLAY_CONTENTS_FUZZ_DUMP`); the 64 seeds of CI pass. The 2000-seed `fuzz_long` of the F163 generator (tables with pseudo-elements, no rows, bare cells) fails on seed 1823 only: 238 of 285 words, a nested fixed table in a cell, the same 238 with the pseudo rules removed and on main's binary.

### F167 — the marker of `list-style-position: inside` is narrower than Chrome's: text after it starts about 6.6 pt too far left  (severity: low, size: small — Blitz)

From the F154 probe W16 (`<ul style="list-style-position:inside"><li>x</li></ul>`, Inter 14 px, A4, 15 mm margins): Chrome prints `x` at x = 89.25 pt, ours at 82.64 pt (marker `•` followed by a space, ours is 10.1 pt wide). Chrome does not extract the bullet as text (it draws a shape), so only the position of the text is known. With leading spaces in the item (`<li> <span> x</span></li>`) both engines give the same position as without them, so F154 does not change it. Check Chrome's marker box (a disc of 0.35 em with a margin) against the `Marker::Char` + space that `build_inline_layout_into` pushes.

### F163b — `::before` / `::after` of a `tr`, a row group or a `display: contents` wrapper inside a table are dropped  (severity: low-medium, size: medium — Blitz / fulgur)

Left by F163 (`table_pseudo.rs` T14, two `#[ignore = "F163b"]` cases). Chrome 148 prints `tr::before{content:"R"}` as an extra cell at the start of the row (`Ra`) and `tbody::before{content:"G"}` as an anonymous row above the group's rows; ours prints neither and does not panic. Cause: rows and row groups are not constructed (`collect_row` clears their damage, `table.rs`), so `flush_pseudo_elements` never runs for them and their pseudo boxes are never created. Fixing it touches `collect_row`, `collect_row_group` and `flatten_contents` (a pseudo of a contents wrapper in a table is the same family) and fulgur's `row_cells` / `collect_table_cells`, which read the row's `children`. Pseudos of cells work (T14 `td::before`).

### F163c — a `display: table-cell` `::before` on a table stops its `thead` from repeating on continuation pages  (severity: medium — a regression of F163 for this shape, size: small — fulgur / Blitz) — **done** (`2026-10-26-f163c-table-cell-pseudo-thead.md`)

Found by the F163 vrt-validator: `table::before{content:"BEFORE-ONCE";display:table-cell}` on a table with a `thead` and 130 body rows (3 pages) prints the header on page 1 only (263 words; Chrome repeats it, 267). Main repeated the header (but dropped the pseudo text). `display: block` / `table-row` pseudos and `::after` alone are fine; `border-collapse` either way. Suspect: the anonymous row that wraps the cell pseudo is planned so that F12's header detection (header cells as the prefix of the table's items) no longer sees the `thead` first, or fulgur's `convert_table` direct-`Cell` arm. Fix with a test in `table_pseudo.rs` (header on every page, word count = Chrome). Rare in invoices (a table-cell pseudo on the table itself), hence filed rather than blocking F163.

### F168 — a trailing `\n` at the end of a `white-space: pre`, `pre-line` or `pre-wrap` block adds an empty line; Chrome adds none  (severity: low, size: small — Blitz)

From the F154 validator, pre-existing (same on main). `<p style="white-space:pre-line">a\n</p><p>z</p>`: `z` is at y 72.9 pt in ours and 58.1 pt in Chrome (CSS Text 3 §4.1.3 / §5: a preserved segment break at the very end of a block does not create an empty line box).

### F169 — an underline hangs under a trailing space at a soft wrap  (severity: low, size: small — Blitz / fulgur)

From the F154 validator, pre-existing for interior spaces and extended to edge spaces by F154 (plan risk §6). `<p style="width:70px"><a href="#">aaa bbb </a>ccc</p>`: the underline runs about 3 pt past `bbb`; Chrome stops at `bbb` (the collapsible space at the end of a wrapped line hangs and is not decorated).

### F170 — a `position: absolute` `::before` / `::after` of a table box is not painted  (severity: low, size: small — Blitz / fulgur, unverified cause)

Found in F163 (`table_pseudo.rs` T15, `#[ignore = "F170"]`). `table::before{content:"B";position:absolute}` on a one-row table: Chrome prints `B` at the static position (x 0, over the first row, which is not moved); ours prints nothing and does not panic. The same `position: absolute` on a DOM child of a `display: table` box is painted (probe T15g), and on the `::before` of a block it is too (T15i), so it is the pseudo box wrapped in the table's anonymous cell. Unverified: the box may be placed out of the paintable area, or fulgur's out-of-flow walk reads the DOM `children` of the anonymous cell and misses the pseudo box. A floating pseudo (T15d) is fine.

### F171 — `border-left: 1px solid #e5e7eb` (a single side with a colour) is painted black  (severity: low-medium, size: unknown — fulgur / Blitz)

Found while drafting fixture 23, on main's binary: `<div style="border-left:1px solid #e5e7eb;padding:8px">` and the same on a `display: table-cell` box draw a black line (also at 3 px); `border:1px solid #e5e7eb` is the right colour. Not checked: the other three sides, `border-left-color` written separately, Tailwind's `border-l` (which sets the width and takes the colour from the preflight variable). Fixture 23 uses full borders to stay clear of it.

### F172 — an `inline-table` in a line is baseline-aligned by its last row; Chrome uses its first row  (severity: low, size: small — Blitz)

Found in F163 (probe T10, `<p>x <table style="display:inline-table"><tr><td>b</td></tr></table> y</p>` with `table::before{content:"B"}`): Chrome puts `y` on the line of the first row (`B`, y 15.0 pt), ours on the line of the last (`b`, y 33.0 pt). CSS 2.1 §17.4: the baseline of an inline-table is the baseline of its first row. Without a pseudo row the difference is the table's vertical offset only (2.2 pt, T10n). `table_pseudo.rs` T10 asserts the words and the column, not the position of `y`.

### F173 — leftovers of F161: a multicol container with a spanner and bare text keeps the old strip path; spanner margins are dropped; a merged column slice is silent in release builds  (severity: medium — (a) is silent loss, size: small-medium — fulgur)

From the F161 plan's review of fulgur `5aa953f6`. (a) `multicol_container` (`pagination_layout.rs`) rejects a container when one of its column groups has an empty flow (bare text, or a `<div>` of text, between spanners), and such a container goes back to the strip path that loses the rows after a spanner over pages; a container of spanners only (`groups.is_empty()`) too. Repro to write: `columns:2` with bare text, an `<h2 style="column-span:all">` and 120 more `<p>` over two pages. (b) The height of a spanner is its border box in the layout pass and in the fragmenter, so UA `h2` margins vanish around it (Chrome keeps them; fixture 24 uses padding instead). (c) `merge_line_slices` and the column-order check in `layout_column_group` are `debug_assert!`s: in release a second slice of one paragraph in a column is merged silently, and the union range is right only for contiguous slices; turn them into a `log::warn!` or prove them unreachable. (d) `shift_box_roots` follows DOM children: a multicol container itself inside a contents wrapper (`<w><div columns>…</div></w><p>Q</p>`) and siblings in anonymous blocks are untested. (e) Pre-existing losses the fuzz test still shows: seed 153 of the CI generator at `DISPLAY_CONTENTS_FUZZ_SEEDS=200` (no multicol, 171 of 180 words on main and on the branch) and seed 1590 of `fuzz_long` (multicol: main 195, F161 228 of 259; 1823 is F166); one or two words of 1295, 1907, 2039, 2289 (the same on main, under the 97 % floor). Bisect each to a minimal document. (f) A container with spanners and a box taller than a page in its flow keeps the old path (fork `1c1c164b`), as all spanner containers did before F161; the fragmenter should cut such a box (F30d-b). **F198b note (2026-10-29):** the box can now be a clip card or hold inline boxes, and still takes this path when the container has a spanner: `spanbefore` / `spanafter` / `spanmid` (`out/scratch/f198v/`): main prints 361 of 2500, 361 of 2600 and 733 of 2400 words on 3, 2 and 4 pages, Chrome all of them on 4 pages, and the warning wrongly says "a paragraph of 423 lines" (the spanner path, not the box path, reports it). The fix touches the F161 spanner path: exempt the boxes of `McContainer::boxes` from the spanner gate, as F174 exempted tables. (g) So does a container with a spanner taller than a quarter of a page (fork `49e16712`): the fragmenter's own spanner cut (`nested_line_split` / `fragment_nested_inline_root` from `fragment_multicol_container`) drew a spanner taller than a page over the group above it, and a spanner that does not fit the room is moved whole; the old path loses words of such a spanner and of groups crossing pages, as on main (fulgur renders of `columns:2` with a 400-word `column-span:all` `<p>` between two groups of one-word `<p>`, on 300 pt pages: with 40 per group a020-a036 and b020-b033 are lost, with 6 per group the spanner's words from s171). Fix the fragmenter's cut, cut a breakable spanner at the page end instead of moving it, then drop the quarter-page gate. (h) A box in a column of a multicol container with `padding` and `border` overflows the page bottom and its last line is lost (`<section style="columns:2;padding:10px;border:1px solid">` with a `<div>` of paragraphs over a page break: w0382, w0383 lost, words drawn to 832 pt against a 799 pt content bottom; the same on main without a spanner; `padding: 40px` alone loses 28 words on both); the room seems not to account for the container's box. Repros in the F161 validator's `out/scratch/t11/` shapes (re-create from this description). **F198 note (2026-10-29):** (f) the spanner gate in `multicol_container` still returns `None` for a container with a spanner and a box taller than a page, boxes in `boxes` could be exempted as tables are; (h) is the fill rule for the first atom of a partial page, not the box path, and F198 does not touch it.

### F174 — a table inside a multicol container loses most of its rows: 77 of about 285 words  (severity: HIGH — silent, size: unknown — fulgur, F30d-b) — **done** (`2026-10-26-f174-table-in-columns.md`)

Found by the F163c vrt-validator (`out/scratch/f163c/g_multicol_*.html`, not committed; re-create: `<div style="columns:2">` holding a `<table>` with a `thead` and 130 rows of two cells, A4): main and the F163c branch print 77-78 words, Chrome 284-286. The table is a box in the F30d flow (placed whole, F30d-b) or takes the old strip path; either way the rows past the first column are not printed and nothing is reported. Find which path takes it, then cut a table in columns like a block (or at least warn). Invoices rarely put the items table in columns, but terms tables in a two-column block are plausible.

### F174b — a table inside a multicol container is laid out at the column width, not shrink-to-fit  (severity: low, size: small-medium — fulgur)

From the F174 plan: `layout_column_group` (`multicol_layout.rs`) gives every child of a column the known width `col_w`, so a `width: auto` table is as wide as its column; Chrome sizes it to its content (CSS 2.1 §17.5.2). In the F174 shapes the second table column's words are up to 118 pt right of Chrome's. Fix: lay a table child out with an available width, not a known one. Fixture 25 uses `width: 100%` and is not affected.

### F174c — a table inside a wrapper `<div>` in a multicol container is still one box: placed whole, overflows, most rows lost (warned)  (severity: medium, size: medium — fulgur, F30d-b)

F174 cuts a table that is a direct child (box-tree child) of the multicol container. In `<div style="columns:2"><div><table>…130 rows…</table></div></div>` the wrapper is a `FlowItem::Block`: 107 of 269 words printed, with the "placed whole in its column and overflows the page" warning. The general F30d-b case (a block in the column flow is not entered); the wrapper's box would need a fragment per column. **F198 note (2026-10-29):** a wrapper that holds a table taller than a page is now `mc_box` = `None` (the box path cannot cut the table), so it still takes this old path and warns; once `mc_box` can describe a table as `McTable` row bands inside an `McBox` (atoms of the same planner), this finding and F198's table case go away together. A table that fits a page inside a card is a `Leaf` and is placed whole. **F198b note (2026-10-29):** `out/scratch/f198b` / `f198v/table.html`: the 130-row table in a card in two columns prints 112 of 260 words on main and now (Chrome 260 on 2 pages); `mc_box_at` refuses the box because the table is a child taller than a page that is not describable. The F198b clip card does not change that. The F174 vrt-validator found the same old path (byte-identical to main, rows lost past the page edge with the warning) for a table with a `<caption>` (it counts as wrapped: 105 of 249 words), a table in a `ul`/`li` or a flex item in the columns, and a table with `opacity`.

### F174d — a multicol container with a table is moved to the next page when its balanced height would fit the room left  (severity: medium, size: small-medium — fulgur)

From the F174 vrt-validator (pre-existing: main does the same or worse). Four `<p>`, then a padded and bordered `columns:2` container holding a 47-row table, then a paragraph: we print 2 pages (the container starts at the top of page 2, page 1 almost empty), Chrome 1. With 36 paragraphs before a 3-column 14-row table the container moves when 201 pt are left; the same content as paragraphs stays on page 1. The decision to move the container (F28 / `multicol_wants_split` with the container's Taffy height, or the first unit) seems to use the unbalanced single-column height of the table; 30 of the validator's 300 fuzz cases print 2 pages where Chrome prints 1 (main: 23 of them 2 pages, 7 three). Repros: re-create from this description (the validator's `out/scratch/f174/fz/z134_r47_p4.html`, `z068_r14_p36.html`, `z047_r5_p45.html`).

### F174e — smaller F174 follow-ups  (severity: low, size: small each — fulgur)

From the F174 vrt-validator and pdf-reviewer: (a) a short table (a header and 2-3 rows) in a balanced container is split between the columns with its header repeated in column 2, where Chrome keeps it in one column (8 words against Chrome's 6; nothing lost); (b) `break-before: column` on a `tr` is ignored (Chrome breaks there); (c) the repeated header copies get no link annotations (Chrome links them); (d) the line under a header copy takes the body rule's colour (gray-200) where Chrome draws the header's (gray-400) — the F12 copy on one page does the same, F22's tie rule, the F132 family; (e) header copies are written at the end of the page's content stream, so `pdftotext -raw` reads a copy after its piece (mark them `/Artifact /Pagination /Header` and emit them before the piece); (f) under collapsed borders column 2's rows sit 0.75 pt higher than column 1's; (g) the footer group is not repeated per piece (F12b).

### F177 — an image inside an SVG bypasses the image checks: a huge header aborts the process, a broken one fails the whole document  (severity: HIGH — robustness, size: small-medium — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-26-f177-svg-image-checks.md`)

From the F99e pdf-reviewer, the same on main: an inline SVG with `<image href="data:image/png;base64,…">` whose header claims 100000 x 100000 RGBA makes the process abort ("memory allocation of 40000000000 bytes failed", exit 134); `catch_unwind` cannot catch an allocation failure. A truncated embedded raster fails the whole render (`PDF generation error: Image(…, "unexpected end of file")`, exit 1) where a top-level `<img>` is skipped with a warning. SVG-embedded rasters reach krilla-svg's `Image::from_png` / `jpeg` / `gif` / `webp` directly, without F32a's `validate` and `MAX_IMAGE_PIXELS`. Since F99e they are decoded on rayon workers. Fix: validate the rasters of an SVG (usvg's image nodes) like top-level images before handing the tree to krilla-svg — drop or replace a bad one with a warning, strict mode refuses. Test in `image_robustness.rs`.

### F177b — usvg reads host files for the non-`data:` image hrefs of an SVG (relative to the working directory, or absolute)  (severity: medium-high — offline / determinism invariant, size: small — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-26-f177b-svg-href-offline.md`, ADR-0006)

From the F177 plan: an SVG `<image href="logo.png">` or `href="/etc/…/x.png"` that is not a `data:` URL is resolved by usvg's `default_string_resolver`, which reads the path from the host disk — relative to the process's working directory (`resources_dir` is `None`) or absolute. That breaks "never read host files, the output depends only on the input and the bundle" (AGENTS.md, goal 3) and lets a document embed any image file the process can read. Decide: resolve SVG hrefs through the `AssetBundle` like a top-level `<img>` (by name), or refuse with a warning (strict: error). F177 keeps the reading as it is but validates what it reads. The F177 vrt-validator: `<image href="/dev/zero">` is an unbounded read on main and on F177 (killed at 8-12 GB); whatever the policy, cap the size read.

### F177c — a broken raster inside an SVG in a page margin box is warned about but not refused in strict mode  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

From F177: the margin-box documents (`@top-*` / `@bottom-*` content) are converted in `render_v2`, after the engine has reported its findings, so their SVG rasters are checked and left out with a warning but `strict_images(true)` does not refuse the document. Fix: collect the margin-box findings in `render_v2` and return them (a new parameter or return value), then refuse like the body's. Since F177b an inline `<svg>` in a margin box has no bundle, so `<image href="logo.png">` is warned as "not a bundled image" even when `logo.png` is bundled (F177b vrt-validator): say "margin boxes have no bundled images; use a data: URL", or give margin documents the bundle.

### F177d — with a `base_path`, fulgur's net provider serves SVGs that Blitz parses with usvg's default (host-reading) resolver  (severity: low — unreachable from our crate, size: small — Blitz / fulgur)

From F177b: Blitz's `net.rs` calls `parse_svg(&bytes, None)` for files a `FulgurNetProvider` serves from `base_path`, so their `<image href>`s are read from the host disk by usvg's default resolver. Our crate never sets `base_path` (grep), so it is unreachable through htmltopdf; a direct fulgur user is exposed. Fix: pass the offline resolver there too.

### F177e — small F177 / F177b follow-ups  (severity: low, size: small each — fulgur)

From the F177b vrt-validator: (a) strict mode lists the SVG image problems sorted by name, not in document order as the plan says; (b) an empty or blank href gives a message with a blank name (`<svg> no. 1:  is not drawn: …`); (c) "an SVG used as an image loads no external files" gives no remedy ("embed it as a data: URL"); (d) the CLI calls `realpath` on `<img src>` and CSS `url()` strings such as `/etc/passwd` (metadata only, never opened; the same before F177) — find the caller and drop it; (e) a character that cannot be escaped in an inline `<svg>` drops the whole SVG with one Blitz warning and does not fail strict mode.

### F152b — usvg prints to stdout for a COLRv1 face used in SVG text  (severity: low — no bundled COLRv1 face, size: medium — usvg fork or a filter)

Left from F152: usvg 0.45.1 `src/text/colr.rs:188` and `:288` call `println!` when a COLRv1 glyph with a sweep gradient or an unsupported blend mode is drawn. Since F152 only bundled faces reach usvg, so it fires only when a caller bundles a COLRv1 (colour emoji) face and an SVG uses it; it writes into the CLI's `-o -` stream. Fix: leave COLR faces out of the SVG font database (then the glyph falls back), or carry a usvg fork that logs instead.

### F152c — inline SVG text does not inherit `font-family` / `font-size` from the HTML  (severity: low, size: medium — Blitz)

Chrome draws `<svg><text>` with no `font-family` in the HTML's font (Inter in fixture-like pages, F152 §7); Blitz parses the SVG's `outer_html` alone, so usvg sees no inherited style: the family is the bundle's `serif` mapping (since F152) and the size usvg's default (12 px), not the page's. Fix: pass the computed `font-family`, `font-size`, `font-weight`, `font-style` and `fill` (`color`) of the `<svg>` element as root presentation attributes or as `usvg::Options` defaults when Blitz builds the tree.

### F180 — an SVG used as `background-image` is drawn at 4/3 scale and clipped  (severity: medium — wrong output, size: small — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-27-f180-svg-background-scale.md`)

Found in F152's probes, on `main` too: a 300 x 60 SVG as the background of a 300 x 60 box draws its content 1.33 times too large (a 40 px square becomes 53 px, the right part is clipped by the box); the same SVG as `<img>` is correct. A px / pt mix-up in `convert/style/background.rs` (or where the SVG background is painted). Repro: `out/scratch/f152/shape.html` + `shape.svg` (red and blue 40 px squares at x 10 and 200). Check `list-style-image` SVGs too. Test: pixel positions of both squares within 1 px of the `<img>` copy.

### F152d — SVG text: odd weights and missing-glyph warnings  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur / usvg)

From the F152 vrt-validator: (a) `font-weight` 550 / 650 in SVG text picks Inter-Regular, where HTML picks SemiBold / Bold (usvg or our `fontdb` query rounds differently; check usvg's weight parsing and the CSS Fonts 4 §5.2 weight matching); (b) a character no bundled face has logs one usvg `No fonts with a … character were found` warning per occurrence, undeduplicated (a CJK paragraph in an SVG floods stderr) — route it through the capped warning list; (c) krilla-svg writes one `BT … Tj ET` per glyph (pdf-reviewer: ~100 B decoded per glyph; Flate packs it, size only).

### F181 — tagged PDF: SVG content is outside marked content; an inline `<svg role="img">` gets no Figure  (severity: low until PDF/UA is on the roadmap, size: medium — fulgur)

From the F152 pdf-reviewer, before F152 too but wider since SVG-as-image text is drawn: in `--tagged` output the text and paths of an SVG are outside any BDC (r-rich-t.pdf: 359 text-show operators); the `/Figure` of `<img src="x.svg">` has `/Alt` but an empty `/K` (as F20 for rasters); an inline `<svg role="img" aria-label="…">` gets no structure element; a `background-image` SVG is not an `/Artifact` — and no background is (gradients, per-tile images and the F180 tiling patterns alike are unmarked content in `--tagged` output; F180 pdf-reviewer). ISO 32000-1 §14.8.2.2 / PDF/UA-1 §7.1: content is either in the structure tree or an artifact. Fix in fulgur: wrap `<img>` / inline SVG drawing in the Figure's MCID (and give an inline `<svg>` with `role="img"` a Figure with its label as `/Alt`), wrap background drawing in `/Artifact` BMC/EMC.

### F182 — `htmltopdf render -o -` writes a file named `-`  (severity: low, size: small — ours, CLI)

From the F152 vrt-validator: the CLI has no stdout output; `-o -` creates `./-`. Either support `-` as stdout (fulgur's CLI does, with its stdout isolation) or reject it with a clear error. Also: a strict-mode error prints a stack backtrace when `RUST_BACKTRACE` is set (check that the CLI prints only the error).

### F152e — two scanner / SVG edge cases from the F152 re-review  (severity: low — contrived, size: small)

(a) Our tag scanner treats an SVG `<title>` / `<script>` / `<style>` body as raw text, html5ever closes the `<svg>` on a literal `</svg>` inside it: `<svg><title></svg></title><text style="font-family:ui-monospace">X</text></svg>` leaves the following `<text>` as HTML whose `ui-monospace` is not mapped, so its text is dropped (strict: a false "text without a font" error); main drew it. (b) Pre-existing: a `<![CDATA[ … ]]>` inside an inline `<svg>` makes Blitz fail with `SVG data parsing failed` and the SVG is not drawn.

### F178 — a bundled face that Parley accepts but krilla rejects loses its text silently, even with `--strict-fonts`  (severity: medium — silent, size: small — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-27-f178-krilla-rejected-faces.md`, ADR-0008)

From the F99e pdf-reviewer, the same on main: Inter-Bold with its `head` table renamed shapes fine in Parley, krilla's `Font::new` returns `None`, and every run in that face is skipped (exit 0 with `--strict-fonts`, the bold lines missing from the PDF). A face whose `glyf` is renamed is embedded as a non-subset Type 3 font. Fix: check each bundled face with krilla's parser when it is registered (`AssetBundle`), refuse it in strict mode and warn otherwise; count skipped runs in the dropped-text warning.

### F180b — an SVG with no natural size as `background-image` uses its `viewBox` size  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

From F180's plan: an SVG with only a `viewBox` (or a percentage `width` / `height`) has no natural size; usvg reports the `viewBox` size (or 100 x 100), so `background-size: auto` draws it at that size, where css-images-3 §5.3 contain-fits an image with only a natural aspect ratio into the background positioning area. Test against Chrome with `printBackground: true`.

### F180c — `list-style-image` markers are clamped to the line height  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

`convert/list_marker.rs` scales a marker image taller than the line down to the line height; Chrome draws it at its natural size (the line grows). From F180's plan; check with a 32 px marker on 16 px text.

### F183 — an inline `<img>` sits on the floor of its line box, with no gap for the strut's descent  (severity: low-medium, size: medium — Blitz / Parley inline layout) — **done** (`2026-10-27-f183-inline-img-strut.md`; forks `subapavel/parley` `htmltopdf/inline-box-strut` and `subapavel/blitz`; fixture 27)

Found in F180's probe (`out/scratch/f180/shape.html`): an `<img>` (inline, `vertical-align: baseline`) followed by a block: Chrome leaves about 4 px below the image (the line box holds the strut's descent under the baseline, CSS 2.1 §10.8.1), ours none — the next block starts right at the image bottom. Affects every logo or QR code written as a plain inline `<img>` in a block (fixture 03?). Compare a 60 px `<img>` in a `div` with `font: 16px Inter` against Chrome; `display:block` or `vertical-align: top` images are unaffected.

### F183b — an `inline-block` sits on its last line's baseline without the strut's descent below it  (severity: low, size: medium — Blitz / Parley)

Non-goal of F183 (plan §6, test G3): an empty 48 x 48 `inline-block` followed by a block starts the block at 48 px; Chrome at 52 (the
box's bottom margin edge sits on the baseline, CSS 2.1 §10.8.1, and the line box holds the strut's descent). F183 flags only
`<img>` / `<svg>`; extend the flag to inline-blocks once their baseline is taken from their last line box.

### F183c — inline boxes with `vertical-align` other than `baseline` get no strut  (severity: low, size: medium — Parley)

Non-goal of F183: `middle`, `top`, `bottom`, `text-top`, lengths and percentages on an inline `<img>` are still placed as Parley 0.6
places every inline box (bottom on the baseline). Parley 0.12 (`vertical_align`) implements them; a bump is the likely fix.

### F183d — the strut is not applied to text-only lines whose spans are all smaller than the root font  (severity: low, size: small — Parley)
