Non-goal of F183: a line holding only a `<span style="font-size:8px">` in a 16 px block is as tall as the span's line height in ours;
Chrome keeps the root's strut (CSS 2.1 §10.8.1). Rare on invoices (small print sits in its own block).

### F183e — no quirks mode: a document without a doctype renders as in standards mode  (severity: low, size: large — Blitz)

From F183 step 0 (plan §7.1): without a doctype Chrome drops the strut gap under a lone inline image (the "line height quirk"), so
ours is 4 px off on such documents. Every fixture has a doctype; documents without one are also F91 (NoQuirks class matching).
Decide whether to emulate quirks mode at all before planning.

### F183f — image lines with a fractional `line-height` are up to 2 px taller than Chrome's  (severity: low, size: small — Parley fork)

From the F183 vrt-validator (`out/scratch/f183v/c_long_para_imgs.html`): a 14 px / 1.4 paragraph with 12 image lines puts the next
block at 734 px, Chrome at 713 (main 660, without the strut). The strut path rounds the leading split like a text line
(`floor(leading / 2)` above, the rest below), Chrome keeps the fractional half-leading in LayoutUnits. An integer `line-height`
matches Chrome (787 / 787, `c_long_para_imgs_lh_px`).

### F183g — an image line taller than the page is not drawn  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur pagination)

From the F183 vrt-validator (`c_fs_1000`, `c_lh_huge`): a 10 x 10 `<img>` on a line with `font-size: 1000px` or `line-height:
100000px` is drawn on no page (main drew it, Chrome splits the line box across pages). Unrealistic on invoices.

### F183h — a line with an image after a 1059 px spacer moves to page 2; Chrome keeps it on page 1  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur pagination)

From the F183 vrt-validator (`c_pg_1059`): 1059 + 64 = 1123 px against Chrome's 1122.52 px page; Chrome lets a line overflow
the page by a fraction of a pixel, ours does not. Spacers 1060-1064 match Chrome.

### F184 — `html` / `body` backgrounds are not propagated to the canvas  (severity: medium, size: medium — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-27-f184-canvas-background.md`)

From the F180 vrt-validator (pre-existing, unchanged by F180; repro harness `scratchpad/f180v/cases/07b-html.html`): a `body` background image is positioned against the body box (20, 30) instead of the canvas origin; `html { background: url(...) repeat }` fills only the html content height (~340 of 1123 px), not the page; `background-position: 50% 50%` on body is centred per page fragment, not over the canvas. CSS Backgrounds 3 §2.11.2: the root's background (or body's, when the root has none) is propagated to the canvas, painted over the whole page, positioned against the root element's box. Matters for invoices with a watermark / letterhead background on `body`.

### F184b — `100%` canvas background positions sit up to 1 px left  (severity: low, size: small — Blitz viewport)

From F184's plan: Blitz lays out at a whole-px viewport (793 px for A4's 793.7), so a canvas background positioned at `100%` (or centred) is up to 1 px left of Chrome's. Fix with a fractional viewport width if Blitz allows it.

### F184c — canvas background `%` positions differ when the document has forced breaks or avoided-break gaps  (severity: low, size: medium — fulgur)

From F184's plan: Chrome's stitched root box includes the space left by `break-before: page` and `break-inside: avoid` pushes, so it is taller than our layout height; a canvas background positioned in `%` (or sized `cover` / `contain`) of the root box lands differently. Measure the root height as the sum of the page fragments it occupies. The F184 vrt-validator showed the visible cost: a `linear-gradient` body background in a document with `break-inside: avoid` blocks restarts with a hard colour seam on the last page (repro `scratchpad/f184v/ed/`); keep-together tables and totals make this common — raise to medium if a gradient letterhead shows up.

### F185 — background edge cases the engine drops silently  (severity: low, size: small each — fulgur)

From the F180 vrt-validator, all pre-existing: (a) an SVG background with an explicit `background-size` (`50% 80%`, `100% 100%`, `150px 30px`) is stretched non-uniformly; Chrome honours the SVG's default `preserveAspectRatio` (xMidYMid meet) and letterboxes (like F32d for `<img>`); (b) `background-attachment: local` draws nothing, `fixed` scrolls with the box instead of being anchored to the page; (c) a WebP background is dropped with no warning (`<img>` warns); `url(data:…)` PNG backgrounds draw nothing either (F25 covers SVG `data:`); (d) an SVG with `width="5em"` uses usvg's 12 px font size (60 px where Chrome draws 80 px; F32g); (e) F180b's viewBox-only SVG is now 80 x 40 (main drew 106 x 53 by the 4/3 accident; Chrome 240 x 120). (f) the tile phase restarts at the top of each page fragment (css-break-3 §5.4 `box-decoration-break: slice` carries it over); (g) `round` with a non-zero `background-position` is ~2 px off Chrome. Repros: `scratchpad/f180v/cases/02-size.html`, `18-webp.html`, `19-datauri.html`, `10-svgsizes.html`.

### F37b — `<html>`'s own border and shadow are painted at its layout size on every page  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

From F37's plan: since F184 html's background is the canvas, but a border or shadow on `<html>` is still painted by the per-page pre-pass at html's layout size on every page (a closed frame per page), not as the slices of its box like body's since F37. Reuse `box_slices` for the root.

### F37c — a body taller than its content (`min-height: 100vh` with a margin, `height: 5000px`) is clamped at the last content page  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

From F37: body's slices end at the last page that holds content; a body whose own height reaches further (a fixed or `min-height` taller than its content) is cut there, where Chrome continues the box and may add a page (the validator saw `body { height: 100vh }` give Chrome 2 pages, us 1).

### F37d — a trailing `<br>` makes an extra empty line box at the end of an inline-root body  (severity: low, size: small — Parley / Blitz inline layout)

Found by F37's K7: `Line1<br>…<br>Line200<br>` directly in body ends 20 px lower than Chrome (700 vs 680 px on the last page), because our layout gives the final `<br>` its own empty line box; Chrome does not (a `<br>` at the end of a block creates no new line, CSS 2.1 §9.4.2 / HTML rendering). Shows as a taller card or an extra blank line before a footer.

F183 makes `<img><br>` alone 8 px worse (block top 120 on main, 128 with the strut, 64 in Chrome; `out/scratch/f183v/c_br_after.html`): the extra line after the `<br>` now has the strut too. Fixing F37d removes both.

### F186 — per-side border colours are lost: the bottom edge takes the top edge's colour  (severity: HIGH — visible on ordinary invoices, size: small — fulgur or Blitz style conversion) — **done** (`2026-10-27-f186-border-side-colours.md`)

Found by the F37 vrt-validator, pre-existing (F184 and earlier identical), confirmed: `<div style="border-bottom: 4px solid #c00">` draws a black bottom border (the top colour, `currentColor`); `border-color: #c00 #0a0 #00c #fa0` draws the bottom edge red (the top colour) instead of blue. Hand-written invoice CSS such as `td { border-bottom: 1px solid #e5e7eb }` therefore prints dark lines; Tailwind fixtures escape it because `border-color` sets all four sides. Repro: `scratchpad/f37v/t/d.html` (`out/scratch/f37b/d.html`). Check every side (left / right too) and the radius path; find where fulgur reads the border colours (`BlockStyle.border_colors` construction in `convert/style`, or Blitz / Stylo's computed `border-*-color` mapping).

### F187 — an absolutely positioned child of a body with a margin is placed by the body margin too far left  (severity: medium, size: small — fulgur / Blitz) — **done** (F187: `2026-10-27-f187-abspos-body-margin.md`)

From the F37 vrt-validator, pre-existing: `body { margin: 30px } div { position: absolute; left: 50px }` lands at x 20..119 (Chrome 50..149). Repro `scratchpad/f37v/cases/abs-m0.html`.

### F186b — mixed border colours with a radius and a dashed / dotted / double side are drawn with square corners  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

From the F186 vrt-validator: the rounded mixed-colour painter (`draw_border_ring`) needs every drawn side solid; otherwise each side is a straight line in its own colour and the radius is lost (`border: 4px dotted; border-color: #c00 #0a0 #00c #fa0; border-radius: 12px`: 1108 → 1519 px from Chrome; a mixed `double` ring is now square). Clip the per-side dashed strokes to the rounded border ring as the solid wedges are. Also from the review: mixed-colour borders cost more PDF bytes (8000 cells with four colours 161 → 341 KB; cull or merge per-side paths if it matters).

### F187b — `margin-left` / `margin-top` of an absolutely positioned box placed by `left` / `top` against the page area is dropped  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

From F187's plan, confirmed in Chrome (§7.1): `left: 50px; margin-left: 10px` on a body-direct abspos box is at x 60 in Chrome (117 at 15 mm); `resolve_viewport_cb_location` drops the start-side margin while the F30g `abspos_x` path adds it. The fix is shared with the fixed and nested passes; check fulgur-wpt `css-position` / `css-page` before and after.

### F188 — an absolutely positioned box in a static wrapper that falls on page 2 is placed at the wrapper, not against the page area  (severity: medium, size: medium — fulgur)

From the F187 vrt-validator, pre-existing (main identical): 60 rows, then `<div><div style="position:absolute; top:20px; left:20px; width:100px; height:100px">`, `@page { margin: 0 }`, `body { margin: 30px }`: Chrome draws the box on page 1 at (20, 20) (its containing block is the ICB), we draw it on page 2 at about the wrapper's position (54, 469). Same inside a `<td>` and a wrapper with a margin. F187 fixed body-direct boxes; nested boxes whose nearest positioned ancestor is the ICB need the same rule (`abspos_x` / `locate_icb` path, CSS 2.1 §10.1). Repros `out/scratch/v187/html/F3-m0.html`, `E2`, `F4`.

### F189 — a static-position (auto inset) or bottom-anchored abspos box on page 2+ sits by the page-break slack too high  (severity: low-medium, size: small — fulgur)

From the F187 vrt-validator, pre-existing: 60 rows of 25 px, then an abspos box with only `left: 50px`: Chrome y 425..525 on page 2, ours 411..511 (main 351). The static position comes from the stitched Taffy y, but a page break leaves slack (1089 is not a multiple of 25), which Chrome's fragmented position includes. Same for `body { position: relative }` over 3 pages with `bottom: 20px` (27-31 px high). Repros `out/scratch/v187/html/E5-m0.html`, `bodyrel3-m0.html`.

### F190 — a flex row with `justify-content: space-between` wrapped its second span in one F187 validator case  (severity: low — needs a repro, size: unknown)

Seen in passing by the F187 vrt-validator (main identical): a `height: 24px` row `display: flex; justify-content: space-between` with two `<span>`s rendered the second span under the first. Checked on 2026-10-27: the plain pattern in a block body renders correctly, with and without a fixed height (`out/scratch/f190/a.html`), so the validator's rows were probably inside a shrink-to-fit abspos box (F94: an abspos box sized against its parent / too narrow) and simply had no room. Re-check with the validator's `out/scratch/v187/gen*.py` rows; close if it is F94.

### F191 — an abspos box taller than its pages, or straddling a page break, is neither paginated nor clipped  (severity: low, size: medium — fulgur)

From the F187 vrt-validator, pre-existing: a box `height: 2500px` with short content gives 1 page (Chrome 3); a box at `top: 3000px` straddling the page 3/4 break is drawn whole on both pages and spills into the `@page` margin (Chrome clips it to each page area); same for a tall fixed box. Repros `tall-m0.html`, `top3000b-m15.html`, `E16-fixedtall`.

### F192 — the last word of a narrow cell in an auto-layout table is not printed: `w026` and `w052` of a three-column table (`out/scratch/f183v6/s/tall_col_none_1040.html`)  (severity: low-medium — silent, size: unknown — Blitz / Taffy or fulgur)

From the F183 vrt-validator (fourth pass), equal on main (`htmltopdf-f99f`): `table { border-collapse: collapse } td { padding: 6px; border: 1px solid #000 }`, `width: 100%`, three cells per row (`w001..w020`, `w021 w022 w023`, `w024 w025 w026`) after a 1040 px filler. The first column takes more than Chrome's share, the third is about 66 px wide and its second line (`w026`, `w052`, the third word of the cell) is not painted: the first line ends at x = 595.4 pt, the A4 page edge (595.28 pt) (`pdftotext -bbox`). Chrome has 14 words on the first line of the first column and every word of the third. The columns are F129's territory (free width divided equally, not by content); the lost word is a cell content that is wider than its cell and is cut at the edge of the page, as F155 / F166. Needs a minimal repro without the filler (is the page break involved?) and a look at where the cell's width comes from. Acceptance: all 52 words of the first two rows are in the PDF. The same family is the `tall_*` documents of sets `a` / `a2` (`out/scratch/f183v6`: `tall_col_tr_700` and 237 more, identical counts on main, the F183 branch and the fix of plan 7.7): 1010 words, 2 to 6 of them lost per document (`w026`, `w052`, `w958`, `w1009`, `w1010`) and one word broken in two (`w1009` appears as `w100` + `9`, the validator's "word cut at the edge of a fixed-width cell").

### F193 — a table that is a direct child of a `columns: 2` box, started near the bottom of a page, with `tr { break-inside: avoid }`, loses 7-28 of 504 words (`out/scratch/f183v6/s/c_mc_col_1030.html`, `c_mc2_*`)  (severity: medium — silent, size: medium — fulgur, F30d / F174)

From the F183 vrt-validator, the same on main and on the F183 branch: a 1030 px filler, `<div style="columns:2;column-gap:20px"><table style="width:100%">...20 rows of 25 + 3 words...</table></div>`, `tr { break-inside: avoid }`, `border-collapse: collapse` or separate: 497 of 504 words at filler 1030, 487 at 1070, 483 at 1095 (`c_mc2_*`, the 2-cells-per-row variant: up to 28 lost). F174 prints all 269 words of its 130-row shape, so this is another path: the container starts in the last 100 px of page 1 (F174d territory: the container is moved to the next page when its balanced height would fit the room left) and the rows with `avoid`. Acceptance: all 504 words, as Chrome; the sweep is `names_c.json` / `names_c2.json` in `out/scratch/f183v6` (`c_mc*`, `C2mc*`: 40 cases).

### F194 — `table-layout: fixed` with `width: 100%` and no column widths: the columns are not equal thirds, so rows are shorter and the page count differs from Chrome (3 pages, Chrome 4: `out/scratch/f183v6/s/g_ht_none_col_800.html` and 60 siblings)  (severity: medium — every page break below such a table is off, size: medium — Blitz / Taffy)

From the F183 vrt-validator: `table { table-layout: fixed; border-collapse: collapse } td { padding: 6px }`, `width: 100%`, a `thead` and 12 rows of `img` + 30 words in the first cell and 3 words in the other two. Chrome lays out three equal columns (5 words per line in the first cell, `pdftotext -layout`), we give the first column about twice the width (10 words per line), so the table is shorter: 3 pages against Chrome's 4, 72 / 288 / 72 words per page against 47 / 169 / 191 / 25. Same on main. CSS 2.1 section 17.5.2.1: with `table-layout: fixed` a column without `width` takes an equal share of the remaining width; `table-layout: fixed` also "still takes widths from later rows" (F15). Related to F129 / F130 (auto layout). Acceptance: the first cell wraps at one third of the table width, `g_ht_none_col_800` has 4 pages and Chrome's words per page.

### F195 — a table cell holding a grid with a flex inside loses words of its neighbouring cell when the row is cut  (severity: medium, size: medium — fulgur)

From the F183 fifth vrt-validator pass (fuzz): `out/scratch/f183v7/s/min/repro_grid_cell_loss.html` loses w355 and w356 at spacer 300
with F183 (main is complete there, but loses them at spacer 15; `z9_5_123`, `z9_5_747` are the same shape). The row cut enters the
grid cell (F83d's flex / grid half); F183's strut only moves which spacer hits it. Over the 800-document fuzz set F183 loses words in
45 documents, main in 99.

### F196 — invoice items with an inline image in every row on A6: a page count off Chrome's in 24 of 34 positions  (severity: medium, size: medium — Blitz table column widths)

From the F183 fifth vrt-validator pass: `out/scratch/f183v7/s/s9_inv_items_A6_299.html` (105 x 148 mm, 25 rows of an inline image plus
12 words, `thead`). Chrome's auto-layout column is wider, so the cell has 2 lines; ours wraps one word earlier into a 3rd line (row pitch
Chrome 69.0 pt, main 78.0, F183 80.3). Main matched Chrome's page count by luck; the strut's 3 px per row removes the luck. A4, A4 with
margins and landscape equal Chrome (46/46, 39/39, 32/32 with F183). Fix the column width (auto table layout, cf. F129).

### F197 — two inline-image lines that end exactly at the page bottom move their paragraph whole; Chrome splits it  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

From the F183 fifth vrt-validator pass: `out/scratch/f183v7/s/s7d_p_img_A4_995.html` and `s7d_li_A4_1039.html`: words per page Chrome and
main 31|201, F183 0|232. The inline-root cut (plan §7.7) treats the exact fit as overflowing; also `s7_p_ib_A4m_376` / `_546` one line
off. The later-row cut path is about 24 px stricter than Chrome's.

### F198 — a padded or bordered `div` inside a `column-count` container loses most of its words  (severity: HIGH — silent, size: medium — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-29-f198-multicol-padded-loss.md`)

Found by the vrt-validator pass on F30f-d; main prints the same bytes. `<div style="column-count:2;column-gap:20px"><div style="padding:16px;border:1px solid #888;border-radius:8px;background:#f5f5f5">` holding 2500 words, `<p>AFTER</p>` (`out/scratch/f30fdv/a/multicol.html`, `c1.py`): Chrome prints 4 pages (690 / 716 / 719 / 375 words); main and ours print 2 pages with 361 / 0 words (2139 lost), the first line at 54.8 pt and its last at 836 pt, below the content box. The same card inside a padded card (`multicol_in_card`) is worth checking. Not F30f-d (the same bytes on main; F30f-d's `inset_split_here` excludes multicol containers on purpose). Cause not traced: probably the column fragmenter has no line splitting for a block with insets (F174 / F193 family). Fix: plan first; start from the column fragmenter's treatment of a paragraph with insets. Queue: right after F30f-h.


**Done 2026-10-29** (plan §7): the cause was the one named in the plan: every column child that is not a `<p>` of glyph runs is one `FlowItem::Block`, a box taller than a page was placed whole in one column ("overflows the page" warning, `F30d-b`). The fork cuts such a box between its lines, column after column and page after page, with its frame sliced at the breaks (`mc_box`, `McFrame`, `McPiece::Frame`, `piece_edges`; fork `4668e60f`). Shapes A-E of the plan (card of text, plain `<div>`, card of `<p>`, three columns, `column-fill: auto`) print every word, 4 / 4 / 4 / 5 / 5 pages like Chrome (main: 2 pages, 84-92 % of the words lost); shape F (a padded `<p>`, which main printed whole but 13 pt too high and 17 px too far left, outside its frame) has its padding. Fixture 30. Left, filed in the notes below: **F174c** (a table taller than a page inside the card keeps the warning and the old path: the whole card stays one box), **F173(f)** (the spanner gate does not look at `boxes`), **F173(h)** (a box shorter than a page under a padded container: the fill rule, not this path), **F193** (separate: row bands, byte-identical in the sweep). Not done on purpose: a box that fits a page is still moved whole to the next column (Chrome cuts it at the column end; F30d-b); the margin of a top-level column child is ignored (F30d-b); `orphans` / `widows` (column ends can differ by a line); `div` text has no tag in tagged PDFs, multicol or not (F200).
### F198b — a tall box in a multi-column container still loses words when it holds an image, float, abspos child, inline-block, table, or is clipped  (severity: HIGH — warned, size: medium-large — fulgur) — **done for the clip card and for lines with inline boxes** (`2026-10-29-f198b-multicol-box-contents.md`); the rest is F198e / F198f / F198g / F198h, F173(f), F174c

From the F198 vrt-validator (`out/scratch/f198v/`, `out/scratch/f198pdf/`): these shapes kept F198's old path (the box stays one `Block` and is placed whole, with the "taller than a page" warning). Words found / total (Chrome prints all): `overflow:hidden` card 361/2500; inline `<img>` in the text 360/2100; a `<p>` with an inline image taller than a page 248/1200 (185 in a card); inline-block 337/1920; inline `<svg>` in the text of the card 358/2500; abspos child 361/2500; float child 344/2500; `column-span:all` 361/2500 and 733/2400; a table with rows in the card 311/771; also `height`, opacity, transform, % padding, an image taller than a page, nesting deeper than 16, multicol nested in the card.

**Done (F198b):** an auto-height clip card (`overflow-hidden rounded-lg border`, Tailwind's card idiom) is cut between its lines and each piece clips to its own padding box (`mc_box_wrapped`, `draw_under_clip` per `Frame` fragment, `Canvas::slice_window`); a paragraph whose lines hold inline boxes (image, `<svg>`, inline-block, inline-flex) is cut between its lines with its boxes (`McBoxItem::Lines::inline_boxes`, `record_line_inline_boxes`, `multicol_source_lines`, boxes painted with the slices). Chrome-numbers tests H-K in `multicol_boxes.rs`, fixture 31. Also: a child that Blitz placed 1 px above the bottom of the one before it (rounding) no longer makes the box undescribable.

### F198c — `column-rule` is not drawn beside a tall box that takes the F198 box path  (severity: medium, size: small — fulgur)

From the F198 vrt-validator: `out/scratch/f198v/rule.html`, `rule_tall1.html` (A4, 2px red rule): red pixels per page main 0/0 (main lost the text there), branch 0/0/0/0, Chrome 526/527/527/386; a container of `<p>` draws it on both (525 vs 526). The rule is painted from the column plan of `Lines`/`Block` atoms; the new `Frame` pieces are not counted.

### F198d — the horizontal geometry of a tall box in columns is ignored: margins, `width`, `max-width` (no loss)  (severity: low-medium, size: small-medium — fulgur)

From the F198 vrt-validator: `out/scratch/f198v/margins.html` (`margin:25px 12px`) gives 4 pages and 333 words per column vs Chrome 5 pages and 286; `w70c`, `mleft` render at full column width with Chrome's wrapping lost; `narrow.html` (`max-width:120px;margin:0 auto`) draws a 120px frame around 330px-wide text, so the border runs through the words (main lost the words instead). `mc_box` re-shapes the text at the column width instead of the box's content width. Also from that pass (LOW, no loss): list items and paragraphs with an image move whole inside the box; `break-before: column` inside the box is ignored; `break-inside: avoid` on a tall box; anonymous text between `<p>`s moves whole.

### F198e — an absolutely positioned child (a PAID badge) in a tall box in columns keeps the old path  (severity: medium — warned, size: medium — fulgur)

From F198b step 1 (`out/scratch/f198v/abspos.html`, `out/scratch/f198b/shapes/abspos.html`): `position:relative` card of 2500 words with `<span style="position:absolute;right:0;top:0">STAMP</span>`: main prints 361 words on 2 pages with the warning (`mc_box_at` refuses: float / abspos child), Chrome 2500 words on 4 pages. A badge usually sits in the header rather than in a card taller than a page. Fix: a placement rule measured in Chrome (css-position-3 §3: the containing block is the card, which is fragmented; Chrome puts the box in the first fragment, to be checked for `bottom`/`right` offsets) and a `Leaf`-like piece at that place. Pinned today by `guard_absolutely_positioned_and_floating_children_keep_the_old_path`.

### F198f — a float in a tall box in columns keeps the old path  (severity: low-medium — warned, size: medium — fulgur)

From F198b step 1 (`float.html`): a `float:left` 60 x 60 box in the card: main 344 of 2500 words, Chrome 2500 on 4 pages. Rare in terms text. The text lines beside a float are shorter, which the cut would have to follow (the Parley layout has it); the float itself is a leaf in the first piece. Same guard test as F198e.

### F198g — shapes of a tall box in columns that stay whole: a constrained clip box or `height`, opacity, transform, % padding, a line or image taller than a page, nesting deeper than 16, multicol in the card, a clip ancestor outside the container  (severity: low-medium — warned, size: per shape — fulgur)

F198b step 1 numbers (main words / Chrome words, all warned): `height:2000px` card 361 / 2500; `height:2000px;overflow:hidden` 361 / 696 on 2 pages (the box hides the rest, ADR-0009: needs the clip band in the pieces); opacity 361 / 2500; transform 361 / 2500; `padding:5%` 378 / 2500 (blocked on F30f-g); an image 1500 px tall 300 / 600; 20 nested divs 240 / 2000 (depth 16); `overflow:hidden` around the multicol container 361 / 2500 (the wrapper paints its descendants through one clip scope, a card piece cannot pass through it); a line taller than a page (an inline box taller than a page, ADR-0004's shape) keeps the warning. Each is a refusal in `mc_box_at` named by the F198b gate dump; the guard test pins the constrained clip box.

### F198h — a line that holds an inline box is 17 px tall in Parley and 16.8 in Chrome; the box of an inline-block with padding sticks out below its line  (severity: low-medium, size: small-medium — Parley fork / fulgur)

From F198b: the Parley line box of a line with an inline `<svg>` of 12 px is `line_height` 17.0 against 16.8 for the line beside it (`LineMetrics`, 50 of 427 lines of `out/scratch/f198b/shapes/svgdiv.html`), and Chrome's is 16.8. With 59 or 60 lines of 16.8 px filling a page to within 1 or 2 px, every column of such a card holds a line less than Chrome's, and the columns drift: the first word of the last column is 37 words (5 lines) late and `AFTER` is 51 pt low (`multicol_boxes.rs` tolerates it for the shapes with the most icons, `ICON_TOL`). The layout is the same unbroken (a paragraph of such lines is 10 px longer than Chrome's per 50 icons). Also, an `inline-block` with border and padding counts all of its height above the baseline in Parley, so it protrudes 3 px below its line and the next line's glyphs cross its bottom border (`ibplain` in `out/scratch/f198b/shapes/`: the same unbroken, main and ours; Chrome makes the line 23 px tall). F183's strut fix is the family. Fix in the Parley fork's line metrics (`htmltopdf/inline-box-strut`), which moves every document with inline boxes: its own PR with the goldens.

### F198i — a tall clip card in columns renders in quadratic time  (severity: medium — speed goal, size: small — fulgur)

From the F198b vrt-validator (`out/scratch/f198bv/sc_clip_1600.html` vs `sc_plain_1600.html`, the same text without `overflow:hidden`, best of 3): 19 pages 107 / 98 ms, 83 pages 490 / 340, 167 pages 1519 / 672, 364 pages 8842 / 1717 ms. A clip card over about 160 pages misses the 200-pages-under-1-s goal. Suspect: `draw_under_clip` walks `block.clip_descendants` for every piece on every page; index the descendants by page (or by piece) once. Not a regression against main (main lost the text of this shape).

### F198j — more shapes of a tall box in columns that are still placed whole and lose words  (severity: medium-high — warned, size: medium — fulgur)

From the F198b vrt-validator (bytes identical to main, warned): a child with `position:relative; top:3px` (`out/scratch/f198bv/r_rel` 330 / 2100, `x_relshift` 354 / 2500; Chrome prints all; `position:relative` without an offset works); a child with a negative margin (`x_negmargin` 360 / 2500; the lead check refuses it); a line taller than the page (`im1200_*` 298 / 1810; edge1006 works, 1008-1012 refused). Extends F198g.

### F198k — tagged output: a link that wraps an `<img>` has no structure; inline-block and figure elements come after their paragraph's text  (severity: medium for PDF/UA, size: small-medium — fulgur)

From the F198b pdf-reviewer (same on main): a link around an `<img>` gets an annotation with no `/StructParent`, no `/Link` element and no OBJR (ISO 32000-1 §14.8.4.4.2, PDF/UA-1 §7.18.1; `out/scratch/f198bpdf/pimg.html`, `c2-bt`: 32 annotations). The `/Span` of an inline-block badge and the `/Figure` of an inline image follow the `/P`'s own content in the structure tree, so the logical order differs from the visual one (10 inversions in `c2`); a slice tagged as a whole puts the badge text in the `/P` and leaves its `/Span` empty. Also LOW (untagged output only, new with F198b): inside a clip piece a nested clip box's text is written before the outer card's slices above it (`nest.html`: w0912 before w0604 in `pdftotext -raw`). Fix with F200 / F164 (tagging).

### F199 — `outline` is not painted  (severity: low-medium, size: small — fulgur)

Found by the vrt-validator pass on F30f-d; main prints the same. `outline:3px solid red;outline-offset:2px` on a card paints nothing (`out/scratch/f30fdv/a/outline.html`, crop `crop_outline.png`); Chrome draws it round the border box, on every fragment of a split box (`outline` is not sliced). An invoice uses it rarely (focus rings do not exist in print). Fix: paint after the box's content, outside the clip, as four strokes or a path of the border box expanded by `outline-offset`; skip `outline-style: auto`.

### F200 — tagged output: text directly in a `<div>`, and lines on pages without a link or figure, get no marked content  (severity: medium for PDF/UA, low otherwise, size: small-medium — fulgur)

Found by the vrt-validator pass and by `pdf-reviewer` on F30f-d (fixture 29's card text is untagged); pre-existing, main prints the same. With `--tagged` the structure tree only has the elements that contain a link or a figure; a paragraph that is the direct text of a `<div>` is neither tagged nor marked as an artifact, and pages whose lines have neither get no marked content at all (`out/scratch/f30fdpdf/fx/*.tag.pdf`, `out/scratch/f30fdv/a/links.html`). PDF 1.7 section 14.8: all real content must be tagged or an artifact. Same family as F123 (`<body>` text), F97, F164; fix together: classify an anonymous inline root as `P` and mark the rest as an artifact.

### F201 — an `<img>` data URI without `width` / `height` does not set the line height  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur / Blitz)

Found by the vrt-validator pass on F30f-d; main prints the same. `<img src="data:...">` with no dimension attribute inside a paragraph keeps the line at the text's height instead of the image's natural height (`out/scratch/f30fdv/a/img_nosize.html`); with attributes it is correct (F9 `seed_image_natural_sizes` seeds bundled images, a data URI is not in the bundle). Fix: seed the natural size of a data-URI image the way bundled ones are.

### F178b — a font collection with one bad face is left out whole  (severity: low, size: medium — fulgur)

Since F178 a TTC / OTC file in which any face fails the krilla check is left out with all its faces (fontique registers a blob with all faces; `unregister_font` matches by attributes and could remove a good face of another file). Chrome's OTS rejects the whole file too. Fix if a real collection needs it: register only the good faces (fontique `register_fonts` per face index, or a rebuilt single-face blob).

### F178c — fonts both Parley and krilla accept but that draw wrongly  (severity: low, size: medium — fulgur)

F178 checks what krilla's `Font::new` checks plus "has outlines". A broken `loca`, a `cmap` pointing at empty glyphs or all-empty outlines still pass and draw nothing (or garbage) silently. An OTS-like sanitiser (glyph count vs `maxp`, `loca` monotonic, sample glyphs for the document's characters have outlines) would catch them; only worth it if a real font shows it.

### F178d — a font corrupt outside the sampled glyphs still fails the whole render, and the error does not name it  (severity: low — loud, size: small — fulgur)

F178's trial subset covers `.notdef` and the first 16 mapped glyphs; 13 of 400 header-fuzz mutants of Inter-Bold corrupt `loca` / `OS/2` / `post` / `hhea` data that only glyphs outside the sample need, so the real subset fails at write time: exit 1, `PDF generation error: Font(Font {..}, "failed to subset font: malformed font")`, in non-strict mode too and without naming the file. Fix: name the font (PostScript name / bundle source) in fulgur's mapping of `krilla::Error::Font`, and, if a real font ever shows it, retry the document without that face (or subset every glyph in the check, measured).

### F179 — every render starts rayon's global pool, which costs a one-page document CPU on many-core hosts  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Since F99e (krilla's `rayon` feature) the global pool is built on every render, even for a one-page invoice (main started it only for 256 or more inline layouts, F99a). Measured by the F99e vrt-validator with `RAYON_NUM_THREADS` on `01-basic` (21 interleaved pairs, 4-vCPU host): wall and CPU unchanged or better up to 16 threads, +0.7 ms wall / +9 ms CPU at 64, +22 ms / +98 ms at 256. A server with 64+ cores rendering many small invoices pays it per process. Options: build the pool with at most `min(cores, 8)` threads, or keep the deflate on the main thread for documents under N pages. Not urgent on the dev VM.

### F175 — a long `inline-table` loses its first rows and the text after it  (severity: medium — silent, size: unknown — fulgur)

Found by the F163c vrt-validator (`z_inline_plain.html`: `<table style="display:inline-table">` with 130 body rows and a trailing `tail` word): 197 words on main and on the branch, the first 33 rows and `tail` missing. With table pseudo-elements the rows are present but `tail` is still missing. An inline-level table taller than a page is one atomic inline in a line box; the line is not cut. Related to F120 / F172.

### F176 — a `display: table` box of divs does not repeat its `table-header-group` on continuation pages  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

F12 repeats the header of a `<table>` only (`repeating_table_header` checks the tag); a `display: table` div with a `display: table-header-group` child prints its header on page 1 only (the F163c validator: `Head1` on 1 of 3 pages; Chrome repeats it). Lift the tag check to the box's display, with a test like `table_header_repeat.rs`.

### F145 — a long table inside a transform / clip / opacity wrapper is painted with scans of all its cells on every page  (severity: low, size: medium — fulgur)

F99c plan A3, not done (it needs the page's node list threaded through `dispatch_page_node` and `paint_wrapper_tables`, more than the ten lines the plan allowed): `table_borders::paint_tables` filters every cell of the table with a geometry lookup on every page, and the wrapper itself
dispatches all of its `descendants` per page by design, so a 4800-row table in an `overflow: hidden` card is pages x descendants. Not in the corpus; measure with a wrapper variant of `--scaling` before fixing.

### F30f — an `inline-block` / `overflow: hidden` wrapper around a long paragraph loses its tail  (severity: HIGH — silent, size: medium — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-18-f30f-inline-block-overflow-wrapper.md` §7)

Named in the F30 plan (§6), measured: 4000 words in `display:inline-block;width:100%` (`ibl_normal.html`, `ibl_1.3.html`) or `overflow:hidden`
(`ovfhid_normal.html`) printed 386 (1 page; 3614 lost) with the warning "a paragraph of 569 lines is placed whole and ends 9917px below the content box". Plan: `2026-10-18-f30f-inline-block-overflow-wrapper.md`.

### F30f-b — what F30f left whole: inline-blocks that share their line, inline-flex / inline-grid, clip boxes that hide content  (severity: medium, size: medium — fulgur) — **clip part done** (`2026-10-28-f30f-b-clip-boxes.md` §7, ADR-0009; an auto-height clip box was wrongly cut in the first version, fixed in §7 "Auto-height clip boxes"); the other bullets stay open

Filed from the F30f step 1 probes (Chrome m141, A4, 15 mm, `/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f30f/p/`, 4000 words, 20 px lines; main = the fork before F30f, same numbers after):
- **An `inline-block` that shares its line** (`<p>Úvod <span style="display:inline-block;width:60%">W</span> konec</p>`): Chrome slices it (20 pages, 4040 words printed, the line at each break twice); ours prints 212 words on 1 page and warns ("a paragraph of N lines in an inline-block that shares its line is placed whole …").
  Needs the carrier to be split between lines around the box (line boxes of the carrier that hold text *and* a tall box), a different model than F30f's alone-on-its-line case. Also `inline-flex`: Chrome 12 pages, ours 8 words (`display:inline-flex > p`).
- ~~**A clip box shorter than its paragraph taller than a page**~~ done in the clip part (a `height` / `max-height` clip box is fragmented by its own block size; the text layer holds only what shows) (`overflow:hidden; height:600px`: Chrome 1 page, 211 words, clipped at the box; ours 1 page and the visible part is the same, but the PDF text layer holds 372 words, the clipped ones included). With `max-height:2000px` Chrome fragments the box and clips it at 2000 px (2 pages, 701 words); ours prints page 1 only (372). Fix: fragment the box with the clip at its block size, like a fixed-height table cell (F48).
- ~~**Ours reveals clipped text**~~ done in the clip part (pre-existing, body-level line path, P10): `<div style="overflow:hidden;height:300px">W</div>` directly in the body prints all 4000 words on 12 pages (11 pages with `normal`), Chrome 105 words on 1 page. The body-level line path of `fragment_pagination_root` has no clip exclusion. The same with a `display:flex` clip box of `height:300px` prints the 372 words of page 1 in the text layer (hidden by the clip, but present).
- **An inline-block that is not first on its page** (A3, `<h1>` then the box): Chrome moves the box whole to the next page and slices it from its top (13 pages); ours starts it below the heading (12 pages), ADR-0004.
- **Limits unchanged from main** (F30f review, validator LOW 7; none of them gets worse): a clip box with a fixed `height` / `max-height` taller than a page, `overflow:hidden` on a `td` or a grid item, two sibling `inline-block`s on one line, an `inline-block` in an abspos box or one that shares its line (the warning names the last).
- A table (`overflow-hidden` wrapper) of 60 rows has 135 / 45 cells per page in ours, 126 / 54 in Chrome (rows are 16.5 px here, 18 px there): not F30f, no finding yet.

### F30f-c — a clip box's text beyond its inline edge, and the page capacity Chrome uses  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Found by the F30f-b (clip part) probes (`out/scratch/f30f-b/`, Chrome m141, A4, 15 mm):
- **Inline-axis culling and `text-overflow`:** a line wider than an `overflow-x` clip box (`white-space: nowrap`) keeps all its glyphs in the PDF's text layer, as before; Chrome writes only what shows. ADR-0009 covers the block axis only. Tailwind's `truncate` is not in the compiled `tailwind.css`, so no fixture needs it yet; `text-overflow: ellipsis` is not implemented at all.
- **Chrome's page content box is a whole px taller than ours:** with A4 and 15 mm margins Chrome's fragmentainer is 1010 px, ours 1009.13 px (a 20 px line fits under a 990 px spacer in Chrome and under a 989.13 px one in ours; `sp989.5` and `sp990` probes). The clip boxes use `PageStrips::chrome_bottom` (rounded up) so that content after them lands where Chrome puts it; ordinary flow still uses the exact height, so a block that ends in that last pixel moves to the next page here and not in Chrome.
- **A clip box that straddles the page bottom with short content** (`height:300px` at 900 px, a 6-line paragraph inside): Chrome fragments the box and splits the paragraph 4 + 2 (`P5s`); ours moves the whole box to the next page (the clip hides nothing, so it is the ordinary F30c "a paragraph that fits a page moves whole" rule). Nested in a block the same rule decides for a clip box that does not fit (F30c).
- **`overflow-x: clip` alone with a `height` below the content:** Chrome puts what follows at the box's bottom, on top of the overflowing text; ours puts it below the text (12 pages instead of page 1).

### F30f-d — a paragraph with a border or padding that spans pages is cut as if its lines started at the top of its border box  (severity: HIGH — silent, size: small — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-29-f30f-d-padded-paragraph-split.md` §7)

Found by the `vrt-validator` run on F30f-b (clip part), 2026-10-28; investigated in plan `2026-10-28-f30f-b-clip-boxes.md` §7 (not fixed there: it does not reach Chrome's numbers). Main already has it.

**Repro** (A4, 15 mm, Inter; `out/scratch/f30fbv/sc/c286.html`, Chrome `c286.chrome.pdf`): `body{font:14px/1.2 Inter}`, `<div style="overflow:hidden;padding:16px;border:1px solid #ccc;border-radius:8px">` holding 1500 direct words (`w0000 …`), `<p>AFTER</p>` after it.

| | page 1 / page 2 words | AFTER |
|---|---|---|
| Chrome | 763 / 737 | page 2, 760.8 pt |
| main (and, with the clip part's auto-height fix, ours) | 776 / 724, **the 13 words of the last line of page 1 are in the text layer and not on the page**: the line is at y 798-811 pt, the page's content box ends at 799.4 pt, the box's clip hides it, and page 2 starts at w0776 | page 2, 735.8 pt |

Every page break of such a box loses a line from the picture (13 words here; `fz/s12_40`, `sc/c106` … 23 of 144 `sc2.py` card configs, `t16/E_leading` with Tailwind classes). With a `<p>` inside the card nothing is lost (the nested path knows the insets); without `overflow` the last line is painted in the page's bottom margin instead (visible, but outside the content box).

**Cause.** The body-level line path of `fragment_pagination_root` (`fragment_inline_root`) fits lines as `paragraph_top + (line_bottom - first_top) <= page bottom`, with line coordinates relative to the content box. The renderer paints the lines at `fragment.y + inset_top` (border + padding above them, reserved on every page, F30) and the clip frame is `frag.height` = the lines' span only, because `line_split_insets` is recorded by `fragment_nested_inline_root` alone. So the last line of each fragment ends `inset_top` (17 px here) below the page bottom, and below the frame's bottom, where the clip cuts it. The text layer keeps the line (main); the clip part's first version dropped it from the text layer with its writer band, which is how the validator saw the loss in `pdftotext` (a content-sized box has no band any more, plan §7).

**Fix tried** (patch kept in the plan's evidence; ~20 lines in `fragment_pagination_root`): send a body-level paragraph that has a border or padding and does not fit the room through the branch the clip boxes use (`line_split_candidate` + `fragment_nested_inline_root`, which counts the insets and records `line_split_insets`). Results over 613 + 477 scratch cases (238 changed): fixtures 01-28 and the corpus byte-identical; c286 reaches Chrome's words per page exactly (763 / 737) and keeps every word on the page; but only 69 of the 238 changed cases reach Chrome's words per page exactly and 83 more are within 14 words, **86 stay further away** (AFTER 13 pt below Chrome's in c286; in the 139 changed cases of `sc/`, `lh/`, `fz/` and `t16/` the pixel diff against Chrome grows in 67, shrinks in 28 and stays in 37, because on main the lost line and the 17 px shift happen to cancel when the line pitch is 17 px), and the Tailwind shapes (`t16/A_direct_pad`, `B_direct_pad_xs`, `E_leading`: Chrome 385 / 617 words, main 84 / 684 / 238) are far off either way. The remaining gap is `box-decoration-break: slice` (F96): Chrome does not repeat the top padding and border on the continuation page, the nested path reserves them on every page (17 px = one line less per continuation page here), and the Tailwind shapes also differ in where the box starts (avoid / orphan rules, not looked at). So the fix needs F96 (and the Tailwind shapes looked at) to be an improvement everywhere; alone it trades a lost line for a shifted page.

**Done** (2026-10-29, plan `2026-10-29-f30f-d-padded-paragraph-split.md` §7; fulgur fork `40f3e7fd`, `779b0c28`, `7a33d58c`, `87946cc3`; ours `padded_paragraph_split.rs`, fixture 29, fixture 16's threshold): a padded or bordered body-level paragraph that does not fit its page, and a paragraph in a block, is cut by the splitter that knows its insets and sliced at its breaks (`box-decoration-break: slice`, the initial value; Chrome m141 measured in §7): the top inset is on the first fragment only, a continuation page starts its lines at the content top, a fragment that is not the last has side borders to the page bottom and no bottom border. c286 prints 763 / 737 words like Chrome with every word inside its page and frame; the last line is no longer in the margin or under the clip. Fixture 29 (the commoner shape, no clip): 3 pages 11.3 / 23.0 / 5.5 % -> 2 pages 9.9 / 12.6 %; fixture 16 page 2 20.8 -> 12.9 %. Fixtures 01-15, 17-28 and the corpus byte-identical. Sweep of 2623 cases (247 changed): words-per-page distance to Chrome 28879 -> 3625; 20 cases end further away in words or ink (line metrics, Chrome's 1010 px page; named in §7), one scratch case gains a page (`sc2/c14`). Two pdf-reviewer defects fixed on the branch (fork `3ee98d70`): the border keeps the radius of the unbroken corners and the box shadow stops at the cut edges (plan §7, "Reviewer defects"). Not done here, filed: clip boxes with a block size keep the closed frame (F30f-i), block boxes and table cells are still closed on every page (F96), a padded paragraph that fits leaves what follows over its last line (F30f-h), the fit test measures glyph-run extents (F30f-j). The `t16/A`, `B`, `E` Chrome references in the first version of this finding's plan were rendered without the stylesheet; the valid ones agree with ours in lines per page.

### F30f-e — a clip box whose height comes from layout, not from `height` / `max-height`, is treated as content-sized  (severity: medium — it reveals what Chrome clips, size: medium — fulgur)

Found by the `vrt-validator` second pass on F30f-b (clip part), 2026-10-28; plan `2026-10-28-f30f-b-clip-boxes.md` §7 ("Auto-height clip boxes", "Not verified"). Main already behaves this way, the clip part leaves it as it was.

The clip semantics of F30f-b (fragmenting by the box's block size, no place for the lines it hides, the writer's band; ADR-0009) apply only to a box whose computed `height` is a length or whose `max-height` is not `none` (`block_size_constrained`). A box whose used height is smaller than its content for another reason has neither, so it is "auto height" to the fork and behaves as on main: its hidden text is laid out, moved to the next page or shown, and written to the text layer, while Chrome clips it. The shapes (A4, 15 mm, `overflow:hidden`, 700 words; `out/scratch/f30fbv2/b/`):

| Box | Repro | main | Chrome |
|---|---|---|---|
| `flex:1` item of a fixed column (`display:flex;flex-direction:column;height:400px`) after an 880 px spacer | `flexcol_grow.s880` | 2 pages, 700 words, ink p1 / p2 [0, 13154] (the box moves whole to page 2 and shows everything) | 2 pages, 271 words, ink [5168, 7662]; the branch before `16d281a3` (which treated every clip box as constrained) [5155, 7864] |
| `aspect-ratio:2;width:400px` (also `aspect-video`, `size-64` in Tailwind) | `direct_aspect.s0`, `aspect.*`, `tw_aspect` | 2 pages, ink [26017, 19248] (the text shown in full, AFTER on page 2) | 1 page, 80 words, ink [5305] |
| flex / grid item with a definite track or a stretched size, `inset:0` abspos box | `a_in_c_flex`, `abs_insets`, `abs_tb` | as `aspect` | as `aspect` |

**Why.** Not a missing rule but a missing input: the used height is known (`final_layout.size.height`) and the lines below it are known (`collect_inline_line_metrics`), so "constrained" is a property of the *layout*, not of two style properties. The obstacle is the one that broke the first version: Blitz's line boxes are as tall as the font (20 px for Inter 16 px) when the line height is below that, so "the last line box ends below the box" is also true for a content-sized box with `line-height:12px` (`clip_hides_nothing` compares the line box's bottom with the box's height; plan §7).

**Fix, suggested by the validator:** treat a box as constrained when its used height is smaller than its content height by more than the line pitch (the distance between two line tops, not the line box's height), i.e. fix `clip_hides_nothing` to compare against the pitch instead of the last line box's bottom, and then use it instead of `block_size_constrained` (or in `||` with it). Test with the shapes of §7 that went wrong (c318, c300, c286, `s12_40`) and the rows above; every auto-height case must stay byte-identical to main (926 scratch cases, `three.py`).

### F30f-f — a constrained clip box that holds anything but direct text or one paragraph at its top keeps main's output  (severity: medium — it shows or reveals what Chrome clips or splits, size: medium — fulgur; depends on F30c)

Found by the `vrt-validator` third pass on F30f-b (clip part), 2026-10-28; plan `2026-10-28-f30f-b-clip-boxes.md` §7 ("Supported shapes"). The clip semantics of F30f-b (fragment by the box's block size, no place for what the clip hides, the writer's band) apply only to a box with a `height` or `max-height` whose in-flow content is direct text or one paragraph (or a block wrapper around one) at its content top, with no `break-inside: avoid` and no percentage vertical padding (`clip_shape_supported`). Every other such box is laid out, fragmented and drawn as on main: it moves whole, and its hidden text is written. Chrome fragments it. Shapes and numbers (words on page 1 / page 2, 500 words, A4, 15 mm, 14 px / 1.4, box `height:300px;padding:16px`, spacer 700 unless noted; `out/scratch/f30fbv3v/`, git-ignored; "fork" is the fork before the narrowing, `aecc013a`):

| Shape | Repro | Chrome | main | fork before the narrowing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| heading above the paragraph | `c/hp_pad.s700` (also `hp_border`, `hp_pad_hmargin`, `bb_pad_hp`, `flowroot_hid_hp`, `wrap_pad_clip_hp`, `maxh_pad_hp`, `small_page_pad_hp`, `lh_small_hp`) | 172 / 14 | 0 / 500 (all words, revealed) | 0 / 28 |
| second, third paragraph | `c/two_p_pad_marg.s700`, `three_p_pad.s700` | the first paragraph's visible lines on page 1 | all words on page 2 | fewer words than Chrome |
| the paragraph's own margin | `d/pm_nopad_default` (spacer 880, no padding), `d/pm_default_pad`, `c/p_margin_in.s700` | 66 / 125 | 0 / 500 | 0 / 121 |
| float or image above | `c/p_float.s700`, `c/p_img_size.s700` | | | the same loss |
| heading and direct text | `d/pm_hp_direct` | | | |
| `break-inside: avoid` on the paragraph | `d/pa_pad.html`, `c/p_avoid.s700` / `s880` | 0 / 212 (moves whole) | 0 / 500 | 185 / 26 |

**Why.** F30c: the fragmenter splits only the first thing on a strip. The second pass of F30f-b made the first paragraph of a padded box "first" (`frame_above`); anything else still moves whole, and with the clip semantics on it moved whole *and* was clipped, which is worse than main. **Fix:** after F30c (a paragraph that is not the first on its strip is split where it is, below the content above it), widen `clip_shape_supported` to the shapes above one by one, each against the numbers in the table; the avoid case needs `nested_line_split` to honour `break-inside: avoid` first. The predicate is the single place to widen.

### F30f-g — a percentage `padding-top` / `padding-bottom` is resolved to 0 by the block layout  (severity: medium — wrong box height, size: small — Taffy fork)

Found while narrowing F30f-b (plan §7, "Supported shapes", cause 3). Taffy's block layout (`generate_item_list`) resolves a child's padding against the parent's `Size<Option<f32>>`: the vertical sides against an indefinite height, so `padding: 5%` comes out as 30 px left and right and 0 above and below, in `final_layout.padding` and in the box's height (`height:100px;padding:5%` is 100 px tall, Chrome 160 px), while the content is laid out below the 30 px. CSS Box 3 §5.1: a percentage padding refers to the width of the containing block, for all four sides. Not clip-specific; `d/pq_1pct`, `pq_5pct`; fork test `a_percentage_padding_is_reported_without_its_vertical_part_by_the_layout`. Until it is fixed, a clip box with such padding is not a supported shape (`clip_shape_supported` reads the style). **Fix:** resolve `padding` and `border` of block children against the width, in the Taffy fork (`subapavel/taffy`, `htmltopdf/minimum-contribution`), then drop the style check from the predicate and expect `pq_5pct` at Chrome's 24 / 72.

### F30f-h — a padded or bordered body-level paragraph that fits its page leaves what follows over its last line  (severity: medium-high — visible overlap, size: small — fulgur)

Found while finishing F30f-d (not caused by it: main renders the same bytes). `<div style="border:1px solid;padding:9px">L1<br>L2<br>L3<br>L4<br>L5</div><p style="margin:0">AFTER</p>` (14px/20px, A4, 15 mm; `out/scratch/f30fd/fit/fit.html`): Chrome puts `AFTER` at 133.8 pt, main and ours at 118.6 pt, over the box's last line (L5 is at 111.1 pt): the paragraph is placed by `fragment_inline_root`, whose returned cursor is the end of the lines, without `inset_top + inset_bottom` (20 px here). A margin between the box and what follows hides part of it (a card with `mb-6` ends up 20 px too high with its border still painted); no committed fixture has a body-level padded multi-line paragraph that fits its page. Only the body-level line path; the nested path knows the insets. Fix: `cursor_y` of the fitting branch of `fragment_pagination_root` adds the insets (and the frame, `line_split_insets`, so that the bottom border is drawn where the lines end plus padding). Pinned today by the fork test `a_padded_body_level_paragraph_that_fits_is_not_sliced` (it asserts the wrong 100 px on purpose, with a comment).

### F30f-i — a clip box with a block size that is cut over pages keeps its closed frame: page 2's first line is `inset_top` too low, and the rule Chrome uses for its last line is not implemented  (severity: low-medium, size: small-medium — fulgur)

Found by the first version of F30f-d step 4 (plan §7, "stop condition"). `overflow:hidden; height:300px; padding:16px` at the page bottom: Chrome slices the box (first line of page 2 at the content top, 43.1 pt) and shows only the lines that start above the end of the last fragment's content box (fragment 23 px - 16 px padding = 7 px: one line); the writer clips at the padding box, so a sliced version shows one line too many (15 sweep cases one line from Chrome where main was exact). Ours keeps the F30f-b geometry: the top inset reserved on every page, the first line of page 2 12 pt low, the last page's lines hidden by the writer band. Fix: slice these boxes and drop, on the last fragment, the lines that start at or below `frame - inset_bottom` into the hidden tail (the first line stays), then re-run the sweep's `f30fbv/c`, `f30fbv3/pad16t_*`, `f30fbv4/sw2/notesD.*` (all must stay at Chrome's words).

### F30f-j — the line-fit test measures glyph-run extents, not line boxes  (severity: low, size: small-medium — fulgur)

Found by the F30f-d sweep (plan §7, exceptions). `collect_inline_line_metrics` gives each line `(top, bottom)` as the extent of its runs: `(-1, 16)` for a 15 px line of 14 px Inter, so a span of n lines is `(n-1) * pitch + 17` where the line boxes span `n * pitch`. With `line-height` below the font's content height (1.21; of Tailwind's `leading-*` only `leading-none` is below it) the fit test is up to 2 px too conservative per paragraph: a card whose 67th line ends 1 px inside the room (`sc/c260`) loses it to the next page (66 / 45 lines per page against Chrome's 67 / 44). 16 sweep cases. Main matched Chrome there by accident (it ignored the insets). Fix: measure line boxes (`line.metrics().min_coord/max_coord` against the line's own height), which moves every paragraph split in the corpus: byte gate and sweep needed.

**Also from the vrt-validator pass on F30f-d** (finding 3; `out/scratch/f30fdv/a/bw1_p0.html`, `pd00`, `pad1`, 4000 words, `border:1px solid`, no padding, 14px/1.2): page 1 holds 786 words against Chrome's 800 (main 799): the glyph-extent fit test plus the 1 px border inset passes 1009.13 px one line early, so page 1 ends a line short and every later page is shifted; `AFTER` at 762.1 pt against Chrome's 748.8 (main 748.6, which ignored the inset and matched by accident). The same cause, only the border (no padding) as the inset. Fix and gate are the ones above.

### F30f-k — Chrome restarts its line snapping on every fragment of a sliced box; we keep the absolute phase  (severity: low-medium, size: small-medium — fulgur)

Found by the vrt-validator pass on F30f-d (findings 2 and 6; `out/scratch/f30fdv/a/pd00..pd40` from `b4.py`, `lh16_pd*`, `bw6_p8`, `bw10_p16`). With a fractional line pitch (14px/1.2 = 16.8 px, `line-height:16px` with a padded box) Chrome snaps each line box to whole pixels from the top of the *fragment* it is in, so the pattern of 16 / 17 px steps starts again at the content top of every continuation page; we lay the lines out once for the whole box and cut it, so the phase of page 2 is the one the lines had in the unbroken box. A page then holds one line more or less than Chrome's: padding 18 to 32 px, page 2 one line short (`lh16_pd18`: words per page 834 / 902 / 886 / 378 against Chrome's 839 / 912 / 889 / 360, `AFTER` 393.1 against 369.3 pt); thick borders (`bw10_p16`: 712 / 761 / 772 / 734 / 735 / 286 against 712 / 776 / 775 / 736 / 738 / 263, `AFTER` 364.6 against 339.3 pt; `bw6_p8`) are the same cause with a larger first inset. Main was farther off in most of these (its lines stood in the margin); the cases are not regressions of F30f-d. Fix: when a sliced fragment starts a page, re-snap the line tops of its strip from zero (the line `(top, bottom)` of `LineSlice` are paragraph-local; the rounding belongs to the strip), then the sweep and the byte gate. Related to F30f-j (line boxes against glyph extents).

### F30g — an absolutely positioned wrapper of a long paragraph loses most of it  (severity: HIGH — silent, size: medium — fulgur) — **done** (`2026-10-18-f30g-abspos-paragraph.md` §7)

F83 `vrt-validator`: an absolutely positioned `<p>` itself (not a wrapper) prints 0 of 200 words, same on main (`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f83/att/cases3/abspos.html`).

Named in the F30 plan (§6): `position:absolute` wrapper (or an `absolute` parent / relative parent) around 5000 words prints 379 words (4621 lost) on 14 pages
(`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f78/ab/abs_normal.html`, `abs_1.3.html`, `absparent_*.html`, `absrel_*.html`; found by `vrt-validator` on PR #30, pre-existing). From reading, not traced: the absolute pass
(`append_position_absolute_body_direct_fragments`) replaces the node's fragments by one per intersected page with no line ranges (it clears the F78 slices on purpose). Plan: `2026-10-18-f30g-abspos-paragraph.md`.

### F31 — `break-before: page` on a first child or on a `<tr>` is ignored  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Found by `vrt-validator` on PR #16 (pre-existing). Also: negative `margin-top` ignored; `@page { size; margin }` in the
document overridden by the renderer's defaults (see F6).

### F32 — image sizing gaps beyond F9  (severity: medium, size: medium — fulgur/Blitz) — **partly done** (F32a: PR #25, `docs/plans/2026-10-17-f32a-oversized-and-broken-images.md` §7; remaining: F32b–F32m)

Found by `vrt-validator` on PR #17 (mostly pre-existing, reachable by bare `<img>` since F9; probes
`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f9/p/`, copied to `out/scratch/f32a/`). Split into F32a–F32m; **F32a is done**
(`2026-10-17-f32a-oversized-and-broken-images.md` §7: an `<img>` wider than the content box is painted and takes one line
box; an `<img>` or `inline-block` in a multi-line paragraph is painted; a truncated or corrupt raster no longer fails the
render; `strict_images` / `--strict-images` and a warning for an unregistered or empty `src`). The rest, in this order:

- **F32b** an image taller than a page is drawn whole on the last page instead of the remainder (`sz_400x1200`);
- **F32c** inline images do not grow the line box (lines overlap them, `p4` / `p7` 1-3 px off Chrome);
- **F32d** `object-fit` / SVG `preserveAspectRatio` are not implemented (96 x 96 instead of letterboxed 96 x 48);
- **F32e** ratio rules: applied when both axes are given (`width:200px; max-height:30px` → 60 x 30, Chrome 200 x 30),
  `aspect-ratio: auto 1/1`, `width`/`height` attributes overriding preflight `height:auto`, `height: 50%` in an
  auto-height parent → 0;
- **F32f** `src` matching is exact-key: `logo.png?v=1`, `#frag`, `sub/../logo.png`, surrounding spaces render nothing
  (HTML strips whitespace; Chrome resolves the others); F32a only warns on them;
- **F32g** unsized SVG / `<canvas>` defaults (Chrome 300 x 150), SVG `width="50%"`/`em`, text in SVG `<img>` dropped (no
  fonts);
- **F32h** EXIF orientation of JPEGs;
- **F32i** `float` on `<img>` is ignored;
- **F32j** absolutely positioned `left` + `right` stretch;
- **F32k** the `alt` text of a broken `<img>` (HTML §15.4.2; Blitz builds `img` as a childless replaced leaf, so nothing
  is drawn for `alt="Logo firmy"`; Chrome draws the text and an icon);
- **F32l** partial rows of a truncated PNG (Chrome draws the decoded rows in the kept box; we draw nothing);
- **F32m** (speed, low) a PNG or GIF that a document uses is decoded three times in a one-shot render: `validate` in
  `Renderer::check_images` (lazily, once per `Renderer`), `validate` in the fork's `krilla_image` (once per render) and
  Krilla's own decode at `finish()`. 50 distinct 2000 x 1000 PNGs: main 270 ms, F32a 480 ms; one PNG drawn 50 times: main
  290 ms, F32a 28 ms (the cache). A fork API taking the verdicts the `Renderer` already has would save one decode.
  Also the JPEG decode in `validate` is capped at 16 Mpx (`MAX_JPEG_VALIDATION_PIXELS`); larger JPEGs get the header check.

### F60 — a JPEG with `0xFF` fill bytes before a marker draws nothing, without a warning  (severity: medium, size: small — fulgur; **done**, see the F60 bullet below)

Found by `pdf-reviewer` on PR #25 (pre-existing, identical on main): ITU-T T.81 §B.1.1.2 allows any number of `0xFF` fill
bytes before a marker (`/tmp/claude-0/pdfrev-f32a/img/fill.jpg`); viewers and Chrome draw the image, we paint nothing
and say nothing. The drop happens before `krilla_image` (the header read that seeds the natural size or Krilla's
own header parse), so neither `validate` nor `--strict-images` sees it. Probably `ImageRender::decode_dimensions` (it
reads the marker after `0xFF` literally) and/or Krilla's header decode; find which, then skip fill bytes there. Note that
`image::strip_jpeg_header_padding` (F32a) already tolerates them for validation only.

### F61 — a JPEG without an EOI marker passes validation and is embedded as it is  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Found by `pdf-reviewer` on PR #25: `noeoi.jpg` decodes fully (zune-jpeg strict accepts it) and is passed through, but
`qpdf --check` reports invalid JPEG data in the image stream. Append `0xFFD9` when the stream lacks it, or refuse it.
Matters for PDF/A validators, not for viewers.

### F62 — PDF/A-3 readiness gaps  (severity: medium — blocks the ISDOC / ZUGFeRD item of M5, size: large — fulgur / Krilla; indicative, veraPDF not run)

Found by `pdf-reviewer` on PR #25: image XObjects carry `/Interpolate true`, hard-coded in
`crates/fulgur/src/image.rs` (`to_krilla_image(.., true)`); ISO 19005 §6.2.8 requires `false`. There is no `/OutputIntents`
entry, image ICC profiles are dropped, there is no `pdfaid` XMP part, no `/AF` or `EmbeddedFiles` (ISO 19005-3 §6.8,
needed to attach the invoice XML), and `Producer` says "fulgur" (see `docs/upstream.md`). Run veraPDF on a fixture and
turn the result into the real list before planning; F44 (`.notdef` glyphs) is part of the same goal.

### F63 — PNG pixel data is re-deflated without predictors  (severity: low — size, size: medium — Krilla / fulgur)

Found by `pdf-reviewer` on PR #25: a 168-byte PNG becomes 6.3 KB in the PDF, a 215 KB solid 8192 x 8192 PNG becomes
880 KB. Krilla decodes to samples and deflates them again with no PNG predictor; for photographic invoices this is a
size cost only. Passing the IDAT stream through with `/DecodeParms` (`/Predictor 15`) when the PNG allows it would keep
the original size.

### F64 — `content: url(x) " před"` loses the string part  (severity: low, size: small — fulgur)

Found by `pdf-reviewer` on PR #25 (pre-existing): only single-item `content` is read
(`extract_content_image_url`, "multi-item content is out of scope"), so an image followed by text paints neither or only
the image. Not a Tailwind idiom.

### F65 — a broken CSS image is only logged at debug level  (severity: low, size: small — ours + fulgur; "L2" in the F32a review)

Found by `pdf-reviewer` on PR #25: an unregistered or undecodable image used as `background-image`, list marker image or
`content: url()` is dropped with `log::debug!` in the fork and nothing in `htmltopdf`; `--strict-images` checks `<img src>`
only. Scan `url()` in `style=""` and `<style>` / bundled CSS (the `css_text` scanner exists) and report them like `<img>`.

From the second `pdf-reviewer` round on PR #25 (low): `--strict-images` does not fail on a damaged JPEG that is still embedded (truncated: exit 0 with a warning), although the CLI help says strict fails when "a bundled image does not decode" — reword the help or add a `--strict-images` level for damaged images; image validation uses zune-jpeg 0.5 while Krilla reads JPEG headers with 0.4.21, so an edge case could pass validation and be refused by Krilla (only a debug log, nothing drawn) — none seen.

### F66 — `white-space: pre` / `nowrap` still wrap  (severity: medium — preformatted text, `whitespace-nowrap` / `whitespace-pre` in Tailwind, size: medium — Blitz / Parley) — **partly done** (PR #28, `docs/plans/2026-10-17-f66-white-space-nowrap.md` §7; remaining: F66b–F66g)

Found by `vrt-validator` on PR #25 (pre-existing; `/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f32a/p/a9.html`): a `<p style="white-space: pre">` with long
lines wraps at the content box instead of overflowing, and a `nowrap` paragraph wraps too. Chrome keeps the lines intact
(newlines honoured for `pre`). Tailwind's `whitespace-nowrap` is common in invoice tables (amounts, IBANs). Not
recorded before (F50b mentions only `white-space: pre` in an anonymous cell run). Find whether Blitz maps the property to
Parley's break settings at all.

**Done** in the Blitz fork (`aebe118`, `2026-10-17-f66-white-space-nowrap.md` §7): `nowrap` and `pre` no longer soft-wrap,
`pre-line` collapses indentation, `td[nowrap]` works. What is left is filed below as F66b-F66f.

From the `vrt-validator` run on PR #28, noted here, not fixed: **RTL** — with `direction: rtl; text-align: left` an overflowing
`nowrap` line overflows to the right in ours, while Chrome start-aligns it and overflows to the left
(`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f66/atk/x8.html`); **`<wbr>`** under `nowrap`: ours never breaks, Chrome broke in one probe
(`atk/x5.html`; the spec text was not checked, CSS Text 3 §5.1 should say whether `<wbr>` is an opportunity under `nowrap`).
The review's high finding H1 (an explicit cell `width` below the `nowrap` text's width) is fixed in the Blitz fork, see the
F66 plan §7 "Review follow-up"; H2 is F66g.

### F66b — `white-space: nowrap` on a span inside a wrapping paragraph still wraps, and a `normal` span inside a `nowrap` root does not  (severity: low, size: medium — Parley / Blitz)

From F66 (a limitation of its design): Parley 0.6 has no wrap-mode style, so the Blitz fork decides per inline formatting
context from its root (`TextLayout::nowrap`). A `<span class="whitespace-nowrap">` inside a paragraph that wraps is wrapped
like the rest, and a wrapping span inside a `nowrap` root is not. Needs a wrap mode in Parley (a later release or a Parley
fork, which the owner must create; there is none today). NBSP / word-joiner substitution was rejected: it changes the
extracted text.
Repros from `vrt-validator` (PR #28): `<nobr>` still wraps (an inline `nowrap` inside a wrapping root is the same gap);
M1, `<div style="white-space:nowrap">a <span style="white-space:normal">long text …</span></div>` — the `normal` span no
longer wraps because the root's `nowrap` applies to the whole context (`/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f66/atk/spn1.html`), a documented
non-goal of F66 (plan §6).

### F66c — `text-overflow: ellipsis` is not drawn (Tailwind `truncate` clips without the `…`)  (severity: low, size: medium — Stylo pref + fulgur)

From F66: Stylo 0.8.0 does not parse `text-overflow` (`servo_pref="layout.unimplemented"`, false in `stylo_static_prefs`
0.8.0), so `truncate` clips at the box (the `overflow: hidden` part works) but Chrome ends the line with `…`
(`out/scratch/f66/p6`: Chrome's text ends at x 347 with the ellipsis, ours at 352 with a cut glyph). Needs the
pref (the F2b route, a Stylo fork) or a polyfill in `htmltopdf` that reads the declaration and fulgur drawing the ellipsis.

### F66d — the `text-wrap` shorthand (Tailwind v4 `text-nowrap`) is dropped  (severity: low, size: small — ours)

From F66: `text-wrap` is `engines="gecko"` in Stylo 0.8.0, dropped at parse time, so `text-nowrap` wraps
(`out/scratch/f66/p11`: Chrome one row, ours two). `text-wrap-mode` itself parses and works. Polyfill in `htmltopdf` like
F8: rewrite `text-wrap: nowrap | wrap` to `text-wrap-mode` (and drop `balance` / `pretty` / `stable`, which only change
quality).

### F66e — a `truncate` (overflow hidden + nowrap) table cell is clipped where Chrome widens the column  (severity: low, size: medium — Blitz / Taffy)

From F66, measured (`out/scratch/f66/p13`, `p14`): in `<table><tr><td>long sentence</td><td class="truncate">418 px text</td>`
Chrome gives the second column its full min-content (red text 319-736, not clipped, table stretched to the page; with
`width: 400px` the table grows to about 500 px, `p14`). Ours clips the cell at about 295 px (`p13`) or 160 px (`p14`):
Taffy's automatic minimum size of an `overflow: hidden` grid item is 0, so the track may shrink below the cell's
min-content. A plain `nowrap` cell is fine (F66 widens it). CSS 2.1 §17.5.2.2: a column's minimum is its cells' min-content,
whatever their `overflow`. Not a Tailwind-table idiom with `max-w-*` (`p10`: Chrome and ours both clip at the max width).

### F66f — a newline or `<br>` at the end of a block adds an empty line  (severity: medium — 20 px too much after every address block with a trailing newline, size: small — Blitz / Parley)

Found while probing F66 (pre-existing, same bytes before and after F66): `<div style="white-space:pre-line">\n    Firma\n    Ulice 1\n</div>`,
`<div style="white-space:pre">a\nb\n</div>` and `<div>a<br>b<br></div>` are one line taller than in Chrome (marker below the
text at y 117 instead of 97, `out/scratch/f66/p5`, `p15`, `p16`). CSS: a forced break ends its line and creates no line of
its own when nothing follows it in the block; Parley's `break_all_lines` commits an empty trailing line after the final
newline. F32a's `break_lines` already takes back an empty line after an overflowing one; extend it to a final empty
line after any forced break (the line holding the newline has a `BreakReason::Explicit`). Check `<br><br>` (Chrome keeps
the first as an empty line) before changing it.

**Seen by the vrt-validator on F30f-d** (finding 4, widows at an exact fit): `padding:0 10px 21px;border:1px` with 47 / 48 `<br>` lines of 21 px (`out/scratch/f30fdv/a/exact_n47_pb21.html`, `b7.py`, `b9.py`): Chrome splits 45 / 2 (or keeps the card whole), we 46 / 1. It is not the widows rule: `plan_line_breaks` does pull a line back, but the trailing `<br>` of the test makes an extra empty line box (this finding, F37d), so the layout has 49 lines where Chrome has 48, the last page holds "one word plus the empty line" and the widows rule counts the empty line. It goes away with this finding; no change to `plan_line_breaks` (probe: `PLB n=48 first_room=988.1 last_extra=22`, 47 word lines + 1 empty).

### F66g — overflowing content is clipped at the page edge where Chrome shrinks the page  (severity: medium — wide `nowrap` tables lose their right columns in the PDF; since F66 only warned, size: large — fulgur)

From the `vrt-validator` run on PR #28 (introduced by F66 by design; probes `/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f66/atk/t1.html` (seven `nowrap`
columns, four visible), `wide.html`, `hd.html`, `x7.html`, `y4.html`): `nowrap` / `pre` content and boxes wider than the page
run past the page box. The glyphs stay in the content stream but lie outside the MediaBox, so viewers do not show them and
`pdftotext` loses them. Before F66 the same text wrapped inside its cell. Chrome shrinks the whole page to fit (Blink's print
shrink-to-fit, F32a §7); that is a print UA feature and no specification asks for it. WeasyPrint and Prince clip like us.

**Decision (2026-10-17): keep the CSS behaviour** (css-page-3 §3.2 lets content overflow the margins, the page box is the
medium; css-overflow-3 §3 has `overflow: visible` content paint where it falls), **and warn.** fulgur's `render_v2` logs one
`log::warn!` per page: "content extends N pt past the right page edge on page P and is clipped" (`Canvas::paint_extent`:
glyphs, inline images and boxes, plus boxes with a background or border; nothing recorded under a clip path or a transform).
It is a warning in strict mode too (strict is for fonts and images). Test: `crates/htmltopdf/tests/page_overflow_warning.rs`.
Quirks: `position: absolute; left: 9999px` (the off-screen trick for `sr-only`-style text, F56) paints past the page and triggers the
warning; overflow past the *left* edge (negative offsets, RTL) is not warned. Not done: shrinking the page, scaling the content (`zoom` / `transform` on the root by the template author works today),
counting transformed or inline-block content, warning about the left edge (RTL), and a `Renderer` option that turns the
warning into an error. The usual cure is on the template side (`max-width: 100%`, a smaller font, `table-layout: fixed` with
`break-words`); a later product decision (owner: scope) could add an opt-in fit-to-width.

### F70 — a bare `<pre>`, `<code>`, `<kbd>`, `<samp>` or `<tt>` drops ALL its text when only Inter is bundled  (severity: HIGH — silent data loss, rc 0, no warning even with `--strict-fonts`, size: small–medium — ours / Blitz UA sheet) — **done** (PR #29, `docs/plans/2026-10-17-f70-ua-monospace.md` §7; F74–F76 are filed separately)

Found by `vrt-validator` on PR #28 (pre-existing): Blitz's UA sheet gives these elements `font-family: monospace`, with
`system_fonts(false)` and no generic mapping (F3) the run matches no face and is dropped. F3b maps generic keywords only in
author CSS and `style` attributes, so `style="font-family:monospace"` works but the UA rule does not. Measured with the
release CLI, Inter only, `--strict-fonts` (`out/scratch/f66h/mono.html`): `<code>`, `<kbd>`, `<samp>`, `<tt>` and `<pre>`
all vanish; `<var>` and an author `font-family: monospace` are fine; `textarea` / `input` (also monospace in some UA sheets)
were not checked. `pre{font-family:Inter}` works. Fix in `htmltopdf`: rewrite the UA-sheet generic families too (add a bundled
`pre, code, kbd, samp, tt { font-family: <mapped monospace> }` author-level rule, or let the strict check count UA families
and warn), and make `--strict-fonts` fail on a UA family that matches nothing. The repo's own JetBrains Mono is bundled
(`crates/htmltopdf/fonts`) and is what `monospace` maps to when the document registers no mono face: check why `<code>` does
not reach it.

**Done** in the fulgur fork (`2026-10-17-f70-ua-monospace.md` §7): the engine resolves the generics Stylo parses from the map
`htmltopdf` hands it (`AssetBundle::generic_families`), so the UA sheet's `monospace` reaches JetBrains Mono (or whatever
`generic_family(Monospace, …)` names); text that no glyph run covers is now reported (a warning per distinct text, an error
under `--strict-fonts`). Filed from the work: **F74**, **F75**, **F76**.

Review of PR #29 (`vrt-validator`, `pdf-reviewer`), fixed on the same branches: (1) a paragraph over 64 KiB of text was reported as text without
a font (and failed `--strict-fonts`) because Parley keeps a cluster's offset in its run as `u16`; the fulgur fork now restores the ranges for
left-to-right text, which also repairs the text layer of such paragraphs (was: the tail extracted as the head, `word00001…`), see **F79** for what is left;
(2) the dedupe of dropped texts was quadratic (40 000 dropped paragraphs: 8.4 s, now 0.5 s, the baseline's 0.46 s); (3) the error message named every
dropped text (409 KB on stderr), now 20 and a count; (4) the static check treats `-moz-fixed` as `monospace`; (5) docs: "no glyph run was produced",
`.notdef` glyphs (F44) and margin-box text are not covered.

### F77 — `->` and `=>` in Inter text lose the `>` in the text layer  (severity: low, size: small — Inter / fulgur)

Found by `vrt-validator` on PR #29 (pre-existing, `/tmp/claude-0/vrt-f70/atk/jb.html`): in an Inter paragraph `fi ffi fl ->  =>` extracts as
`fi ffi fl - =`; the same arrows in a JetBrains Mono `<pre>` extract fine. Probably Inter's contextual alternates turn `->` / `=>` into
one glyph whose ToUnicode entry covers only the first character (not checked). The picture is right; copy-paste and search lose the `>`. Needs a look at how fulgur maps
a multi-character cluster to its ToUnicode entry (ligatures such as `ffi` extract correctly).

### F78 — a long paragraph can lose a line at a page break  (severity: high — silent line loss, size: medium — fulgur; **done**, see the F78 bullet below)

Found while testing F70: `<p>` of 7000 words `w00000 w00001 …` (49 000 bytes, so not the 64 KiB wrap) with Noto Sans bundled, default A4 page, 14 pages: `pdftotext`
finds 6980 words, the missing ones being `w03490…w03509`, a line or two at the break between pages 7 and 8 (page 7 ends at `w03489`, page 8 starts at `w03500`). With Inter
through `htmltopdf` the same 7000 words are all there (and the PDF is byte-identical to the one from before F70), but 20 000 words (140 000 bytes, about 40 pages) lose 10 of them
(`pdftotext`: 19 990 found, `htmltopdf` on A4 with `font-family:Inter`), so it is not about the font and not from F70 (a page break falls between two lines; the paragraph text is unchanged by F70). Not investigated:
it looks like the line-splitting of F30 (`fragment_nested_inline_root`) at a break that falls between two lines of different heights. Reproduce with `<p>` of `w00000 w00001 …` words on A4 (a `render_smoke` test with `noto_bundle()` and 7000 words, or `htmltopdf` with Inter and 20 000);
the F70 tests use one tall page to stay out of it.

**Cause and fix** (plan `2026-10-17-f78-lost-line-at-page-break.md`): not the F30 splitter. The fragmenter measured lines by their
quantised Parley boxes (20 px for an Inter 16 px line) while the renderer re-derived each page's lines from `ShapedLine.height`
(the unrounded 19.2 px advance), so every page counted 0.8 px too much and after about 24 pages the start index skipped a line
